# General backlog — ranked, with triggers

Everything outstanding across the plan, the harness, and follow-ups discovered during execution — ranked by take-order (value weighted by effort), each with the **trigger** that makes it actionable. Component candidates live in [`component-backlog.md`](./component-backlog.md) (own queue, own triggers); this list covers everything else. Status ground truth: plan Phases 0–3 are merged (PR #8), and Phase 4's architecture, migrations, and the 2026-09-01/02 incident-response rounds (PRs #10–#35) have since landed on `main` — see [`plan.md`](./plan.md) for the current phase-by-phase status.

## Fresh-session order (2026-09-04)

The table below is ranked by take-order already; this block is a **recommendation for what a fresh session should pick up first**, layered on top of it — easy to reverse, not a renumbering of the rows. Rationale: fix what's broken before building more, cheap infrastructure before expensive components, and keep CI-gated items visibly separate from everything else since they aren't sequenced at all (they wait on the user lifting the CI freeze).

**Environment note (2026-09-04):** this sandbox got a working Playwright container for the first time this session — `dx` 0.7.9 and the wasm32 target installed, chromium/firefox/webkit browsers all installed, `dx build --web --release` succeeds. Three `dx`-managed tool downloads had to be placed by hand because `dx`'s HTTP client does not trust the agent proxy's CA (`invalid peer certificate: UnknownIssuer`): esbuild 0.27.3 and binaryen/wasm-opt into `~/.local/share/.dx/tools/`, and wasm-bindgen compiled from source. A fresh session hitting the same proxy should expect the identical failure and know the workaround rather than rediscovering it. A firefox+webkit calibration run (row 4) was started in this same sandbox and was **deliberately stopped partway** (178/1094, 14 failures recorded in firefox) as a scope decision — it is user-gated group-6 work (item 5 below) and was blocking the queue. Its outcome is genuinely incomplete, not merely unreported: a fresh session resuming calibration should rerun it cleanly rather than try to pick up where it left off.

**Environment note (2026-09-18):** a fresh container again had no `dx`. GitHub over HTTPS is blocked by this environment's egress policy (403 on `api.github.com` and on release downloads) but `git fetch` from github.com works — forks and upstream `refs/pull/N/head` included — and crates.io works, so `cargo install dioxus-cli --version 0.7.9 --locked` (~20 min on 4 cores) is the route. `dx` then self-compiles wasm-bindgen from source when its GitHub download fails (144 s, into `~/.local/share/.dx/tools/wasm-bindgen-0.2.121/`), but its own reqwest client (webpki roots) rejects the proxy CA even for `NO_PROXY` hosts, so the other two tools were placed by hand at the paths `dx` checks first: esbuild 0.27.3 from the npm tgz (curl trusts the CA) at `~/.local/share/.dx/tools/esbuild-0.27.3/esbuild`, and `wasm-opt` at `~/.local/share/.dx/tools/binaryen-129/bin/wasm-opt` as a wrapper over the npm `binaryen@129.0.0` JS/wasm build (Ubuntu's apt `binaryen` is version 108 — it accepts every flag `dx` passes but predates the sign-ext/multivalue defaults rustc 1.94 emits, so it was not used). Parallel lanes ran in git worktrees sharing one cargo build directory (`~/.cargo/config.toml` `build.target-dir`), which reuses compiled dependencies across worktrees; cargo serialises on its lock, so "Blocking waiting for file lock" is expected, not a hang.

**New lints and tools landed this session, all wired to rows 31b/32:** two mechanical-check scripts joined `scripts/check-preview-composition.sh` and `scripts/check-cfg-axis.sh` — `scripts/check-dx-class-prefix.sh` (every themed component's shipped classes must be namespaced `dx-<component>[-...]`, the collision-safety property `#[css_module]` hashing used to provide, now that row 32 has dropped it) and `scripts/check-css-literals.sh` (a themed stylesheet may not hard-code a value that exactly matches a row-31a design token, enforcing row 31b's migration against drift). Both should run alongside the existing gates before any CSS-touching change lands. Two Playwright files are TOOLS rather than oracles, not run as part of the normal suite: `playwright/dx-class-migration.spec.ts` (generic per-component delivery + unhashed-class assertions — this is the one that caught `sidebar`'s stylesheet not reaching its iframe-embedded demo, and now also the calendar/date_picker one row 51 fixed) and `playwright/computed-style-snapshot.spec.ts` (skipped unless `DX_SNAPSHOT` is set; captures computed styles across every themed page so a literal→token migration can be proven value-preserving by diff rather than by eye).

**Update, later the same date:** priorities 1 and 2 below have now both landed — see rows 10, 26, 27, 50, 51, 52, 53, and 54 for the individual accounts. This block's numbering stays as a record of the order actually taken, rather than being renumbered out from under the rows it points at; the paragraph after the table says what a fresh session should pick up now that both are clear. **Verified by a full Chromium run: 162 passed / 3 failed.** Of the 3: two are `top-layer.spec.ts` Rule 8 cases, pre-existing and failing 100% in this sandbox — row 21's own documented anchor-freeze, not a regression from anything landed this round; the third was a genuine `button_group` axe `button-name` violation (the two icon-only buttons — see row 27), fixed in the same round, so the run's own 3-failed count already reflects a defect this pass caught and closed, not one still outstanding.

1. **Known bugs — empty of blockers, all the way through.** Row 26 (route DOM leak) closed as not-reproduced across chromium/firefox/webkit, and its own `dropdown-menu.spec.ts` workaround is now removed, since the workaround's stated mechanism was disproved, not merely unconfirmed. Row 37 (dashboard email client's `nested-interactive` axe violation) landed and verified by execution; the top-layer ink-colour defect (row 50) is closed, including its first-paint-window residue (see row 50 below). Row 51 — `Calendar`'s stylesheet failing to load on the `date_picker` route, the one small unfixed finding this step used to carry forward — is now **landed**: the fix and the audit that found five siblings clean are both recorded on that row. Everything else this step used to list stays landed: row 47 (Select full width) and row 25 (`ContextMenu`/`Menubar` submenu `aria-labelledby`) as of 2026-09-03/04; rows 36, 38, and 44 as of 2026-09-04.
2. **Phase 5 residual + nested submenus — landed, including the last deliberately-left-open piece.** Row 10's vertical shift and size clamp landed (new Rule 15), mirroring the 2026-09-03 horizontal fix construction exactly; nested submenus landed too (row 54, `DropdownMenu.Sub`/`ContextMenu.Sub`, built on the top-layer engine this item was waiting on). **2026-09-14: `ContextMenu`'s point-anchor clamp (5.2) has now also landed** — see row 10's own addendum for the construction (`top_layer::use_point_anchor_clamp`, a small parallel hook, not a branch inside `use_anchor_position_fallback`). The one thing that does NOT count as done here and should not be mistaken for it: the acknowledged conforming-engine CSS gap row 10 also carries forward (deliberately not mirrored into the `@supports` contract — see that row for why; no CSS primitive exists for either the anchor-relative shift/size clamp or this point-anchor one, so this is not a gap 5.2's landing could have closed). Alongside this, a **new, separate item landed**: the menu-family role consolidation's stage 1 (row 52, Navbar now reads its menu-pattern roles from `menu_semantics` instead of hand-writing a fourth set), with a stage-2 assessment recorded but explicitly **not built** (row 53 — a scoped three-band extraction, not a single shared submenu component).
3. **Row 27 — landed.** All nine styled-only components (Alert, Breadcrumb, Button Group, Empty, Field, Input Group, Kbd, Spinner, Native Select), authored against the row-31a tokens in the row-32 styling system, checked against both new lints above, each with its own smoke + axe spec. One axe finding along the way, fixed the same round: see row 27 for the two icon-only `button_group` buttons axe's `button-name` rule caught.

**What a fresh session should pick up next, now that 1 and 2 above are clear:**

4. **The three-band menu extraction from row 53 — landed 2026-09-14.** No longer something a fresh session needs to pick up; see row 53's own account for the construction (`primitives/src/menu_root.rs`) and its Escape-refocus finding (fixed in `Navbar`, root-caused as a genuine oversight rather than a deliberate hover-model exemption). Kept as its own numbered item, not renumbered away, matching items 3's and 5's identical treatment above.

Kept as its own numbered item, not renumbered away, so this list stays a record of what was actually recommended and taken, matching item 3's identical treatment above.
6. **The cheap primitives wave** — Command, then Input OTP, then Drawer/Navigation Menu/Resizable, per the re-rated effort table in [`component-backlog.md`](./component-backlog.md). **Command landed 2026-09-14** — new `primitives/src/command.rs` (original composition against `Combobox`'s own underlying `crate::selectable`/`crate::listbox`/`crate::collection` layer, `Dialog`, and `SelectGroup`/`SelectGroupLabel`'s ported shape; the two reference forks' own `command.rs` files read for shape only, neither vendored), themed `preview/src/components/command/**`, oracle `playwright/command.spec.ts` (smoke + axe — see that file's own header for why no tier-1 APG citation applies), `cargo check`/`fmt`/`clippy` and all four `check-*.sh` lints plus `stylelint` clean. **First real-browser run of `command.spec.ts` (same day) found 2 of 9 tests failing** — a real `aria-dialog-name` axe violation (the demo never rendered a `DialogTitle`, so `DialogRoot`'s always-on `aria-labelledby` pointed at nothing) and a `page.goto` timeout on one test, root-caused to sandbox CPU contention during concurrent cold WASM compilation, not a product defect — both fixed same day, re-verified 9/9 (twice). See `component-backlog.md`'s Command row and its Recommendations item 1 (and addendum) for the full account. **Input OTP landed 2026-09-14 too** (`dev-docs/component-backlog.md`'s own row has the full account — original composition on `Checkbox`'s `BubbleInput` *shape*, not a port; upstream PR #255 re-confirmed still draft/blocked; 5/5 real Playwright pass, all lints clean). Drawer, Navigation Menu, Resizable remain next in this wave.
7. **CI reactivation items** — rows 4, 22, 33, whenever the user lifts the CI freeze. These are **user-gated, not sequenced**: they don't wait their turn behind 4–6, they wait on a decision outside this ranking entirely, and can land whenever that decision changes regardless of where the rest of this list stands.

Row numbers above are not renumbered — this is a reading order layered on the table, not a new ranking of it.

## 2026-09-18 — queue evaluation and what was taken

A fresh session was asked to evaluate this queue and, if it concurred, continue. **Verdict: concur, with three corrections**, all applied the same day (evidence on the rows they point at):

1. **The ordering rule stands and the queue state was accurate.** Rows 55–65 (PRs #51–57) were user-reported docs-site and Input OTP fixes; the known-bugs step is empty again; Phase 5 is complete (`plan.md`'s header still said "partially done" — refreshed).

Taken.
3. **Six "Ready", S-rated rows idled behind the wave with no stated reason** — rows 7, 8, 9, 11, 12, 19 — whose file sets are disjoint from the wave's, so under `CLAUDE.md`'s lane rule they run *alongside* it, not after it. Taken as parallel lanes.
4. **An unstated dependency: row 7 depends on row 19.** Row 7 was marked "Unblocked", but row 19 records that the web arm's `hidePopover()` fires before any close animation can play, so the fade-out CSS alone is invisible there. Sequenced 19 → 7 in one lane.

| 10 | **Phase 5** — collision detection | **5.1 REFRAMED by the 2026-09-01 bits-ui/Radix source comparison, FLIP sub-problem landed same day, PR #17**: both delegate to Floating UI (identical flip/shift/size/arrow/hide pipelines; bits-ui is a line-for-line Svelte port of Radix's Popper). Since CSS Anchor Positioning is now in our stack, `position-try-fallbacks: flip-block, flip-inline` covers the FLIP sub-problem natively (Chrome/Edge 125+, FF 147+, Safari 26+), zero JS, additive to the existing @supports blocks — landed, and `use_anchor_position_fallback` makes the same flip decision from viewport math on engines without native support. **2026-09-03: the JS-fallback path's first piece of shift also landed** (user device report — the home page widget-masonry `ColorPicker` clipping against a small screen's edge; `top-layer.spec.ts` Rule 12): `use_anchor_position_fallback` now clamps `target.left` into the viewport with a small edge margin, on top of the existing flip decision, closing the gap for `side="top"`/`"bottom"` placements (whose horizontal position comes from `align` alone and was never checked against the viewport on *either* engine before this). **2026-09-04: the vertical shift and size clamp landed too**, in `use_anchor_position_fallback` — new **Rule 15** in `top-layer.spec.ts`, passing. Two pieces, the exact mirror of the horizontal fix: (1) a `side="left"`/`"right"` placement's *vertical* position — like a `side="top"`/`"bottom"` placement's horizontal one — came only from `align` and was never checked against the viewport, clamped into `[EDGE_MARGIN, vh - EDGE_MARGIN - ch]` the same way; (2) when the content's natural height exceeds `vh - 2*EDGE_MARGIN`, an inline `max-height`/`overflow-y: auto` is set (and `box-sizing: border-box` forced alongside it, since `offsetHeight` is always border-box but `max-height` is defined relative to whatever `box-sizing` the element carries — under `content-box`, padding/border would silently under-clamp), cleared again once no longer needed. Deliberately **not** mirrored into the CSS `@supports` contract, and the reasoning is worth keeping on record: `position-try-fallbacks` has no shift primitive and no shipped primitive shrinks an anchor box to fit, so a conforming engine has the identical latent gap — this is not Firefox/WebKit-specific, it is a real gap in the standard as shipped today. The size clamp uses an inline style rather than the width contract's `--dx-anchor-width` custom-property approach (row 47) specifically because a zero-specificity `:where()` rule can never beat a component's own `overflow: visible` (`select/style.css`'s own comment: "never scrolls ... collision-aware ... positioning belongs upstream" — this clamp is that upstream). **2026-09-14: 5.2, `ContextMenu`'s point-anchor clamp, has now landed too — the one piece of this row that was left deliberately open.** `ContextMenu` opens at a raw click/long-press point (`ContextMenuCtx::position`, set by `ContextMenuTrigger`'s mouse `contextmenu` handler and its manual touch/pen long-press timer) with no anchor element at all — `use_anchor_position_fallback` above cannot cover it regardless of engine, not because of a Firefox/WebKit gap but because that function is keyed on resolving an anchor element via `document.querySelector('[style*="anchor-name: ..."]')`, and `ContextMenuContentRendered` never wires up an `anchor-name` for this content in the first place (see that component's own doc, the `manual`-vs-`auto` section, for why: there is no persistent trigger to key positioning off of). Closed by a **new, parallel construction** in `top_layer.rs`, `use_point_anchor_clamp` — not a branch threaded through `use_anchor_position_fallback` itself: that function's script is already long, heavily cross-referenced, and execution-verified around anchor-relative `place()`/`matches()`/flip machinery this case has no use for, so adding a no-anchor special case there was judged a real risk to its own passing oracle coverage (rules 5-7, 9, 11-15) for a case that shares only the final clamp *arithmetic*. The new hook reuses exactly that arithmetic — the same `EDGE_MARGIN` (4px) convention, the same `Math.max` viewport-too-small-to-fit-at-all fallback, the same `border-box`-forced max-height/`overflow-y: auto` clamp `use_anchor_position_fallback`'s own 2026-09-04 vertical-shift-and-size-clamp addendum above describes — against the raw `(x, y)` point directly instead of an anchor rect, measuring `ContextMenuContentRendered`'s own rendered size once it's promoted to the top layer (called right after `use_popover_sync` in that component, the same ordering requirement `use_anchor_position_fallback` documents for its own call sites). No scroll/resize tracking and no multi-frame settle loop, unlike `use_anchor_position_fallback`: `ContextMenuContent`'s items are ordinary synchronously-rendered children, not `DatePickerPopover`'s asynchronously-growing calendar grid, and the menu already suppresses page scroll while open (its own pre-existing wheel/touchmove effect) rather than following it. `menu_sub.rs`'s `ContextMenuSub`/`ContextMenuSubTrigger`/`ContextMenuSubContent` were checked and confirmed **not** to share this bug: a submenu anchors to its own `ContextMenuSubTrigger` (an ordinary menu item sitting inside the already-positioned root content), the same anchor-relative shape `DropdownMenuSub` uses, so it already goes through `use_anchor_position_fallback` like every other anchored overlay — untouched by this fix. Regression oracle: two new `playwright/context-menu.spec.ts` cases ("clamps the menu into the viewport for a mouse right-click near a corner" / "... for a touch long-press near a corner"), covering both of `ContextMenuTrigger`'s `position.set(...)` call sites — not `top-layer.spec.ts`, whose own header doc explicitly disclaims this exact case (Migration A slice 2/3's note: "Phase 5.2's point-anchor case is explicitly out of scope for this file"). **Verified by actual execution (Chromium, this sandbox), 2026-09-14:** `cargo check --workspace` and `cargo fmt --check` clean (no new warnings beyond the two pre-existing, unrelated ones already on `main`); `cargo clippy` raises no new warnings anywhere outside the confirmed pre-existing, unrelated `menu_sub.rs` doc-lint failures (present unchanged on `main` at `124c4d5`). The two new cases pass. **A third case regressed and was root-caused, not just re-run until green:** the pre-existing `menu lands at the tap coordinates on touch long-press` case started failing after this fix landed (menu Y off by ~187px) — verified by a direct baseline comparison (same test, same browser, run against `124c4d5` before this fix: passes) that this was a real, reproducible difference correlated with the fix, not a flake. Root cause, confirmed by instrumented execution: **not a bug in `use_point_anchor_clamp`.** The test's own premise (push the trigger down with `padding-top: 300px` on `<main>` so the tap point sits "away from any edge") had gone stale — on this component's current preview-page chrome, that padding places the trigger's center at `y ≈ 735` against a 720px-tall default viewport, 15px *past* the bottom edge, not away from it. That was already true on `124c4d5`, before `use_point_anchor_clamp` existed — nothing corrected it yet, so the unclamped menu simply rendered past the fold and the test's tap-coordinate assertion passed vacuously (it never checked on-screen visibility, only that the menu's top-left matched the tap point). `use_point_anchor_clamp` landing gave the clamp something to correct for the first time, and it correctly pulls the menu back on-screen (measured: `vh=720`, `tapY≈735`, content height `168`, for which `top + ch > vh - EDGE_MARGIN` is genuinely true) — a real, intended behavior change exposing a latent bad premise in the test, not a regression in the fix. Fixed by pinning the trigger to an explicit `position: fixed; top: 120px; left: 160px` instead of relying on ambient padding, making "nowhere near an edge" true by construction rather than by an increasingly-stale coincidence. **All 13 cases in `context-menu.spec.ts` pass together** after this correction. A firefox/webkit run has not been attempted. Still open in this row: only the acknowledged conforming-engine CSS gap described above (no CSS primitive exists for the point-anchor case either — a raw click point was never something `anchor()` could place, so there is nothing for a future `@supports` rule to cover here even in principle) | S (flip, done) + S (JS-fallback shift, done) + S (vertical/size clamp, done) + S (5.2, ContextMenu point-anchor, done 2026-09-14) | Keep the CSS clamp (5.3) regardless — still pending, see `recommended-implementations.md` §6. Two adoptable ideas from the comparison, not yet taken: bits-ui's debounced nested-lock cleanup (~24ms, survives same-tick unmount/remount races), and Radix's right-click carve-out for backdrop dismiss |
| 11 | Phase 6 — typeahead for menus (`dq typeahead.rs`, **not** `select/`) | **LANDED 2026-09-18.** APG "Menu and Menubar" pattern, Keyboard Interaction (pinned commit `7e4034b` of `w3c/aria-practices`): "Any key that corresponds to a printable character (Optional): Move focus to the next item in the current menu whose label begins with that printable character." None of `DropdownMenu`/`ContextMenu`/`Menubar` implemented this; a conformance test (new rows in `playwright/oracle/tier1-apg/keyboard-matrix.spec.ts`, 12 rows, menu sections only) was written first and would fail red against the pre-existing code. Construction: ported `dignifiedquire`'s `typeahead.rs` *concept* (prefix matching over a timed buffer) — not `select/`'s deliberately-kept Levenshtein matcher (`recommended-implementations.md` §8 already settled that comparison; this row's own trigger note said "not `select/`", honored). New `primitives/src/typeahead.rs`: a pure `find_next_match(labels, current, buffer)` function (unit-tested: wrap, skip-disabled, same-character cycling, case-insensitivity, Radix-parity narrowing) plus `TypeaheadState`, mirroring `select/context.rs`'s buffer/timeout idiom (`Signal<String>` + a self-cancelling clear `Task` via `dioxus_sdk_time::sleep`, 1000ms window). Wired into each host's existing keydown handler next to its Arrow/Home/End arms, and into `menu_sub.rs`'s `DropdownMenuSub`/`ContextMenuSub` content handlers too, since the collection plumbing made it free; never intercepts Ctrl/Meta/Alt-held keys or Space. Item labels: an explicit optional `text_value` prop (on `DropdownMenuItem`/`SubItem`, `ContextMenuItem`/`SubItem`, `MenubarItem`, and the Sub triggers), falling back to the item's own value where meaningful; `collection.rs` gained one additive `text_value` channel through the existing per-item registration plus a `text_entries()` accessor — not a second item registry (commit `6b724bb`). **Follow-up defect found same day:** each menu-family themed wrapper (`preview/src/components/{dropdown_menu,context_menu,menubar}/component.rs`) takes the primitive's own `*Props` type directly but re-lists fields individually when constructing the inner primitive component, so adding `text_value` to the primitive props did not automatically forward it through the wrapper — a caller setting `text_value` on a themed component had it silently dropped before reaching `collection.rs`'s registration. `cargo check` alone did not catch it (an unused/dropped prop field is not a compile error); found by re-checking the compiled demo actually carries the label through. Fixed at all seven forwarding sites this bug class touches, not just the one the demo happened to need, per this repo's "two occurrences means a class" convention (commit `330211b`). | S–M | Also a prerequisite-ish for Command palette in the component queue. 129 lib + 120 doc tests passed (10 new typeahead unit tests); clippy clean outside the pre-existing `menu_sub.rs` doc-lint. Playwright not run by the lane |

Filed **tier 3** (Radix-parity, labelled opinion), not tier 1/2: `@radix-ui/react-direction` and each component's `useDirection` consumption is Radix's own design, not a W3C/APG requirement. `Resizable`/`Calendar` have no Radix/shadcn-with-Radix-primitive original to cite (shadcn wraps `react-resizable-panels`/`react-day-picker`, neither a Radix primitive); their rules are labelled an extrapolation, not a citation. 25 rules, 25/25 on the lane's own server; scoped through `#component-preview-frame`/`#component-preview-frame-<variant>`, since every variant of a component now renders on the same page at once (the same architectural fact behind row 45's own menubar/navbar locator fix). **The CSS half** (rtl-css lane + this batch's integration): 29 stylesheets converted from physical to logical properties by category (accordion, alert_dialog, calendar, collapsible, color_picker, combobox, command, context_menu, dialog, drag_and_drop_list, dropdown_menu, hover_card, input_otp, item, menubar, native_select, navbar, pagination, popover, progress, select, sidebar, slider, switch, table, toast, toggle, toggle_group, tooltip) — reading-direction concepts (`text-align`, `margin-left`/`-right`, `left`/`right` anchors) converted to their logical equivalents; screen-geometry facts (`data-side`, a drawer/sheet's own `side` prop) and transform-paired centering (`left: 50%` + `translate(-50%, ...)`, no logical equivalent in any shipping engine) kept physical, marked `/* rtl-physical: <reason> */`; non-zero `translateX`/keyframe values needing a sign flip got a `[dir="rtl"]` counterpart instead (navbar mobile menu, switch thumb, progress sweep, sidebar indent). | 21 | **RESOLVED 2026-10-05 (round 3 final verification); the engine diagnosis is WITHDRAWN** -- was: Residual env-only anchor flake: top-layer ColorPicker CSS-path test fails ~40% in this sandbox (initial anchor() freezes at a wrong offset, correlated with slow page loads; end users unaffected — the JS fallback compensates) | **RESOLVED 2026-10-05** -- was: a different environment (real CI machine) to check reproducibility, or a Chromium anchor-positioning fix upstream | S (done) | Investigated 2026-09-01: hook-side settle-loop bug fixed, scroll-at-open quirk worked around test-side; this residue looks like a browser-build engine bug, deliberately not papered over. **2026-09-03 addendum:** the same failure shape (a `toBeCloseTo` position mismatch after CSS-native anchor tracking) now also reproduces on `date-picker.spec.ts`'s two anchoring tests in this sandbox, confirmed pre-existing and unrelated to the row 34 axe-coverage round (reproduces identically against `git stash`-ed, unmodified `HEAD`) — this is the same engine-level class, not a new, separate defect, and not something the axe round touched. **2026-09-04 addendum, re-measured with a working Playwright container for the first time:** date-picker's two anchoring tests and `top-layer.spec.ts`'s two Rule 8 scroll-tracking tests failed **100% (0/23 across reruns)**, up from the ~40% previously recorded here, all with the same "frozen after scroll" signature (content offset drifts by exactly the scroll delta). Chrome build 148.0.7778.96 (headless shell). None of `top_layer.rs`/`date_picker.rs`/`calendar.rs` were touched this session, so this is not self-inflicted. One of the four failing tests is the **JS fallback** path specifically (`use_anchor_position_fallback`, `top-layer.spec.ts:814`), which is this repo's own code rather than native `anchor()` — its participation in the identical failure signature deserves a separate look at whether it is a distinct bug in that hook's own scroll listener, rather than simply inherited bad initial state from the native-anchor case. Also measured, for the date-picker case specifically, a separate/narrower observation (not a competing explanation for the scroll-tracking failure above): the content is 278px tall while only ~275px of room exists below the trigger at the 720px-tall default viewport, so a native `flip-block` fires 3px short of fitting; at a 1000px-tall viewport the same content places correctly below with an 8px gap. **2026-09-19 addendum (batch 2 integration, row 82):** both of `date-picker.spec.ts`'s anchoring tests are now named explicitly, closing the ambiguity row 82's own docs-pass notes flagged — this row's class covers "anchors the popup next to its trigger, not viewport-centered or offset" (the one already tracked here since 2026-09-03) and "offset to trigger is unchanged after scrolling (CSS anchor tracks scroll natively)" (seen failing in one full-suite run and absent in another, consistent with this row's own load-timing correlation rather than a new, separate defect). **2026-09-19 addendum (batch 3, date-picker lane, re-measured with more precision):** content 278px vs. 275.03px of room below the trigger (2.97px short, not just "~275px" as recorded above) — the native `flip-block` fallback consequently places the popover ABOVE the trigger (measured `contentTop` 138.97 vs. `triggerBottom` 444.97), which is the exact, now-confirmed mechanism behind "anchors the popup below" failing; the scroll-tracking test's own jump was remeasured at 314px. Not independently re-investigated further this round, per this row's own standing instruction not to paper over a browser-engine-level residue **2026-10-05 (round 3, FINAL VERIFICATION) -- THE ENGINE DIAGNOSIS ABOVE IS WITHDRAWN for both cases, kept only as history:** `top-layer.spec.ts` Rule 8 (ColorPicker) and `date-picker.spec.ts` `:73`/`:144` were never an engine bug or a frozen anchor. They were test geometry plus a selector bug: the panels do not fit below their trigger at the default 1280x720 viewport (date picker 278 px tall against 274 px of room below a trigger at y=446; ColorPicker 386 px tall under a trigger at y~453), so `flip-block` correctly opens them ABOVE the trigger, and a 150 px scroll frees room and flips them back, which read as "frozen / drifted" (the 314 px and 436 px jumps recorded above are the flip, not a tracking failure); and, since the header theme picker landed (`b9b8e1f7`), `document.querySelector('[style*="anchor-name"]')` matched that header button first, so the tests measured the wrong trigger. Fixed in the specs (a taller viewport, a precondition assert, the real locators; row 172 has the account), and `top-layer.spec.ts` Rule 8 (ColorPicker) now PASSES, so it is no longer red and round 3's earlier "still red, pre-existing" note is superseded. The JS-fallback case (`top-layer.spec.ts:814`) was not among the final release run's failures. Nothing here needs a different machine or an upstream fix. |

| 31 | **Design tokens (option A) — split in two (user decision 2026-09-03).** **31a — LANDED 2026-09-04.** Token set added to `preview/assets/dx-components-theme.css` (today colour + dark-mode only): type scale (9 steps), spacing scale (16 steps on the 4px grid, matching Tailwind v4's default), radius scale (8), elevation/shadow (5, dark-mode-off per existing convention), motion durations/easings, breakpoints (reference-only — `var()` does not resolve inside `@media`, stated in-file), z-index scale (9 steps, all 25 existing literals map 1:1) — as CSS custom properties, which survive any outcome of row 32 (Tailwind v4's `@theme` block *is* CSS variables). Every scale was derived from a literal survey of the 46 component stylesheets, not invented; `line-height`/`font-weight` were deliberately NOT tokenised (no clean clusters emerged). The touch floor (row 30) is folded into `--dx-text-base`; row 39's ≥4.5:1 contrast requirement is stated in-file as a rule for future token pairs. Also landed: a small **generated utility sheet** — new `scripts/generate-dx-utilities.js` emits 336 atomic `dx-`-prefixed classes (spacing, gap, flex/grid basics, text sizes, radius, shadow) to `preview/assets/dx-utilities.css` from the token file, with a `--check` staleness mode, so it cannot drift; variants (`hover:`, `md:`, `data-[state]:`) stay in component rules, not in the generator. A shared `:where([data-dx-interactive])` interaction-rules layer (hover opacity / focus-visible ring / disabled opacity / reduced-motion, driven by tokens) was also added but is **scaffolding only**: nothing renders the `data-dx-interactive` attribute yet (wiring it onto components is `primitives/src` work), so the hover-opacity and disabled-opacity rules are inert by design; reduced-motion IS live today, via the same tag/role selector list `main.css` already uses for `touch-action`. **31b — LANDED 2026-09-04.** 683 substitutions across 44 component stylesheets, EXACT-value matches only: a literal is replaced solely when it equals a token's value in the token's own unit (`8px` where a token says `0.5rem` is left alone and logged as an informational note, never auto-swapped — that would be a real behaviour change under a consumer overriding the root font-size, a design decision rather than a mechanical one). Verified value-preserving by measurement, not assertion: `playwright/computed-style-snapshot.spec.ts` (a TOOL, skipped unless `DX_SNAPSHOT` is set) captured 24 computed properties across all 7,774 `dx-` elements on the 46 component pages before and after the migration — the diff is exactly one entry, `dx-progress-indicator`'s width, which re-samples to 34/54/32/54px on a single *unchanged* build because that demo animates, so zero real differences. `scripts/check-css-literals.sh` (new lint) enforces this going forward: exact-unit matches only, property-family-aware token suggestions (so `gap: 1rem` is never suggested as `var(--dx-text-base)` just because `1rem` also happens to be a type-scale step), scoped to the root `component.json`'s `members` (excludes `top_layer`, an oracle fixture whose hand-tuned geometry is deliberately literal). Several traps were correctly declined rather than tokenised: `tabs`' dark-branch inset ring, `dialog`/`sheet`/`toast`'s shadows with no dark/light wrapper (tokenising would have silently dropped the shadow in dark mode), `999px` (not `9999px`), and non-`--dx-ring` focus rings (3px, `color-mix()`, or component-local). **Important correction, discovered mid-round, worth recording as a third instance of a known bug class:** a first attempt consolidated 11 components' `:focus-visible` rings into the shared interaction layer by naming their classes directly in the global theme sheet (`.dx-checkbox:focus-visible`, …). That cannot work: `#[css_module]` hashes each class in **both** the DOM and the component's own stylesheet, so an unhashed selector written in a *different*, global sheet never matches — confirmed against the built artifact (the wasm carries `dx-alert-dialog-cancel-e59ac016` while the theme sheet said `.dx-alert-dialog-cancel`). Shipping it would have silently removed the focus ring from 11 controls (WCAG 2.4.7), so it did not ship: each component instead keeps its own `:focus-visible` rule, rewritten against the new `--dx-ring` token. Consolidation becomes possible once row 32 drops `css_module` hashing, or once `data-dx-interactive` is wired onto components. This is the **third** instance of the `css_module` hashing/scoping class — after row 32's own originating instance (the shared anchor-positioning `@supports` workaround) and row 47's (`select/style.css`'s dead `@supports` block) — cross-reference all three | **31a: landed 2026-09-04. 31b: landed 2026-09-04** | 31a S–M (done); 31b M (done) | Evidence and per-axis counts: the 2026-09-02 design-system research (`recommended-implementations.md`, design-system note); non-breaking for consumers either way (`dx components add` copies the same theme file, richer) |

| 40 | **`Auto`-kind popovers' exposure to the opening-gesture light-dismiss false positive — audited 2026-09-03; Navbar half FIXED 2026-10-04 (`d9bcfda`), the Menubar/DropdownMenu/Select/Popover half closed as refuted 2026-10-03.** `DropdownMenu`/`Menubar`/`Select`/non-modal `Popover` (`PopoverKind::Auto`) rely on the browser's own native light-dismiss algorithm; an engine whose touch-to-click synthesis can retarget a click at whatever DOM mutation just occurred under it (the same class of quirk `native-dialog.spec.ts` Rule 8 fixes for the modal arm's own JS listener) could in principle read the tap that opened one of these as "outside" too, since none of their triggers declare a `popovertarget` invoker. Not reproducible in this sandbox's Chromium (confirmed by execution, touch-emulated) | **Navbar half FIXED 2026-10-04 (`d9bcfda`, verified on the release build of `b9e9580`); the rest CLOSED as refuted 2026-10-03, so no device is needed any more.** Original trigger: A real device confirming this actually happens (unlike the modal-arm case, no construction fix was applied here this round — see below for why), or a report against one of these four specifically | M (done) | **2026-09-26 (audit correction):** audit-only by design — no action expected here without a real-device report confirming the issue actually occurs. `popovertarget` (WHATWG's own declared-invoker answer) was evaluated and rejected for this round: every one of these four triggers is a *toggle* button (`onclick`/`onpointerup` computing `!open`), and `popovertarget`'s default action is `"toggle"` too — layering a second, browser-native toggle onto the same click as this crate's own Rust-driven one risks a double-toggle race with no way to verify the outcome without a real device. Switching to `Manual` + `use_outside_dismiss` (Combobox's own precedent, `combobox/components/list.rs`) was also considered and deferred: it would lose `auto`'s free cross-instance exclusivity (opening one closes unrelated open ones) for four components with green existing suites, a larger behavior change than a single unreproduced device report justifies on its own. **2026-10-03 re-audit (main @ 2241da8) -- the premise is refuted for the click-open family; a different, real exposure was found and a probe added; fix deferred until the probe is run.** (a) **Refuted:** the WebKit quirk this row (and `native-dialog.spec.ts` Rule 8) worries about retargets a *synthesized `click`*. The browser-native light-dismiss algorithm never reads `click`; it uses only trusted `pointerdown` + `pointerup` (WHATWG HTML "popover light dismiss", fetched 2026-10-03), and a popover that is not yet open at `pointerdown`/`pointerup` is ignored entirely ("if topmost auto popover is null, return"). `DropdownMenu` (`dropdown_menu.rs:530` `onclick`), `Select` (`select/components/trigger.rs:244` `onclick`) and non-modal `Popover` (`popover.rs:871` `onclick`) open from `click`, i.e. after `pointerup`, so they cannot be dismissed by their own opening gesture on any engine; `Menubar` opens from `onpointerup` (`menubar.rs:621`), the same. No `popovertarget` is needed, which also dissolves the double-toggle worry in the 2026-09-26 note. (b) **The real exposure the audit missed:** `NavbarTrigger` opens on `onpointerdown` (`navbar.rs:621-632`) and its content is `popover="auto"` (`navbar.rs:907`). If `showPopover()` lands between that gesture's `pointerdown` and `pointerup` (any hold longer than Dioxus's render+effect, which a real finger always is), `pointerup` sees an open auto popover, `sameTarget` is `null === null` = true, and the browser hides it. `navbar.spec.ts` 'mobile navigation' cannot see it (Playwright `tap()` has zero hold); on a real device `NavbarNav`'s compat `onmouseenter` can re-open it and mask the flash. NOT confirmed by execution (no build this lane). **Probe:** `playwright/oracle/tier2-html/auto-popover-opening-gesture.spec.ts` (CDP touchStart, 250ms hold, touchEnd; asserts no `toggle`->closed on any `[popover]`; Navbar = suspected red, DropdownMenu = green control). **If the Navbar case is red the fix by construction is to stop opening an `auto` popover from `pointerdown`:** open `NavbarTrigger` on `click` (keeping the pointerdown `prevent_default()` for the blur guard, `navbar.rs:624-630`), or move `NavbarContent` to `manual` + `use_outside_dismiss` (Combobox precedent). That subsumes row 40's Navbar half and the class "auto popover opened mid-gesture"; it does not subsume the genuinely device-only iOS retarget case for the modal arm (already fixed, Rule 8). If green, close this row as refuted **2026-10-04 resolution (NAVBAR lane; Navbar half FIXED by construction, probe confirmed RED on a release build before the fix):** `toggles=[{open},{closed}]` for a 250ms-held touch on `NavbarTrigger` (HTML light dismiss: pointerdown target popover null === pointerup null), DropdownMenu green. Fix = option (a): `NavbarTrigger` now toggles on `onclick` (after `pointerup`, so a native light dismiss can never see its own opening gesture); `onpointerdown` keeps only `prevent_default()` (blur guard: tapping a child menuitem must not blur the trigger) plus a snapshot `open_at_down = Some(is_open())`, which `onclick` consumes. The snapshot is needed because on touch `NavbarNav`'s compat `onmouseenter` hover-open fires between `pointerup` and `click` -- toggling off live `is_open()` would close the menu the tap just opened; desktop hover-open precedes `pointerdown`, so the snapshot is `true` and click closes, as before. A click with no snapshot (Space activation, AT) falls back to live state; any `keydown` clears a stale snapshot. Keyboard (Enter/ArrowDown/ArrowUp on `NavbarNav`, Escape, roving focus) and hover-open untouched; native (non-web) arm shares the same trigger code, no cfg change. Class audit: grep of every `popover="auto"` consumer (`dropdown_menu` content + sub, `context_menu` sub, `menubar`, `popover`, `select`) -- none other opens from `pointerdown` (they open from `click`/`pointerup`/hover timers/keyboard); `ContextMenu` main content is `manual`. Class made a written contract on `top_layer::PopoverKind::Auto` ("auto popovers are shown only from click/pointerup/keyboard/hover-compat/timer, never `onpointerdown`"), pinned by the probe. Row 40's Menubar/DropdownMenu/Select/Popover half stays closed as refuted (2026-10-03). Verification still owed by the build lane: probe + `navbar.spec.ts` + keyboard-matrix + top-layer. **2026-10-04 verification record:** the probe was RED on release 9f8399d before the fix (`toggles=[{open},{closed}]` for a 250 ms held touch on `NavbarTrigger`, i.e. open -> closed; `DropdownMenuTrigger` GREEN control and the spec's non-vacuity assertion passed; `baseline/RELEASE.md` section 3b), so the exposure is real on release, not just in reading. Verification of the fix `d9bcfda` against the final build: on the release build of `b9e9580` (2026-10-04) the probe `auto-popover-opening-gesture.spec.ts` is 2/2 GREEN (Navbar: no `open`->`closed` toggle under the 250 ms held touch; DropdownMenu control still green, non-vacuity assertion passed) and `navbar.spec.ts` 5/5, `keyboard-matrix.spec.ts` 77 passed / 1 skipped, so the red-before / green-after pair on the same build type confirms the fix. |

Cites the W3C Long Tasks API (a long task is, verbatim, any occurrence whose duration exceeds 50ms) and the W3C Event Timing API (`durationThreshold`, clamped to a 16ms floor by spec, not an arbitrary choice) — both reachable, HTTP 200, checked 2026-09-18. Thresholds unchanged from this row's own original wording: 50ms/task, 200ms/interaction. Red proof without a build (kept out of the repo, per this row's own instruction): a synthetic 120ms busy `pointerdown` handler turned the drawer/slider drag tests red (137/147ms); a synthetic 250ms busy `click` handler turned dialog-open red (312ms). **55/58 green** across four full runs (Chromium-only skip for the rest, this row's own original caveat, unchanged). **Three subjects RED BY DESIGN, load-independent** (measured at loadavg 1.2 through 17+, same signature every time): `combobox: open` 59-75ms, `command: open` 59-83ms, `date_picker: open` 60-88ms — over the 50ms long-task threshold, under the 200ms interaction one. A dedicated debug lane (open-cost, no commits) root-caused the mechanism: DOM-node counts on open (dropdown_menu +10, combobox +10, select +14, command +39, date_picker +247 — 35 day cells each with dynamic attributes plus an `onmounted` effect) point at ONE mechanism behind two trigger sites — `top_layer.rs`'s `use_anchor_position_fallback`'s `reposition()` (combobox/select/date_picker) and native `showModal()` via `use_dialog_open_driver` (command) both perform a synchronous DOM mount plus a forced-layout read inside the same event-handler task (this row's own §11 rules 1 and 5). A one-frame deferral of combobox's `initial_focus` write was implemented and measured (66-139ms → 57-63ms — real, insufficient) then reverted, not committed. Three proposals for a dedicated future batch, none applied here: (1) defer the mount itself to the frame after `open` flips — most complete, HIGH risk, since `top_layer.rs`'s own comments record a rAF-wrapped `reposition()` that "silently never ran at all on a real Firefox build"; (2) defer `showModal()` promotion by one frame in `use_dialog_open_driver` (shared by Dialog/AlertDialog/modal Popover/Sheet) — partial win, medium-high risk; (3) give Combobox the menu family's deferred-collection-focus shape `collection.rs` already has. Needs a symbolicated wasm profile (debug info/sourcemap) to name the exact Rust function still costing ~55-65ms on a 10-node mount before any of the three proposals should be attempted. Do not widen the 50 ms bound; judge a main-thread red by its serial rerun, and keep timing specs on release builds only (debug builds give false reds, row 116). |
| 46 | **Every SSG-prerendered component page is the "Component not found" shell.** `dx build --ssg` prerenders `/component/?name=X&` as `<main class="dx-component-demo-not-found">` for every X (confirmed on the deployed bytes with `curl`, 2026-09-03) — a query-string route is not enumerable at build time, so the served HTML is the not-found branch and the client corrects it on hydration | **Closed 2026-09-19 (batch 3)** — fixed by construction, executed, and verified against a real production build | M | Construction: the path-segment route this row's own text anticipated — `/component/<name>/` is now enumerable by `Routable::static_routes()`, so `dx build --ssg` prerenders the REAL page for every component instead of the not-found branch; the legacy `?name=X&` links still resolve client-side (full account, dioxus-source citations and commits: row 22's own closure). **2026-09-04:** reinforced, not landed that session (see row 22's own 2026-09-04 addendum). **2026-09-19, verified by execution, not just architecture:** a production-shape build (release+ssg+fullstack) produced **71 routes, 0 shipped not-found shells** (`grep -rl dx-component-demo-not-found` against the built site — zero matches), and hydration-parity's now-extended Rule 5 ("no prerendered page serves the not-found shell") passes across all of them. **One small, unowned defect found and fixed at the same integration** (the SSG lane's own finding, not this row's original claim): the field demo's `<label>` elements carried a literal `class="dx-field-label"` attribute AND a spread `class="dx-label"` from `props.attributes` — two same-named attributes on one element is a WHATWG duplicate-attribute parse error (the same class `conformance-harness.md`'s hydration-parity Rule 4 paragraph already documents; server and client tie-break it differently). Fixed with `merge_attributes` (`5fc1439`, `preview/src/components/label/component.rs`), confirmed by Rule 4/4-extended now passing everywhere, not just the one field demo that surfaced it |
| 47 | **LANDED 2026-09-04: styled `Select` listbox rendered full viewport width on the home page gallery — the "dropdown menu has full page width" report, misidentified against `DropdownMenu` in the original (not-reproduced) write-up of this row.** Re-investigated against the actual subject: opening either styled `Select` on the home page (`/`, desktop 1280 wide, both the "Select an option" and "Choose a fruit" cards — also reproduces on `/component/?name=select&`) rendered `.dx-select-list-<hash>[popover]` at 1237–1247px wide against a 1280px viewport, clamped only by its own `max-width: calc(100vw - 2rem)`. Root cause: `min-width: 100%` in `preview/src/components/select/style.css` was written pre-migration, when this listbox was `position: absolute` inside `.dx-select`'s `position: relative` box (100% = trigger width); promoting it to the top layer via `popover` made its containing block the viewport instead, so the same percentage silently started meaning 100% of the *viewport*. A `@supports (anchor-name: --a) { .dx-select-list[popover] { min-width: anchor-size(width); } }` rule had already been added to restore trigger-relative sizing, but was dead in the served stylesheet: `#[css_module]`'s class-hashing does not scope selectors written only inside an `@supports` body (`dev-docs/issues/css-module-supports-scoping.md` — see row 32's notes for this being a second instance of the same gap), so the rule targeted the unhashed `.dx-select-list` while the live element carries `.dx-select-list-e8d9eb03`. And `use_anchor_position_fallback`'s JS fallback (the path a non-anchor engine like Firefox/WebKit takes) only ever corrected *position*, never *width* — so no engine had a working fix. Fixed at the engine level (`primitives/src/top_layer.rs`): every anchored content's trigger width is now published as the custom property `--dx-anchor-width`, unconditionally, from `use_anchor_position_fallback`'s own measurement (`reposition()`, every engine, kept fresh by its existing scroll/resize/visualViewport tracking) and, redundantly, from the shared `@supports` stylesheet's marker-class rules (`--dx-anchor-width: anchor-size(width);`, verified by execution to substitute correctly through a custom property on this sandbox's Chromium). `select/style.css` now reads `min-width: var(--dx-anchor-width, 100%)` and its dead `@supports` block/comment were rewritten to explain the contract. Regression oracle: two new `select.spec.ts` cases (component page and home gallery) asserting the listbox's width tracks its trigger and its left edge stays within 8px of the trigger's — RED before the fix (1237.76px / 1241.14px against ~141–156px triggers on a 1280px viewport, matching the report), GREEN after (listbox width equals trigger width exactly: 156.36px / 141.41px). `oracle/tier2-html/top-layer.spec.ts`'s own Rule 13 ("fit-content width") runs on that file's unstyled fixture and cannot see a themed component's CSS, so it never covered this — the new `select.spec.ts` cases are the real oracle for it; see that rule's own header doc, updated to say so | Landed | S–M | Every other themed overlay `style.css` (`dropdown_menu`, `menubar`, `navbar`, `context_menu`, `popover`, `tooltip`, `hover_card`, `date_picker`, `color_picker`) was checked for the same class of bug (a percentage `width`/`min-width` on a top-layer content element) and found clean — each uses a fixed pixel `min-width` (200px/220px) or a `max-width` clamp against the viewport that was already viewport-relative by design (`position: fixed`'s containing block is always the viewport, promoted or not). `combobox`'s list is a fixed `width: 200px` matching its input wrapper's own fixed `width: 200px` — no viewport-relative bug there, and no "wide input" case exists today to make matching via `--dx-anchor-width` worth doing; left as-is |
