A different bug mechanism from the `css_module`/`@supports` hashing class (rows 31a/32/47) — a dead-property-order cascade bug, not a selector-scoping one; noted separately so the two classes aren't conflated |
| 49 | **LANDED 2026-09-04: `pinNearTop` scroll-helper bug in `top-layer.spec.ts`, found on first execution.** Rule 11(c)'s Popover case — the section's only executable assertion — had never once actually opened its popover; it burned the full 5-minute per-test timeout every run. `pinNearTop` assumes its trigger is in normal document flow and scrolls the page by `rect.top`, but `#edge-bottom-popover-trigger`'s row is `position: fixed; bottom: 4px`, so it computed a large arbitrary scroll (+659px measured) that dragged the fixture's unrelated `#stack-sibling` (`position: absolute; z-index: 9999`) into the trigger's rectangle, where it intercepted the click. Fixed in the helper itself — it now walks ancestors for `position: fixed` and returns early — rather than in the one case, so a future fixed-position trigger cannot hit the same bug again. The case now passes in ~15s. **Not a regression from this session** — this code dates to 2026-09-02 and was authored without a browser | Landed 2026-09-04 | S | First execution of this exact case since it was written (2026-09-02) — this session is the first time this repo has had a working Playwright container, so this was the first chance any session had to notice the helper never worked. Harness-only fix, no product code touched |

| 50 | **LANDED 2026-09-04: top-layer overlays inherited the UA's ink colour instead of the app's, sitewide.** User-reported against the deployed preview: Menubar submenu text and the Popover menu render the wrong colour. Root cause: `[popover]` and `<dialog>` each carry a UA stylesheet rule setting `color: CanvasText` DIRECTLY on the element, and a directly-cascaded value beats an inherited one regardless of specificity or source order — so every overlay wrapper that never declares its own `color` renders UA black instead of `--secondary-color-4`. Navbar only appeared correct because `.dx-navbar-item` declares its own colour and masks the identical bug; in dark mode the defect degrades to black-on-dark-grey. **Explicitly NOT a regression from rows 31b/32** — proven by building and serving the pre-session commit `963c570` side by side for byte-identical measurements, and by `git diff 963c570..HEAD` touching zero `color:` lines in those stylesheets; the hypothesis that row 32's per-component `document::Link` changed cascade order was falsified directly, since neither `main.css` nor `dx-components-theme.css` declares a `color` rule for these elements at all. Fixed as a class, not per instance, because row 44's `ensure_toast_base_styles` had already fixed exactly this for the raw toast and never generalised it: `ensure_top_layer_ink_styles` (`primitives/src/top_layer.rs`) injects zero-specificity `:where([popover]), :where(dialog) { color: inherit }` from the only three hooks by which anything enters the top layer — `use_popover_sync`, `use_popover_shown_while_mounted` and `use_dialog_open_driver` (`lib.rs`), covering the popover-promoted and native-`<dialog>` arms. Execution then showed the wrapper rule alone was insufficient: `<button>`/`<input>`/`<select>`/`<textarea>` carry their own direct UA colour, so DatePicker, Select and Combobox stayed black (their items are real buttons, e.g. `.dx-calendar-grid-cell`); the rule was extended to those controls **inside** a top-layer surface, scoped there rather than app-wide. Two instances the shared rule cannot fix alone were also fixed: `.dx-menubar-item` never declared a colour, and the Popover demo's `<h3>` was rendered with no class so `.dx-popover-content-title` never applied. Oracle: new `playwright/oracle/tier2-html/top-layer-ink.spec.ts`, 15 surfaces x light/dark, **red-first 20 failed/10 passed before, 30/30 after**, asserting against the token read from the page rather than a hardcoded rgb so it survives a theme retune, and deliberately retaining the cases where a component sets its own colour (tooltip; select/combobox's `--secondary-color-1`) as proof the shared rule does not override them. 199 further specs across the overlay family pass. **Known residue — CLOSED, same date.** The ink baseline was injected from its own `use_effect` while the overlay's `showPopover()`/`showModal()` ran from a different effect's `document::eval`, leaving a first-paint window (observed as the 2-in-N flake described above). Fixed with the construction this repo had already proven for the identical problem: `anchor_positioning_inject_js`'s own doc records that cross-`document::eval` ordering between two effects is unreliable even when both are declared, in order, in the same function, and that the fix is to prepend the injecting script directly into the dependent script so both run as one synchronous JS program with no ordering assumption left to make. `ensure_top_layer_ink_styles`'s injection is now prepended the identical way at all three top-layer entry hooks — `use_popover_sync`, `use_popover_shown_while_mounted` (`top_layer.rs`), and `use_dialog_open_driver` (`lib.rs`) — the same three exhaustive call sites the original fix touched. `toast.rs` was checked and deliberately left unchanged: it already shares this construction, reads nothing back that the window could race, and its own ink is covered via `use_popover_shown_while_mounted`. Proven by repetition rather than one run, since the defect was itself a flake: **150/150 across five repeats** of the full `top-layer-ink.spec.ts` oracle | Landed 2026-09-04 | S–M | Cross-reference the `css_module`/`@supports`-scoping bug class (rows 31a/32/47): this is a *different* bug mechanism (UA direct-cascade beating inheritance), not another instance of that one — noted so the two aren't conflated. See row 51 for a second, unrelated defect surfaced by this same investigation |
| 51 | **`Calendar`'s stylesheet does not load on the `date_picker` route — LANDED 2026-09-04.** Found incidentally during row 50's ink investigation: `document.styleSheets` on `/component/?name=date_picker&` contained no `calendar`-named entry at all, so `DatePicker`'s calendar day-grid cells (`.dx-calendar-grid-cell`, real `<button>`s) had no author `color` — or any other `Calendar` styling — to fall back to on that route, only their own UA default. Pre-existing and unrelated to row 50's fix (which no longer causes black text there regardless, since the extended rule reaches top-layer-descendant form controls independent of whether their own stylesheet loaded). **Root cause:** `date_picker` composes `CalendarRoot`/`CalendarMonthView` — the calendar primitive — rather than the themed `Calendar` wrapper, so after row 32 moved CSS delivery into each themed wrapper's own `document::Link`, `date_picker`'s composition path never rendered `Calendar`'s wrapper and never rendered its link either. **The audit matters more than the fix.** Checked as a class, not patched as a one-off bug: two shapes were considered — a raw `dioxus_primitives::` component composed outside its owning wrapper (already guarded by `check-preview-composition.sh`, zero violations across the codebase) and a themed component composing another's `pub(crate)` internals directly, which that script exempts wholesale rather than checking. Six components do the latter; of them, `date_picker`/`calendar` was the **only** one missing CSS. **Fixed** by moving the `document::Link` onto `CalendarRoot`/`RangeCalendarRoot` themselves — the `pub(crate)` roots every consumer (including `Calendar`/`RangeCalendar` themselves) funnels through — mirroring `popover`'s existing pattern of a `Link` on every one of its own composable entry points, not just its "main" one; `document::Link`'s `(href, rel)` dedup makes the now-redundant `Link` that used to sit only on `Calendar`/`RangeCalendar` harmless to leave, so it was simply removed. **The guard is generic, not a calendar special case:** `playwright/dx-class-migration.spec.ts` now asserts, per route, that every `dx-<component>` prefix actually rendered in that route's own demo content has its owning component's stylesheet reachable from the page — no per-component "what composes what" list needed, and it would have caught this pair red before the fix | Landed 2026-09-04 | S | Found 2026-09-04 building row 50's oracle; landed the same date rather than folded into row 50, since it is a separate, unrelated defect |
| 52 | **Menu-family role consolidation, stage 1 — LANDED 2026-09-04.** `menu_semantics` exists, in its own module doc's words, so that fixing `dropdown_menu`'s roles in place (row 24) would not "leave the *next* menu-pattern component free to hand-write a fourth, possibly-different set" — and Navbar was exactly that fourth set: `primitives/src/navbar.rs` hand-wrote `role="menu"` (×3) and `role="menuitem"` (×2) at five sites while `dropdown_menu.rs`, `context_menu.rs`, and `menubar.rs` all already read theirs from the shared module. All five now read from `menu_semantics::MENU_ROLE`/`MENU_ITEM_ROLE`, each with a comment naming the `menubar.rs` counterpart it mirrors; no new constant was needed, and Navbar never used `aria-haspopup` at all. Two literals stay inline on purpose, not by oversight: `role="navigation"` is a landmark, not part of this pattern class, and `role="menubar"` is the Menu-and-Menubar pattern's own container role, which the module's own Scope section explicitly disclaims — `menubar.rs` keeps its own inline `role="menubar"` for the identical reason. The module doc now lists Navbar among its consumers and names it in that carve-out, since listing only three consumers is how this gap went unnoticed in the first place. `playwright/oracle/tier1-apg/menu-roles.spec.ts` did **not** cover Navbar at all — the omission that let the drift happen unnoticed — and now does, opening by hover (Navbar's real gesture, not Menubar's click), with the same two-elements-both-`role="menu"` disambiguation the existing Menubar block already needed. Emitted markup is unchanged; only its source moved — menu-roles, navbar, and keyboard-matrix all stay green | Landed 2026-09-04 | S | Found while assessing whether Navbar and Menubar should share a submenu primitive (row 53) — the drift check that assessment required surfaced this gap directly. See row 53 for the larger stage-2 question this stage was scoped out of |
| 53 | **Menu-family submenu consolidation, stage 2 — LANDED 2026-09-14, verified by execution.** Following stage 1 (row 52), the earlier assessment of whether `DropdownMenu`/`ContextMenu`/`Menubar` should share more plumbing concluded **extract three narrow bands, not a whole shared component** — that scope is what actually got built, in a new module `primitives/src/menu_root.rs` (registered via one `mod menu_root;` line in `lib.rs`), the same "identical plumbing here, host-private everything else" shape `primitives/src/menu_sub.rs` (row 54) already established as this crate's precedent. **The construction, band by band:** (1) `use_open_focus_sync` — the "open-index container": the effect that resyncs a menu's own `open` boolean from whether its roving-focus collection currently holds DOM focus (gated on no descendant submenu being open) was **byte-identical, word for word**, between `DropdownMenu`'s and `ContextMenu`'s root components before this change — now one shared function, called from both. **Deliberately not extended to `Menubar`**: that host's analogous effect resyncs a *which-index-is-open* signal (`Signal<Option<usize>>`), not a plain boolean, and has no `submenu_open_count` concept at all (`Menubar` doesn't support `DropdownMenuSub`-style nested submenus) — forcing the two shapes into one generic function would either lose the index `Menubar` needs or invent a translation layer with no existing duplication to justify it, so `Menubar`'s own version stays exactly where it was. (2) `use_menu_content_lifecycle` — the "content lifecycle/positioning pipeline": id resolution (`use_unique_id` + `use_id_or`, honoring a caller-overridden id) plus `use_animated_open`'s open/close-hold render gate, identical across all three hosts' `*Content` components — now one shared call in each, returning `(id, render)`. The *positioning* half of the name stays host-private, for the identical reason `menu_sub.rs`'s own doc gives for its own submenu content: `top_layer.rs` hard-codes a fixed list of `dx-anchor-*` marker classes, so the actual `use_anchor_position_fallback`/`use_point_anchor_clamp` call still has to live in each file's own `#[cfg(feature = "web")]` leaf render function — this band only factors the lifecycle gate that runs *before* that call. (3) `content_labelledby_attributes` — the "trigger id-plumbing": the APG-required accessible-name-from-trigger-id block (row 25) was duplicated across all six `*ContentRendered` web/native arm pairs (three hosts × two arms) — now one shared function called from all six. **What stayed host-private, exactly as the original assessment said it should:** the keyboard state machine (`MenubarMenu` still concentrates its whole contract in one handler; `Navbar` still splits it three ways) and the item components (`NavbarItem`'s routing-aware `Link` vs. `MenubarItem`'s plain `div`) — neither was touched, and `Navbar` itself was **not** made a consumer of `menu_root` at all, matching the original scoping to `DropdownMenu`/`ContextMenu`/`Menubar` only. `menu_sub.rs` was read for precedent and left untouched, per this row's own file-ownership rule — its nested-submenu state machine shares no code with this extraction (different level of nesting, already its own module). **The Escape-refocus divergence, investigated and root-caused, not left open:** `Menubar` explicitly refocuses its trigger on Escape; `Navbar`'s Escape handler did not. Root-caused as a **genuine oversight, not a deliberate hover-model exemption** — `Navbar` fully supports keyboard-driven opening (`NavbarNav`'s ArrowDown/ArrowUp move focus into the open content exactly the way `MenubarMenu`'s do), so a keyboard user who arrows into an open nav and presses Escape faces exactly the same "where does focus go" question Menubar already answers, and no test asserted either behavior before this fix. Fixed in `Navbar`'s own `onkeydown` (host-agnostic, not gated behind `#[cfg(feature = "web")]`, so it applies to both arms): read the open index before clearing it, then re-focus that trigger through the collection, mirroring `MenubarMenu`'s own "clear then set to force a real transition" construction exactly. **A genuine surprise found during verification, worth recording:** on this sandbox's Chromium, the new red-first test for this fix (see below) passed even with the fix *reverted* — root-caused, not just observed: `NavbarContent` (like `MenubarContent`) renders via `popover="auto"`, and the WHATWG Popover API's own "focus fixup" behavior already restores focus to the element that was focused before the popover opened whenever it closes (native light dismiss *or* a scripted `hidePopover()` call), which for `Navbar` happens to be the trigger itself in every case this bug could occur. So on the **web arm**, this specific defect was already invisible to Chromium's native popover behavior, independent of this fix. The fix is still real and still needed: it is not gated to the web arm (unlike `use_anchor_position_fallback`-style code), so it is the only thing providing this contract on the **native (Blitz) arm**, which has no Popover API and thus no such platform behavior — the same reason `MenubarMenu` doesn't rely on the platform either, and does the refocus explicitly itself. This could not be verified on Blitz in this sandbox (no such test harness exists here); the finding is disclosed rather than presented as a red→green proof on the engine actually exercised. **Red-first verification (per `dev-docs/plan.md`'s definition of done):** (a) For the three shared bands themselves: rather than inventing redundant coverage, existing specs already exercise all three hosts' open/close and positioning behavior end to end — `playwright/dropdown-menu.spec.ts`, `playwright/context-menu.spec.ts` (including its "touch tap outside closes the open menu" case, which exercises band 1's exact codepath), `playwright/menubar.spec.ts`, and the full `keyboard-matrix.spec.ts` open/close rows for all three hosts — all of which stayed green through the refactor, proving the extraction preserved behavior. The one genuinely new piece of coverage this row adds for bands 2/3 is the "trigger id-plumbing" band's `aria-labelledby` value, previously asserted only for `DropdownMenu` (`menu-roles.spec.ts`) — **new assertions added for `ContextMenu` and `Menubar`** (mirroring `DropdownMenu`'s existing one), which would catch a future drift between the three hosts' now-shared `content_labelledby_attributes` call. (b) For the Escape-refocus fix: a new `keyboard-matrix.spec.ts` case, `NavbarNav — Escape`, graded against `MenubarMenu`'s identical contract — confirmed it passes with the fix in place; per the finding above, it does not distinguish fixed-from-reverted on this sandbox's Chromium (both pass, for the platform-behavior reason described), so it is disclosed as a regression guard and a stated contract, not a red→green proof on this engine. **Verified by actual execution (Chromium, this sandbox), 2026-09-14:** `cargo check --workspace` and `cargo check --workspace --features web -p dioxus-primitives` both clean (only the two pre-existing, unrelated `preview` warnings present on `main`); `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --tests --examples -- -D warnings` fails exactly as this task's own brief predicted — 5 pre-existing `doc_lazy_continuation` errors in `menu_sub.rs` (a file this row did not touch), and **zero** new warnings or errors anywhere else, confirmed by grepping the full clippy output for every touched file. `npx playwright test --project=chromium oracle/tier1-apg/keyboard-matrix.spec.ts oracle/tier1-apg/menu-roles.spec.ts dropdown-menu.spec.ts context-menu.spec.ts menubar.spec.ts navbar.spec.ts` (97 tests, Chromium only, `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers` already matching this repo's pinned Playwright 1.60.0/chromium-1223): **96 passed, 1 skipped (a pre-existing, unrelated `test.skip` in the Accordion section), 0 failed.** Firefox/WebKit not attempted (disallowed in this sandbox, per row 4) | Landed 2026-09-14 | M (done) | Files touched: `primitives/src/menu_root.rs` (new), `primitives/src/lib.rs` (+1 line), `primitives/src/dropdown_menu.rs`, `primitives/src/context_menu.rs`, `primitives/src/menubar.rs` (all three: swap the duplicated blocks for the shared calls, no behavior change), `primitives/src/navbar.rs` (the Escape-refocus fix only), `playwright/oracle/tier1-apg/keyboard-matrix.spec.ts` (+Navbar Escape case), `playwright/oracle/tier1-apg/menu-roles.spec.ts` (+ContextMenu/Menubar labelledby assertions). Builds on the top-layer/popover engine (Phase 4.4) and `menu_semantics` (row 52), same as row 54 |
| 54 | **Nested submenus (`DropdownMenu.Sub`/`ContextMenu.Sub`) — LANDED 2026-09-04, verified by execution.** Closes the "Nested submenu" row in [`component-backlog.md`](./component-backlog.md) (rated **L**, called "the highest-value primitive-needing gap with no clean source") — see that file's own row for the catalog-status update. Built on the landed top-layer/popover engine (Phase 4.4, row 2) and reading its roles from `menu_semantics` (row 24/52), so it inherits both foundations rather than re-deriving them. New shared module `primitives/src/menu_sub.rs` factors the plumbing genuinely identical between `DropdownMenu`'s and `ContextMenu`'s submenus — open/close state, the submenu's own roving-focus item collection, the trigger/content id pair, and the hover-intent open/close timer (`DelayedAction`, generalised from `context_menu.rs`'s pre-existing long-press timer, this crate's only prior art for a timed pointer-driven open/close) — while deliberately leaving each host's own private root context lookup and content-positioning call in `dropdown_menu.rs`/`context_menu.rs` themselves, since Rust's module privacy has no way to let a shared module see either host's private context type without widening its API surface for no reader benefit. Scoped to one level of nesting only, matching the module's own doc and this row's original **L** rating: a `Sub`'s own content may hold plain items but not a further nested `Sub`, since a sub-trigger registers in the *root* menu's collection by reading that root's context type directly, which only resolves correctly at exactly one level of depth. New APG oracle `playwright/oracle/tier1-apg/menu-submenu.spec.ts` (430 lines, cited to the same pinned APG "Menu and Menubar" pattern commit `keyboard-matrix.spec.ts` already uses) — passing. A `use_effect_cleanup` fix was needed in both `dropdown_menu.rs` and `context_menu.rs` to make submenus actually open at all: a submenu's own open-count bookkeeping effect had no cleanup path for the case of the enclosing menu (or an ancestor submenu) unmounting the submenu while it was still open, which runs no further effects, only cleanups — without it the shared open-count state could desync and submenus would fail to open. Keyboard-matrix stayed **64/64** with no regressions | Landed 2026-09-04 | L | References checked, per `lifting-from-forks.md` hygiene, and NOT taken: `molikto/dioxus-components@ed681439` (stale, shape-only, pre-dates the collection/focus refactors this repo now has) |
| 55 | **`Sidebar` right-side mirroring silently no-op'd when `collapsible: SidebarCollapsible::None` — LANDED 2026-09-15, user-reported.** `Sidebar()`'s `None` branch renders a parallel `.dx-sidebar-static` element (distinct from the main desktop branch's `.dx-sidebar-container`), and unlike that branch it never set `data-side`. The only CSS rule mirroring the sidebar to the right (`style.css`: `.dx-sidebar-wrapper:has([data-side="right"]) { flex-direction: row-reverse; }`, previously scoped to `.dx-sidebar-desktop[data-side="right"]`) requires that attribute, so with "Collapse: None" selected in the demo's own settings panel, toggling "Side: Right" had zero visual effect — the sidebar stayed pinned left. The default Offcanvas/Icon collapse modes already mirrored correctly; the bug was scoped specifically to `None`, which is why it only appeared "when choosing other configs." A second, same-root-cause defect found alongside it: `.dx-sidebar-static` also never set any `position`, so its child `SidebarRail` (`position: absolute`) had no positioned ancestor and rendered pinned to a page corner instead of the sidebar's own edge, for both sides. **Fixed by construction, not per-instance:** (1) `component.rs` — added `data-side` to the `None` branch's attributes; (2) `style.css` — broadened the mirroring selector to key on `[data-slot="sidebar"][data-side="right"]` (a slot all three Sidebar render paths already share) instead of the desktop-only class, so one selector covers every current and future branch; added `position: relative` to `.dx-sidebar-static`, giving `SidebarRail` the same containing block the main branch already gets for free from `.dx-sidebar-container`'s `position: fixed`. **Verified by execution:** `getBoundingClientRect()`/`getComputedStyle()` before/after at 1280x800 — sidebar rect identical for Left/Right before the fix (bug), correctly mirrored after (`x=0–256` vs `x=1009–1265`); `SidebarRail` moved from a page-corner rect (`x=1257–1273` / `x=-8–8`) to hugging the sidebar edge (`x=248–264` / `x=1001–1017`) on both sides. New geometry-based regression test `playwright/sidebar.spec.ts`, "desktop: side switch mirrors sidebar with collapsible=none" (mirrors `select.spec.ts`'s convention — asserts rects actually swap, not just that `data-side` changes, since the pre-existing "side switch updates data-side" test only checked the attribute and is exactly why it missed this). Full `sidebar.spec.ts` suite: **7/7 passed** (6 pre-existing + 1 new), no regressions. The test's own width assertion was initially written against `document.documentElement.clientWidth` and failed by exactly 15px — root-caused to row 1's own `scrollbar-gutter: stable` baseline (not a new bug), fixed by comparing against the wrapper element's own `boundingClientRect` instead | Landed 2026-09-15 | S | Found via user report ("alignment bugs" when "choosing other configs, like the right side"); delegated to a Sonnet subagent per this repo's debugging convention, independently re-verified (including the scrollbar-gutter false start) before landing |

| 56 | **Docs site now dogfoods the real `Sidebar` component as its own navigation — LANDED 2026-09-15, user-requested.** The docs site's own nav was a hand-rolled `DocsSidebar` in `main.rs` (raw `<aside>`/`<nav>`/`<button>` elements, its own `dx-docs-sidebar-*` classes, its own toggle/backdrop) — a parallel implementation duplicating what the shipped `Sidebar`/`SidebarProvider`/`SidebarInset` already do, never dogfooded even on the site showcasing them. Replaced with a new `DocsLayout` composing `SidebarProvider`/`Sidebar`/`SidebarContent`/`SidebarInset`, mirroring the composition `sidebar/variants/main/mod.rs`'s own demo and the email-client dashboard already use (`SidebarMenuButton`'s `as` prop swaps in the real router `Link` for SPA navigation; `SidebarInset` supplies the page's one `<main>` landmark). Applied to all three site pages — `/docs`, each component page, and (newly) the **homepage** `/` itself, whose hero + component gallery previously had no sidebar nav at all and are now the `SidebarInset` content of a real sidebar layout. `DocsNavActive` replaces the old `Option<&'static str>` active-param, which conflated "on Docs" with "on Home" (both were `None`) — each page now gets its own distinct active state, verified via direct DOM query (not just visual) to confirm zero false-positive active links on the homepage. **A real cross-cutting CSS conflict found and fixed along the way:** `sidebar/style.css`'s desktop rendering assumes its wrapper IS the whole page (`position: fixed` column, `100svh` tall) — true for the two other call sites (`ComponentBlockDemo`, `EmailClientDashboard`), both rendered outside the site's own Navbar/Footer chrome; `DocsLayout` shares the page with those, so a fixed column would float over the footer past the fold. Re-scoped under `.dx-sidebar-wrapper.dx-docs-shell` to a `position: sticky` in-flow column with a width-collapse instead of the fixed-position slide-off, keeping the same collapse transition without fighting page chrome the component doesn't own. **Verified by execution:** `cargo check`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `check-preview-composition.sh`/`check-dx-class-prefix.sh`/`check-css-literals.sh` all green; `preview.spec.ts` (axe on home + a component page), `sidebar.spec.ts`, `navbar.spec.ts` — 13/13 passed, independently re-run; active-nav-link state and footer/sidebar non-overlap after scrolling confirmed via direct DOM query and screenshot, not visual inspection alone | Landed 2026-09-15 | M | User-requested after two rejected placement options (a homepage-grid card, a small thumbnail) clarified the actual ask was site-wide nav dogfooding, not a demo-card change; delegated to a Sonnet subagent, independently re-verified before landing. `oracle/hydration-parity.spec.ts` not run — needs a separate SSG build lane orthogonal to this composition-only change |
| 57 | **`ContextMenu`/`DropdownMenu` submenus closed the instant the pointer left the trigger toward the submenu's own content — LANDED 2026-09-15, user-reported.** The pre-existing hover oracle (`menu-submenu.spec.ts`) stayed green because its one hover test only ever moved the pointer from the trigger to a *sibling root item*, never into the submenu's own content — exactly the path the report described ("on sub-menu mouse hover, the sub-menu disappears / submenus not selectable"). **Root cause 1 (shared plumbing, both hosts):** `SUBMENU_CLOSE_GRACE_DELAY`'s `DelayedAction` timer was private local state inside `*SubTrigger` only (`menu_sub.rs`); the trigger's own `onmouseleave` scheduled a close on it, but **`*SubContentRendered` had no `onmouseenter`/`onmouseleave` handlers at all**, so nothing could ever cancel that timer once the pointer entered the submenu's own content — ~200ms later the stale close fired regardless of where the pointer actually was. Fixed by construction: hoisted `hover_open`/`hover_close: DelayedAction` onto the shared `SubMenuState` (read via context by both trigger and content instead of the trigger's own private locals), then wired `onmouseenter`/`onmouseleave` on all four `*SubContentRendered` functions (`ContextMenuSubContentRendered`/`DropdownMenuSubContentRendered`, web + native arms) to cancel/reschedule the same shared timer the trigger already does — this alone fully fixed `DropdownMenu.Sub`. **Root cause 2 (`ContextMenu`-specific, on top of root cause 1):** `ContextMenu.Sub` still failed after the above — `.dx-context-menu-content` (shared by `ContextMenuContent` and `ContextMenuSubContent`) sets `pointer-events: none` **unconditionally**, unlike `.dx-dropdown-menu-content`'s identical-looking reset, correctly scoped to `[data-state="closed"]` only. `ContextMenuContentRendered` (the root) already works around this with an inline `pointer_events: open().then_some("auto")` override, but `ContextMenuSubContentRendered` never had the equivalent — so an open submenu was non-interactive the entire time, explaining why root cause 1's new hover handlers couldn't even fire. Fixed by mirroring the same inline override onto both of `ContextMenuSubContentRendered`'s arms. `DropdownMenu` never needed this fix (confirmed via grep — no `pointer_events` usage in `dropdown_menu.rs` at all, since its CSS already scopes the reset correctly). **Verified by execution:** two new regression tests in `menu-submenu.spec.ts` (real `page.mouse.move(..., {steps})` sequences, not `.hover()`, from trigger onto the submenu's own item, past the grace delay) — full oracle **14/14 passed** (5 pre-existing unchanged + 2 new, both hosts); `context-menu.spec.ts` + `dropdown-menu.spec.ts` + `keyboard-matrix.spec.ts` combined, independently re-run: **82 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failed**; `cargo check` clean for both default and `--features web` on `primitives`, `cargo fmt --check` clean, all three composition/class-prefix/css-literal lint scripts green | Landed 2026-09-15 | M | Found via user report; delegated to a Sonnet subagent, independently re-verified (including a full oracle + broad regression re-run, not just the two new tests) before landing |

| 58 | **`Sidebar` block-demo iframe rendered empty on live production; homepage Sidebar card now exposes live config for the real site nav — LANDED 2026-09-15, user-reported + user-requested.** Visiting `/component/?name=sidebar&` on the deployed site showed the demo's own Side/Collapse settings panel rendering and responding to clicks, but the actual `Sidebar` preview never appeared — no `[data-slot="sidebar"]` node anywhere, just empty space, on every collapse/side combination. **Root cause:** `Sidebar`'s own responsive check (`component.rs`, `MOBILE_BREAKPOINT = 768`, `use_is_mobile()`) reads `window.innerWidth` of whatever document it's running in — for an `<iframe>`, that's the iframe's own content window, not the outer page's viewport. The block demo's iframe (`BlockComponentVariantHighlight`) was `width: 100%` inside the page's content column; once row 56 (above) narrowed that column with the new site-nav sidebar, the iframe's measured `innerWidth` dropped to ~684px — under the 768px breakpoint — flipping `Sidebar` into its mobile branch, which renders a closed `Sheet` (nothing painted) rather than the desktop branch. Proven directly on the live, unmodified production site: widening the existing iframe in place from 684px→820px via the exact `resize`-event path `use_is_mobile()` listens for flipped the rendered sidebar count 0→1. **Fixed** by giving the shared iframe a `min-width: 820px` (comfortably clear of the breakpoint) plus `overflow-x: auto` on its wrapper (`html,body{overflow-x:clip}` in `main.css` would otherwise silently clip rather than scroll) — one shared construction point (`BlockComponentVariantHighlight`), so it covers every current and future `ComponentType::Block` component's iframe, not just Sidebar's. **Second part (user-requested):** the homepage's Sidebar gallery card previously just linked out ("Open full preview") instead of showing anything live. `DocsLayout` now takes optional `side`/`collapsible` signals (every other call site still gets its own fixed local defaults, seeded identically to before, so no behavior change there); `Home()` owns and provides them via a `HomeSidebarControls` context; `ComponentGalleryPreview`'s `Block` branch, only for `component.name == "sidebar"` (`try_consume_context`, degrading gracefully to the old link anywhere else), swaps the link for `SidebarGalleryCardControls` — the same Side/Collapse button shape as the demo's own `DemoSettingControls`, but writing straight to those signals, so clicking them visibly reconfigures the real, on-page site sidebar live. Confirmed no state leakage across navigation (Dioxus remounts `DocsLayout` fresh per route). **A second, unrelated-but-newly-exposed layout bug found while verifying this:** at a plain 1280px viewport the new card's Side/Collapse controls rendered genuinely off-screen and unclickable (not a scroll issue — `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` was *less* than `clientWidth`, so the content was clipped, not scrollable-to). Root-caused to a classic Flexbox pitfall, not this card's own layout: `.dx-sidebar-inset` is `flex: 1 1 0%` with no `min-width` override, so its *automatic* minimum width defaults to its content's min-content size rather than 0 (CSS Flexbox spec) — any wide-enough content anywhere inside the inset (here, the pre-existing, unrelated `WidgetMasonry` homepage widget, confirmed by live DOM bisection removing subtrees one at a time) makes the whole inset refuse to shrink and silently overflow (`overflow-x: clip` on `html`/`body` hides it rather than scrolling), which in turn widens every sibling section sharing that inset's `width: 100%` — the gallery card whose own content happened to reach flush to its card's now-off-screen edge was just the first thing to make it visible and unclickable. **Fixed by construction, at the one flex item that needs to shrink** (`min-width: 0` on `.dx-sidebar-inset`) rather than re-constraining whichever content exposes it — covers `WidgetMasonry` today and any future wide content by the same mechanism, per this repo's standing "class not instance" rule. **A pre-existing, unrelated `dx build --ssg` tooling race found and guarded against during verification:** an independently-reproduced, systematic 9-build A/B test (alternating branch/baseline source, full `rm -rf target/dx/preview` each time) showed **100% correlation between build speed and correctness, 0% correlation with source content** — every build that had to actually recompile the `preview` crate (cold cache) came out correct on both branch and pristine baseline; every build that hit a warm cache (~10s, skip straight to bundling/SSG) came out with every non-home SSG route missing its `<script type="module">` hydration bootstrap tag entirely on both branch and baseline. This is a `dx`-cli/dioxus-fullstack race (the SSG render pass fires all routes' requests at the freshly-started fullstack server concurrently, seemingly before some server-side dependency of the client-bundling phase is ready) — not caused by, or fixable from, this app's own code. Guarded against rather than left latent: `scripts/deploy-preview.sh` now greps every built route's `index.html` for the bootstrap script tag right after the build and exits 1 with the affected route list before touching `/docs` if any are missing — verified both halves for real (a genuine cold `cargo clean` + full rebuild passes silently; re-running immediately after, cache now warm, naturally hit the same race and the script correctly caught it and exited 1 without touching `docs/`). **Verified by execution, independently re-checked twice more by the orchestrating session after two rounds of subagent-reported-but-unverified issues** (the SSG race initially mis-attributed to the diff; the card-overflow bug initially mis-attributed to the card's own layout) — both corrected via direct measurement before landing: `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `check-preview-composition.sh`/`check-dx-class-prefix.sh`/`check-css-literals.sh` clean; final clean `deploy-preview.sh` run passed its own new sanity check (6/6 routes); iframe `innerWidth: 820-822`, sidebar count 1, for all three Sidebar demo variants; homepage card's `Right`/`None` buttons clickable without `force`, `data-side` flips `left`→`right` live, `/docs` still defaults `left` after navigating away (no leakage); `document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= clientWidth` at 1280px | Landed 2026-09-15 | M | Both reports from direct user testing of the live deployed site; delegated to a Sonnet subagent across three rounds (bug1+feature2 → SSG-race investigation → card-overflow fix), each round independently re-verified against a real browser and a real `deploy-preview.sh` run before accepting, not just the subagent's own claims |

| 59 | **Block-demo iframe's row-58 `min-width: 820px` fix itself broke real mobile viewports; homepage nav now closed by default; homepage checkbox+label gap fixed to match convention — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-reported (×3) + user-requested (×1).** Row 58's flat, always-on `min-width: 820px` on `BlockComponentVariantHighlight`'s iframe fixed the desktop-squeeze case but fired unconditionally, so a genuinely narrow real device/window (390px) also got forced to an 822px-wide iframe — confirmed live on production (`getBoundingClientRect()`: iframe `width: 822` inside a 390px viewport, clipped rather than scrolled since `overflow-x: clip` on `html`/`body` hides the escape). **Fixed by construction:** moved the rule out of the iframe's own inline style/document into a new `.dx-block-demo-iframe` class in `preview/assets/main.css` — a stylesheet loaded by the OUTER top-level page, not the iframe's — gated behind `@media (min-width: 768px)` there. A media query written inside the iframe's own document can only ever see the iframe's own (possibly-squeezed) width, the exact ambiguity at the root of both this bug and row 58's original one; evaluating the query in the outer page's stylesheet instead reads the real visitor viewport, correctly telling apart "outer page's own nav squeezed an otherwise-wide desktop viewport" (>=768px outer: still force 820px, reachable via the wrapper's existing `overflow-x: auto`) from "this is a genuinely narrow device" (<768px outer: no forced min-width, iframe sizes to its real container, embedded `Sidebar`'s own `MOBILE_BREAKPOINT` check sees the true narrow width and renders its real mobile Sheet-trigger layout). One shared construction point, so it covers every current and future `ComponentType::Block` iframe, not just Sidebar's (currently the only one). **Second report, diagnosed as a false positive, not a real bug:** "homepage sidebar config does not work" — the homepage card's Side/Collapse buttons write straight to the same signals `DocsLayout` passes into the real `Sidebar`, confirmed still correct; on mobile the `Sidebar`'s Sheet branch simply isn't mounted until opened, so a card click has nothing visible to update and reasonably read as broken. No code bug found here; see the next item for what mobile actually needed. (One true incidental finding, not itself a bug: the mobile Sheet branch never reads the `collapsible` prop at all, so Offcanvas/Icon/None render identically on mobile — pre-existing, correct, unchanged.) **Third, user-requested: hide the homepage's own site nav by default.** `SidebarProvider`'s existing `default_open: bool` prop (previously unused by `DocsLayout`) is now threaded through a new `DocsLayout { default_open: Option<bool> }` prop; every call site but `Home()` passes `None` and keeps the previous open-by-default behavior, `Home()` alone passes `Some(false)` — the homepage's hero + gallery get full width on first load, the topbar `SidebarTrigger` still opens it identically to before. **Fourth, user-requested: checkbox+label gap.** The homepage's `BlockFilters` dashboard-mockup tag-checkbox row used an inline, inconsistent `gap: 0.55rem` (8.8px) instead of this codebase's established checkbox/radio-to-label spacing token, `var(--dx-space-3)` (12px, already used consistently by `Field`'s horizontal orientation and `RadioGroupItem`) — the other, unrelated `checkbox`/`field` component stylesheets were audited and found already correct, so this was the one actual instance. Fixed at that one row; every other unrelated `0.55rem` use in the same mockup section was deliberately left untouched. **Verified by execution, independently re-checked by the orchestrating session (not just the subagent's own claims), matching this backlog's standing rule after rows 58's two mis-attributions:** `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean (only the two pre-existing unrelated warnings); `check-preview-composition.sh`/`check-dx-class-prefix.sh`/`check-css-literals.sh` all clean; `preview.spec.ts` + `sidebar.spec.ts` (9/9 passed on Chromium; the suite's separate mobile-tap case is out of scope here — `playwright.config.ts` deliberately `grepInvert`s "mobile"-titled tests for all three desktop projects, a pre-existing config gap unrelated to this change, confirmed by inspecting the config itself). Independent, targeted re-measurement against a live `dx serve` instance: mobile (390×844) iframe width now 313px (was 822px), no page overflow; desktop (1280×900) iframe width still 822px (unchanged, >=820 preserved); homepage `data-state` now `collapsed` on load, flips to `expanded` on trigger click; `/docs` still defaults `expanded` (unaffected); homepage card's "Right" button still flips the real sidebar's `data-side` live; tag-checkbox row's computed `gap` now `12px` (was `8.8px`) | Landed 2026-09-17 | S–M | All four reports from direct user testing of the live deployed site + one user request folded into the same round; delegated to a single Sonnet subagent (disjoint files from the concurrent Input OTP and checkbox-audit lanes dispatched the same round, row 60), independently re-verified — cargo/lint/Playwright plus fresh browser measurements at both viewports and both homepage states — before landing |
| 60 | **Input OTP: almost no hit target for the input — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-reported.** Not a geometry/sizing bug, despite the report's own framing: the real, invisible (`opacity: 0`) `<input>` in `primitives/src/input_otp.rs` was already correctly sized to match its full visual row (confirmed unchanged: `position: absolute; inset: 0; width/height: 100%`). The actual cause was paint order — a purely decorative, `aria-hidden` overlay `div` (the slot/separator visuals, rendered *after* the real input in the same stacking context) covers almost the entire row and, with the browser default `pointer-events: auto`, silently intercepts clicks before they reach the input beneath it. Confirmed directly via `elementFromPoint`: clicking dead-center of a visible slot resolved to that slot's own decorative `<div>`, not `#otp-main`; only the few-pixel gaps *between* slots (touched by no decorative box) ever reached the real input — exactly matching the report's "almost no hit target." **Fixed** with a one-line `pointer_events: "none"` on the overlay wrapper `div`, inherited by every descendant (no child sets its own `pointer-events`), making the whole decorative layer transparent to hit-testing to match how it's already transparent visually. New regression coverage in `playwright/input_otp.spec.ts`: a full-row bounding-box geometry check, and a real hit-test clicking all 6 slots' centers *and* corners, confirming every point now focuses the real input. **Verified by execution, independently re-checked by the orchestrating session:** `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; all three composition/class-prefix/css-literal lint scripts clean; full `input_otp.spec.ts` suite re-run directly against a live `dx serve` instance — **7/7 passed on Chromium** (5 pre-existing unchanged + 2 new), independently confirming the subagent's own claimed pass (not just trusting its report) | Landed 2026-09-17 | S | User-reported; delegated to a Sonnet subagent alongside row 59's sidebar-cluster lane and a checkbox-padding audit (disjoint files: `primitives/src/input_otp.rs` + `playwright/input_otp.spec.ts` only), independently re-verified — cargo/lint/Playwright, no shortcuts taken on the subagent's claims — before landing |
| 61 | **Sidebar toggle icon lived in its own strip below the main site navbar instead of inside it — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-reported.** `Navbar` (`main.rs`) was rendered in `NavigationLayout` as a sibling of, and *before*, `Outlet::<Route> {}` — never a descendant of `DocsLayout`'s `SidebarProvider`, which is what actually owns the `SidebarCtx` a `SidebarTrigger` needs (`use_sidebar()` panics without an ancestor provider). `DocsLayout` compensated by rendering its own separate `header { class: "dx-docs-inset-topbar", SidebarTrigger {} }` strip below the navbar — the bug. **Fixed by construction:** made `Navbar` an actual descendant of the `SidebarProvider` on every route that has one, via one defensive check (`try_consume_context::<SidebarCtx>().is_some()`) instead of two separate code paths — this single check subsumes the desktop nav row, the `in_iframe` chromeless nav row, `Demos` (no sidebar at all), and `ComponentDemo`'s "component not found" early-return branch (also bypasses `DocsLayout`, previously lost its navbar entirely once `NavigationLayout` stopped rendering one unconditionally — now gets the same trigger-less `Navbar {}` as `Demos`). **The CSS trap this ran into:** `SidebarProvider`'s own render is just `div { class: "dx-sidebar-wrapper", {children} }`, and the base `.dx-sidebar-wrapper` rule (`sidebar/style.css`) is `display: flex` — a ROW sized for exactly two children, `Sidebar` + `SidebarInset`. Dropping `Navbar {}` in as a third child of that same row rendered it as a third flex item sitting beside them, not a full-width row stacked above. Fixed by grouping `Sidebar`+`SidebarInset` under one new wrapper, `.dx-docs-shell-body` (`main.css`), and making `.dx-sidebar-wrapper.dx-docs-shell` itself a `flex-direction: column` of exactly two children: `Navbar`, then that body row — the former outer-wrapper geometry (width/border/centering/the sidebar-row min-height) moved down onto `.dx-docs-shell-body`, since it's now the box that must match the navbar's own centered, content-width inner row. The base `@media (width >= 768px) { .dx-sidebar-wrapper:has([data-side="right"]) { flex-direction: row-reverse } }` rule (keyed on `:has()`, so it matches ANY `.dx-sidebar-wrapper` containing a right-sided sidebar anywhere inside it) would otherwise have reversed `Navbar` and the body row too, now that they share the same outer wrapper — cancelled at that level and reapplied one level down on `.dx-docs-shell-body`, where it correctly mirrors just the `Sidebar`/`SidebarInset` pair without moving `Navbar`. Neither of the *other* two `.dx-sidebar-wrapper` call sites (the block-demo iframe, the email-client dashboard) were touched — their selectors were left exactly as-is, only the docs-shell-specific ones were rescoped. **Verified by execution, independently re-checked by the orchestrating session, including tracking down a false alarm before landing:** `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `check-preview-composition.sh`/`check-dx-class-prefix.sh`/`check-css-literals.sh` all clean; `sidebar.spec.ts`/`navbar.spec.ts`/`preview.spec.ts`/`oracle/tier2-html/top-layer.spec.ts`/`oracle/tier3-radix/scroll-lock.spec.ts` re-run — the two `top-layer.spec.ts` "flip above bottom-edge trigger" failures reproduced identically against the unmodified baseline (`git stash`), confirming them pre-existing and unrelated (the real docs sidebar, dogfooded onto the `top_layer` oracle fixture page by row 56, intercepting a hover at that specific fixture layout — not caused by this change); a `navbar.spec.ts` "keyboard navigation" failure seen only when all five specs ran together turned out to be parallel-worker contention, not a real regression (passed cleanly alone and in smaller batches). **The false alarm, disclosed rather than silently discarded:** independently measuring the homepage's Sidebar gallery card after this fix initially showed its "Right"/"Left" controls rendered ~1250px off-screen and `.dx-sidebar-wrapper.dx-docs-shell`'s computed `flex-direction` reading `row` instead of `column` — looked exactly like a real regression (the same class of Flexbox/masonry-interaction bug row 58 already fixed once). Root-caused instead to this session's own `dx serve` dev-instance serving a stale cached CSS bundle that was missing the new rule entirely, confirmed by rebuilding via the production `dx build --ssg` recipe (`scripts/deploy-preview.sh`) and re-measuring against that: `flex-direction: column`, card at `x: 77.5` (matches baseline's `x: 76.5`), `Right` click correctly flips `data-side`, no console errors — this class of dev-server staleness is a known, already-documented `dx` tooling quirk (row 58's SSG cache-warm race is the closest precedent), not a new construction to add here | Landed 2026-09-17 | M | User-reported ("the sidebar icon in the overview should be in the main top bar"); delegated to a single Sonnet subagent (files: `main.rs`, `main.css` only), independently re-verified — cargo/lint, the full regression suite (with the two pre-existing top-layer flakes confirmed via baseline stash-diff, not just accepted on the subagent's word), and fresh production-build measurements after tracking down and ruling out a dev-server-caching false alarm — before landing |
| 62 | **`/docs` sidebar's "Start" group had a single "Overview" link and no icons anywhere in the site nav — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-requested.** The user asked for the Overview page's sidebar to expose its own real content (jump-to-section links, generated from its actual headings, not hand-typed) plus icons across the sidebar. **Generated, not duplicated:** a single `const DOCS_SECTIONS: &[&str]` (`main.rs`) and one `docs_section_slug` helper (lowercases, collapses non-alphanumeric runs to one hyphen) are the one source of truth for the `/docs` page's three `h2` headings' visible text, their `id`s, and the sidebar sub-items' labels/`href`s — all four render sites read from the same array/fn, so they cannot drift out of sync with each other the way two hand-typed copies of the same string eventually would. **Wired through `DocsLayout`** via a new `#[props(default)] docs_sections: Option<&'static [&'static str]>`, following the exact `side`/`collapsible`/`default_open` pattern already established there — only `Docs()` passes `Some(DOCS_SECTIONS)`; `Home()`/`ComponentHighlight()` pass nothing and render the "Start" group exactly as before (verified structurally unchanged on both). **Nesting** reuses the existing `SidebarMenuSub`/`SidebarMenuSubItem`/`SidebarMenuSubButton` primitives (`sidebar/component.rs`) — the same ones `sidebar/variants/*/mod.rs`'s own demos already use for nested sub-lists — rather than inventing new markup. **"Jump there" is a plain `a { href: "#slug" }`** via the `as` escape hatch, not the router's `Link`, since this is a same-page anchor scroll, not a route change; `html { scroll-behavior: smooth }` was added (`main.css`, alongside the pre-existing `scroll-padding-top` rule for the sticky navbar) so the jump animates instead of cutting. **Icons** (all `dioxus_icons::lucide`, already imported elsewhere in this file): `BookOpen` on "Start", `FileText` on "Overview", `Hash` (the conventional anchor/TOC marker) on each heading-derived sub-item, and one distinct icon per `ComponentCategory` group (Forms → `SquareCheck`, Navigation → `Compass`, Overlays → `Layers`, Feedback → `Bell`, Disclosure → `ChevronsUpDown`, Data display → `LayoutGrid`) — every icon carries `aria-hidden="true"` (mirroring the dashboard's own `LucideIcon` convention) since each sits beside a visible text label, so axe has no accessible-name gap to flag. Deliberately out of scope: per-individual-component icons (30+ distinct components, a much larger and more subjective task) — only category-level and the new section links got one. `SidebarGroupLabel` gained a `gap` in its CSS (`sidebar/style.css`) to support this icon+label pairing — it had none, being text-only until now; `SidebarMenuButton`/`SidebarMenuSubButton` already had the right icon+label spacing from existing collapsed-icon-mode CSS, so nothing else was needed. **Verified by execution, independently re-checked by the orchestrating session (concurrently with row 63's unrelated Input OTP fix, disjoint files, both re-verified separately before landing):** `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `check-preview-composition.sh`/`check-dx-class-prefix.sh`/`check-css-literals.sh` all clean; `sidebar.spec.ts`/`navbar.spec.ts`/`preview.spec.ts` re-run, 13/13 passed; a fresh manual axe scan against `/docs` itself (not covered by any pre-existing spec) came back with **0 violations**; independently confirmed against a live `dx serve` instance: exactly 3 sub-items under "Start" with the right labels and one icon each, clicking "Add a component" moves that `h2` from `y: 998` to `y: 144` (a real scroll, not just a URL change, and the URL stays on `/docs`), `/` and a component page both show 0 sub-items under "Overview" (unaffected) | Landed 2026-09-17 | M | User-requested; delegated to a single Sonnet subagent (files: `main.rs`, `sidebar/style.css`, `main.css` only), independently re-verified — cargo/lint, the existing suite, and a fresh manual axe scan plus live scroll-behavior measurement not covered by any pre-existing test — before landing |
| 63 | **Input OTP click-to-caret positioning did not generalize past the first slot — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-reported.** Row 60 fixed the OTP input's hit-target (every slot's box now correctly hands a click through to the real, invisible `<input>` via `pointer-events: none` on the decorative overlay) but left a second, deeper bug the user then caught: clicking slot 0 felt normal, but no other slot did. Root-caused live, before writing any fix: on an **empty** field, clicking slot 0, 3, or 5 all placed the caret at `selectionStart` 0; on a field filled with `"123"`, clicking slot 0 landed at index 2 while slots 1/4/5 all landed at index 3. Cause: the real `<input>` is `opacity: 0` with `font-family: inherit` and no `letter-spacing` tuned to the visual 40px slot grid (`input_otp/style.css`'s `.dx-input-otp-input` sets none) — a native browser places a click's caret by mapping its x-coordinate to where it thinks the *actual rendered glyphs* are, and since those invisible glyphs were never spatially aligned to the visible boxes, that mapping is essentially arbitrary past the trivial "empty field, nothing to click past" case that made slot 0 look uniquely correct. This is exactly why upstream `input-otp` (the library shadcn/ui's own `InputOTP` wraps) never trusts native click-to-glyph placement at all — it computes the clicked slot from geometry and calls `setSelectionRange` explicitly, which is what this fix does too. **Fixed** with a new `onclick` handler on the real input (`primitives/src/input_otp.rs`) that reads the click's client coordinates and spawns a one-shot `document::eval` (`snap_caret_to_slot`, mirroring this same file's own `use_caret_sync` and `virtual_list.rs`'s `sync_container_scroll` for the "one-shot eval, not a persistent listener" shape) that does a plain point-in-rect test against every `[data-slot-index]` element's own `getBoundingClientRect()` — not `elementFromPoint`, which the row-60 `pointer-events: none` fix makes blind to the slots entirely — scoped to this specific `InputOtp` instance's own overlay sibling so more than one on a page stays correct, and needing no special-casing for `InputOtpGroup`/`InputOtpSeparator` grouping since every slot's own rect is tested regardless of which group it's in. **Clamping rule:** `min(clicked_slot_index, value.chars().count())` — not an arbitrary choice, the only one `HTMLInputElement.setSelectionRange` itself can honor, since it clamps its own arguments to `[0, value.length]`. Consequence, confirmed and treated as correct rather than a shortfall: on a genuinely empty value every slot's click still lands at index 0, because no real `<input>` can place a caret past text that doesn't exist yet — verified this doesn't make the UI lie about it (`data-active` stays `true` only on slot 0, `false` on every other clicked-but-necessarily-clamped slot). **Verified by execution, independently re-checked by the orchestrating session:** `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; all three composition/class-prefix/css-literal lint scripts clean; before/after comparison via a genuine `git stash`/unstash of just this file (not hypothetical) reproduced the exact bug numbers above pre-fix and the exact corrected numbers post-fix; 4 new regression tests added to `playwright/input_otp.spec.ts` (empty-field clamping, partially-filled clamping across all 6 slots, fully-filled exact positioning, disabled-state no-op) — full suite **11/11 passed** on Chromium (7 pre-existing + 4 new); independently re-confirmed by the orchestrating session with its own live click test against a running `dx serve` instance, matching the subagent's numbers exactly at every slot for empty/partially-filled/fully-filled values | Landed 2026-09-17 | S | User-reported, found while re-testing row 60's fix on the live site; delegated to a single Sonnet subagent (files: `primitives/src/input_otp.rs`, `playwright/input_otp.spec.ts` only, disjoint from row 62's concurrent lane), independently re-verified — cargo/lint, the full existing+new suite, and the orchestrating session's own from-scratch click-position measurements, not just the subagent's reported numbers — before landing |
| 64 | **Input OTP: clicking a slot and typing didn't correct that digit — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-reported.** Row 63 (same day) fixed the OTP input's click-to-caret mapping, but the caret it placed was a *collapsed* one — correct for positioning, wrong for the user's next step: typing over it. Reproduced live before writing any fix: on a full value `"123456"` (at `maxlength`), clicking slot 2 then typing `9` left the value completely unchanged — the keystroke was silently swallowed; on a partial value `"123"`, clicking slot 1 then typing `9` produced `"1923"` (inserted, shifting `"23"` right), not the expected `"193"` (that slot's digit replaced). **Cause:** a collapsed caret makes native typing *insert* at that position; native *replace* semantics only kick in when an actual selection range covers the character about to be typed over — and insert additionally hits a silent `maxlength` block outright once the value is already full, explaining why the full-value case did nothing at all. This is exactly why upstream `input-otp` never trusts a bare caret for this: **fixed** in the same `snap_caret_to_slot` eval (`primitives/src/input_otp.rs`, row 63's own function) by selecting the one character at the clicked slot — `setSelectionRange(clamped, clamped + 1, 'forward')` — whenever `clamped < value_len` (a real character sits there); when `clamped == value_len` (clicked past the last real character, the ordinary "continue typing" case), the collapsed-caret behavior stays, since there's nothing there to select. **Direction chosen deliberately:** `'forward'` (anchor at `clamped`, focus at `clamped + 1`), matching this component's own left-to-right reading order (`ArrowRight` increases the index, `Delete` already acts forward from the caret) and giving Shift+Arrow a sensible extend-direction if this control is ever driven that way — verified to have zero effect on the *unshifted* nav this module already tests, since unshifted `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight` on any selection collapse to that selection's edge regardless of stored direction (the pre-existing "Backspace/Delete/ArrowLeft/ArrowRight" test passes unmodified, confirming this). **Verified by execution, independently re-checked by the orchestrating session:** `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; all three composition/class-prefix/css-literal lint scripts clean; a genuine `git stash`/unstash before/after comparison reproduced the exact broken values above pre-fix (`"123456"` unchanged, `"1923"`) and the exact corrected ones post-fix (`"129456"`, `"193"`); the empty-field and last-character-replace cases were also checked and behave correctly (clicking past an empty value's end still collapses to a plain caret, since nothing exists there to select; replacing the last filled character doesn't insert past `maxlength`); 4 new regression tests added to `playwright/input_otp.spec.ts` under a new "Click-then-type replace" block — full suite **15/15 passed** on Chromium (11 pre-existing unchanged + 4 new), independently re-run and re-confirmed by the orchestrating session with its own from-scratch click-then-type measurements at all three value states, matching the subagent's numbers exactly | Landed 2026-09-17 | S | User-reported, found while re-testing row 63's fix on the live site; | 68 | Cross-worktree cargo build-directory hazard | Found 2026-09-18, four independent occurrences | — | This session's `~/.cargo/config.toml` sets `build.target-dir` to one shared directory so parallel git-worktree lanes reuse compiled dependencies (`evaluation-draft.md`'s Environment notes) — intended as a speed optimization, but the sharing is not safe: cargo's own output-caching keyed the shared directory to another lane's build-script `OUT_DIR` and test binary, and **served them as if they were the querying lane's own**, not just slower-but-correct. **First found by Lane D (Resizable):** its own `cargo check -p preview`/`cargo test -p dioxus-primitives` runs in the shared directory silently picked up another lane's `preview` build-script `OUT_DIR` (containing `data_table`, a component Lane D never touched) and a foreign `dioxus-primitives` test binary — persistently, not a one-off flake. **Independently observed by three more lanes** (their own hand-backs each note re-verifying in an isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` once this was known: Lane B/Drawer — "the shared one proved to leak state across concurrent worktrees"; Lane H/typeahead and Lane G/portal-toggle-calendar — "the shared build directory was found to serve cross-worktree-contaminated artifacts"; Lane C/Navigation Menu also switched to an isolated dir for the same reason) **and by integration round 1 itself**, the other direction: round 1's own gate run used the shared directory against its own instruction to use an isolated one, so its `cargo test --workspace`/`clippy` results are flagged in `round1-report.md` as "to be re-confirmed in round 2's isolated dir" rather than trusted outright. **Rule, stated once so it does not recur a fifth time:** one `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` per worktree, always — the shared-directory speed optimization is incompatible with concurrent lanes and should not be reintroduced without a mechanism that actually isolates build-script output and test binaries per worktree, not just per crate |
| 69 | Axe scan readiness race (`playwright/axe.ts`), baseline group B | Found + fixed 2026-09-18 (construction); live verification confirmed round 2 | S | `playwright/axe.ts`'s `expectNoAxeViolations()` called `AxeBuilder.analyze()` immediately after `page.goto()` resolved, with no readiness wait. For this CSR wasm app the `load` event can fire before Dioxus paints its first frame into the empty `<div id="main">` shell, so the scan occasionally raced the app and saw the bare `<html lang="en">` — axe then reported `landmark-one-main` + `page-has-heading-one` against `html` itself, a false violation: every failing test's own `error-context.md` snapshot, captured moments later, shows a fully-formed page with exactly one `<main>` and one `<h1>`. Members: `empty`, `alert`, `tabs` (tab 1), `collapsible` (collapsed), `toolbar`, `button_group`, `breadcrumb`, `avatar`. **Construction (Lane I, commit `3cb0b16`):** 72 call sites share `expectNoAxeViolations`, so the fix lives in the helper (`waitForAppReady`), not per spec file — wait for the page's one `<main>` landmark to attach, then for a level-one heading to attach (`state: "attached"` only, no `networkidle`/visibility wait), bounded at 15s so a page that genuinely never renders still fails fast. Two waits, not one, because the race produces two violations and every route this suite scans renders both together as part of the same first paint (verified by reading every route fn in `preview/src/main.rs`). **Not verified against a live browser by the lane** (a mid-task sandbox resource correction ruled out starting a dev server there) — checked only with `playwright test --list` (all 694 tests across 61 files still enumerate, no parse/type error introduced). | 70 | `top_layer` oracle fixture probes intercepted by the docs Sidebar, baseline group C | Found + fixed 2026-09-18 (construction); live verification confirmed round 2 | S | Every route now permanently mounts `DocsLayout`'s `Sidebar` (PRs #51-57 dogfooding the real Sidebar site-wide), whose real container (`.dx-sidebar-container`) is `position: fixed; z-index: var(--dx-z-local-sm)` (10), occupying a `left: 0`, 16rem-wide column at any viewport ≥768px — including chromium's default 1280×720. The `top_layer` fixture (`/component/?name=top_layer&`) places several of its own probe triggers with `position: fixed` at literal viewport edges and **no `z-index` of their own** (`z-index: auto`) — the near-bottom-edge-flip row and one inline-styled modal-anchor trigger both sit under the Sidebar's column. A `position: fixed` box with `z-index: auto` always loses hit-testing to a sibling stacking context with an explicit positive `z-index`, regardless of DOM order — so Playwright's real `.click()`/`.hover()` (not `.evaluate(el => el.click())`, which bypasses hit-testing and is what several *passing* sibling tests in the same describe blocks use) retried until the outer 5-minute test timeout, naming `.dx-sidebar-desktop`/`.dx-sidebar-group-label`/`.dx-sidebar-menu-button` as the interceptor in Playwright's own actionability log. Live re-run of the 5 Group-C specs is confirmed: **round 2's targeted Playwright run against the integrated build found all 5 Group-C specs green** (`native-dialog.spec.ts` Rule 6c/6d/6e's `#popover-modal-anchor-trigger`, `top-layer.spec.ts` Rule 5 Tooltip/HoverCard flip tests) — fixed live, not just confirmed by `--list`. | 71 | Rule 11 self-overlap regression, baseline group D | Found + fixed 2026-09-18; live re-run against the integration server confirmed round 2 | S | Found in the 2026-09-18 baseline (4 cases: Select/Combobox "no self-overlap once open", Navbar simulated-keyboard, DropdownMenu simulated-no-anchor-engine) — a discrepancy from `conformance-harness.md`'s own last-recorded-good state (Rule 11 landed 2026-09-02, file "passing" after Rule 15 landed 2026-09-04), not a previously-accepted flake. Root cause, bisected by git archaeology plus an instrumented reproduction (Lane J): commit `daebe40` (2026-09-14ish, PR #54, sidebar TOC) added a global `html { scroll-behavior: smooth }` to `preview/assets/main.css` for legitimate in-page TOC-link navigation. `top-layer.spec.ts`'s `pinNearTop` fixture helper calls `scrollIntoView`/`window.scrollTo` with no `behavior` option, so both silently started animating over several hundred ms instead of jumping instantly — breaking `pinNearTop`'s own documented contract of delivering the trigger at a settled position *before* `overlay.open()` runs. Confirmed by execution: the trigger's `getBoundingClientRect().top` kept changing for 400-600ms *after* `pinNearTop` had already resolved (Combobox: 643→541→93→78, settling ~400ms later); `use_anchor_position_fallback`'s rAF-throttled scroll listener is necessarily one step behind a continuous CSS-driven scroll animation under real scheduling load (this sandbox's concurrent WASM/Chromium contention especially) — a real geometry mismatch between two moments of the *same still-settling* trigger, not a placement-formula defect. One root cause subsumes every Rule 11 failure observed this session, baseline's four plus two more (Combobox(a)/Navbar(a)) caught independently by the lane's own full-suite reproduction — every sub-case calls `pinNearTop` before opening, and the numeric signature (content positioned for an *earlier* trigger reading) was identical across every case inspected. Bisection: `git log -S "scroll-behavior: smooth"` pins the regression to `daebe40` exactly (absent in its parent, present in it); an isolated, non-full-suite Rule 11 rerun did **not** reproduce even pre-fix — this is a timing race whose window opens wide enough only under real CPU contention, so the full `top-layer.spec.ts` suite under 2-worker load is the only reliable regression check for it, not an isolated Rule-11-only run. **Fix (Lane J, commit `55ec9b1`):** qualify both `pinNearTop` scroll calls with `behavior: "instant"` (CSSOM View's standard per-call opt-out of an ancestor's `scroll-behavior: smooth`), restoring the helper's pre-existing contract without touching the legitimate site-wide smooth-scroll feature. Deliberately kept out of `primitives/src/top_layer.rs`: the fallback's tracking is already exactly as good as a rAF-throttled listener can be against a continuous CSS animation it has no visibility into, and scroll-tracking fidelity during a genuine in-flight scroll is Rule 8's own separately-tracked, already-documented limitation (row 21), not Rule 11's. **Repo-wide class note:** any Playwright helper that calls `scrollTo`/`scrollIntoView` without an explicit `behavior` is implicitly coupled to the page's `scroll-behavior` CSS — true everywhere in this suite, not just `pinNearTop`. **Residual, not closed by this fix:** the vertical clamp's own trigger-blind edge-hugging branch remains a plausible independent latent gap — no evidence of it firing today, flagged as a follow-up, not chased further. Verified by execution, own dev server on :8082: full `top-layer.spec.ts` pre-fix 5 failed (2 Rule 11 + 2 Group C + 1 row 21) / post-fix, two consecutive runs, 3 then 2 failed, zero Rule 11 in either. **Live re-run against the shared integration server (not the lane's own isolated :8082) is confirmed: round 2's targeted Playwright run found group D (Rule 11) all green, including all 26 Rule 11 sub-assertions.** `pw-baseline/BASELINE-REPORT.md` Group D is the full evidence trail; distinct from row 21 (Rule 8's own anchor-freeze) and row 70/Group C (a hit-testing interception) — three different mechanisms on the same spec file, not one bug wearing three names |

| 73 | Drawer drag hang — self-subscribing `use_effect` wedged the main thread on drag | Found in integration round 2 (2026-09-18), fixed round 3 same day | S | Round 2's live integration run found 2 of 9 `drawer.spec.ts` tests hanging the 5-minute ceiling at the raw `page.mouse.down()` call, after the drawer opened and geometry was measured ("a long, slow drag past the dismiss threshold closes the drawer", "a short, slow drag snaps back without closing the drawer") — no panic, no console output; a follow-up `page.evaluate` hung too, proving the tab's whole main thread was wedged, not just the test. Root cause: `DrawerContent`'s drag-continuation `use_effect` read `raw_offset()` with the tracked call syntax (subscribing the running effect to that signal) and then, in the same execution, wrote a new value to that same signal via `.set()` — writing to a signal an effect just subscribed to reliably re-triggers it, and nothing on this path ever broke the cycle, so from the first `pointerdown` the effect re-invoked itself synchronously, forever. Fixed (commit `b7037cb`, cherry-picked from `896e603`): `.peek()` instead of the tracked read for both of the effect's self-referential reads of `raw_offset` (the release-decision read and the accumulation read), matching the read-through-`.peek()`-write-through-`.set()` discipline `lib.rs`'s `use_animated_open` already documents and uses for its own generation counter, for exactly this reason. Verified live, own server: before, `mouse.down()`/a follow-up `evaluate` both hung 5s+ with no recovery; after, both return immediately and the real `drawer.spec.ts` run is 7/7, including both drag tests (3.0s, 1.4s). **House-wide review rule this implies:** any `use_effect` that reads a signal with tracked syntax and then writes that same signal in the same run will re-trigger itself forever unless something in the same run breaks the cycle — worth a standing review checklist line, not just a fix on this one file. **Closed 2026-09-18/19, batch 2, row 79:** that standing checklist line is now `scripts/check-self-subscribing-effects.sh` (`f11b98b`), proven against this exact bug by failing on `cdb073b` (this row's own pre-fix commit) and passing once `b7037cb` (this row's own fix) is applied. **Family note (2026-09-19, batch 3):** a second, independent instance of the same defect *shape* (an unconditional reactive cycle wedging the main thread, found live in a Playwright run) recurred in `DateRangePicker` — see row 83. Different construction there (a value-equality guard on the write side, not a `.peek()` read), because that cycle spans multiple effects through a callback, not one effect's own tracked read+write |
| 74 | Navigation Menu first-render panic (hook called inside another hook's closure) + demo/site-chrome accessible-name collisions | Found in integration round 2 (2026-09-18), fixed round 3 same day | S | Round 2's live integration run found 7/7 `navigation_menu.spec.ts` tests failing (2 fast, 5 hanging the 5-minute ceiling) on `getByRole('button', { name: 'Getting started' })` never resolving, plus `disclosure-navigation.spec.ts`'s "Library" block failing the same way (its Reference-page block, R1-R5, passed). Root cause #1: `NavigationMenu`'s root component called `use_delayed_action()` (a hook wrapping `use_signal`, `menu_sub.rs`) three times *inside* the closure passed to `use_context_provider` — calling a hook from within another hook's initializer closure re-enters the scope's hook-list `RefCell` while it is already borrowed, panicking at runtime ("BorrowMutError: The hook list is already borrowed ... hook inside a hook", dioxus-core's `scope_context.rs`) on first render, a wasm `unreachable` trap before anything — not even `main`/`h1` — mounts. **This is a runtime-only invariant**: not `cargo check`, not clippy, not the doc tests catch it, which is why the original lane's own thorough verification never surfaced it. Because `ComponentGallery` (the home page, `/`) renders every demo to build its gallery, this one bad demo's panic took down the entire home page and every home-page test with it (native-dialog Rule 8's "Show Popover" lookup, `preview.spec.ts`, hydration-parity Rule 4b) — **the class: one bad demo takes down `/`**, worth a standing awareness for anything `ComponentGallery` renders. Fixed (commit `c3dda45`, cherry-picked from `963d630`): hoisted all three `use_delayed_action()` calls out of the closure into the component body; audited every other `use_context_provider`/`use_hook`/`use_memo`/`use_effect`/`use_callback` closure in the file, no other instance (every other closure only calls plain non-hook constructors, the same safe pattern `AccordionContext::new` already uses). Root cause #2, found by the same live reproduction: the demo's featured-card link text ("dioxus-components") and its "Docs" link both collide with accessible names the site's own persistent chrome already uses on every route (header navbar brand, footer), so unscoped `page.getByRole('link', { name: ... })` queries resolved 2-3 elements page-wide instead of 1. Fixed by construction, not per-locator: featured-card title renamed "Component Library"; both spec files scope every query to the demo's own `aria-label="Component navigation menu"` landmark via a `nav(page)`/`libraryNav(page)` helper — demo content must not reuse accessible names the persistent chrome already renders, or specs must scope to the demo landmark. Verified live (own server, direct Playwright-API script, coordinator's server being read-only): 22/22 — R1-R5 plus every smoke-spec assertion. **Closed 2026-09-18/19, batch 2, row 79:** root cause #1's exact class (a hook called inside another hook's closure) is now guarded by `scripts/check-hooks-in-closures.sh` (`f11b98b`), proven against this row's own before/after commits (fails on `bb65cd6`, passes on `c3dda45` and current `HEAD`). **Family note (2026-09-19, batch 3):** row 83's `DateRangePicker` hang is a related-but-distinct class from this row's own hook-in-closure panic — grouped together only because both were runtime-only defects a live Playwright run caught that no static check (`cargo check`, clippy, doctests) could |
| 75 | Per-variant `component-preview-frame` duplicate id + unscoped Table caption locator (two strict-mode violations, one class each) | Found in integration round 2 (2026-09-18), fixed round 3 same day | S | Round 2's live integration run found two independent Playwright strict-mode violations. (a) `popover.spec.ts`'s basic test (`page.locator("#component-preview-frame")`, no `.first()`) resolved 2 elements once the `non_modal` demo variant existed alongside `main`, because every "Normal" component page renders one `ComponentVariantHighlight` per variant (main plus each "Variants" entry) and each copy hard-coded the literal id `component-preview-frame` on its own "Demo" `TabContent` — pre-existing for every multi-variant component (calendar's 5 extra variants, sidebar's `floating`/`inset` block variants), just never queried by that bare id before. Verified live on the fixing lane's own server: the basic test, previously reproduced failing with the reported strict-mode violation, passes warmed up (1.2s). (b) `table.spec.ts`'s basic test used an unscoped `page.getByText("A list of your recent invoices.")`, which also appears as literal source text in the page's always-mounted "Code" tab (`ComponentVariantHighlight` renders both "Demo" and "Code" `TabContent`s as siblings regardless of which is active) and in the "Manual installation" `<details>` section — so the unscoped lookup resolved 2 elements. **The same class, not a one-off**: any unscoped `page.getByText()`/`getByRole(..., {name})` whose string also appears in the demo's own rendered source is ambiguous on every component page in this app; the lane audited `table.spec.ts`, `data_table.spec.ts` and `sortable-table.spec.ts` for the same shape and found every other lookup already scoped under an attribute-selected container or constrained by a role the syntax-highlighted code spans never carry. Fixed (commit `fdb88bb`, cherry-picked from `5023dea`): scoped to the real `<caption>` inside the `data-slot="table"`-selected table, asserting both visibility and exact text.
