| 76 | Dev-loop measurements and tooling (`dev-wait.sh`, `inspect.mjs`, `base-url.ts`) | Landed 2026-09-18 (Lane K, round 3) | S | Lane K (commit `89f8e07`, cherry-picked from `a9f651c`) measured the actual edit-to-feedback latency on this repo's `dx serve --web` (debug, 4-core/15GB, isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`) rather than assuming it, and shipped tooling built on the measurements: an RSX text edit hot-reloads in ~0.5-0.8s, but — a newly found trap — **only to already-connected tabs**; a fresh page load or navigation (including a real Playwright test) keeps serving the old wasm regardless, confirmed by a held-open page vs. two independent fresh loads a minute apart. A component `style.css` edit under `src/` costs a full ~28s rebuild (only `preview/assets/` CSS hot-reloads, ~0.9s) — CSS under `src/` is not cheap here, contrary to a natural assumption. A Rust logic edit in a primitive is ~38s; a new module is ~85s end-to-end, subject to a second newly found trap (rapid multi-file saves can log a "successful" but incomplete rebuild before a trailing one actually lands the change). `dx serve --hot-patch` fails immediately on any Rust logic edit inside the `primitives` workspace crate ("Missing rustc args for replay: 'dioxus_primitives'") and stays broken for every subsequent edit on that server, even ones touching only `preview` — not usable as this workspace's default today. Delivered: `scripts/dev-wait.sh` (blocks on the dev server's log signal rather than the `/_dioxus/build_status` websocket, which exists and is documented but has a gap — no new message on a second hot-reload-only edit in a row — so log parsing stays primary), `scripts/inspect.mjs` (direct browser inspection — a11y/DOM outline, `--eval`, `--click`/`--press`, `--axe`, `--screenshot` — against a warm server in ~1-5s, no spec file, no full suite needed), and `playwright/base-url.ts` (a `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL` env override consolidating three pre-existing ad hoc `BASE_URL`/`BASE` patterns into one, default behaviour unchanged; migrated as a proof of concept in avatar/toolbar/sidebar/drag_and_drop_list.spec.ts, verified live against a non-default port). Full write-up, the complete measurement table, and the trap catalogue: [`dev-docs/dev-loop.md`](./dev-loop.md) (not edited by this docs pass). **Partially superseded/closed 2026-09-18/19, batch 2:** the 4-spec proof-of-concept migration this row describes is now essentially complete, not just started — see row 81 (`e0bb23f`/`2cb1b06`/`3cc0881`, every remaining spec migrated) and row 80 (`238c8f4`, the CSS-hot-reload root cause this row's own measurement table first surfaced, corrected from "move the files" to "stop compiling them in") |
| 77 | User-reported UI round (2026-09-18/19, batch 2): Command palette focus, Data Table filter, Tag Group sizing, Sheet, Resizable, Navigation Menu reflow, Drawer ×3 | Landed 2026-09-18/19 (batch 2) — six independent user reports against components rows 66-76 had just shipped | M | **2026-09-26 (audit batch): Navbar sub-item FIXED** in `7a2f68e` -- confirmed dead by in-page rAF sampling (open→closed in one frame, unmounted the next); the ENTRANCE was dead too. Fixed by the Navigation Menu construction: `use_popover_shown_while_mounted` instead of `use_popover_sync`, and `@keyframes ... forwards` instead of transitions (beats the inline `transform: none`). `navbar.spec.ts` regression test red 3/3 on main, green 3/3 fixed. Known, accepted limit: a close forced by the browser's own `popover="auto"` light dismiss still skips the animation. **Six user reports, each root-caused and fixed independently; the batch-2 lane hand-backs and its integration report are this row's source, cited here as session scratchpad, not as a repo artifact.** **Command palette first-open focus** (`1e6ae0f`): `command.rs` keeps its existing `autofocus` on the input and adds an `onmounted` → `spawn(set_focus(true))` backstop, mirroring the pattern `toggle`/`slider`/`dropdown_menu` already use, plus a forwarding `onmounted` prop. Root cause unconfirmed — the defect never reproduced on the CSR `dx serve` dev server across 40+ trials; the original report was against the deployed SSG+hydration build, so the backstop covers the class regardless. Follow-up: execute the SSG lane (row 22) to confirm against the build shape that actually showed it — batch 3. **Data Table filter question:** no defect — the existing predicate already matches shadcn's TanStack `includesString` (trimmed, case-insensitive substring). `0c0f627` gives the existing behaviour a name (`state.rs`'s `includes_string`, exported from `component.rs`) and 8 unit tests, changing nothing about how it matches; the dashboard email client's `filters.rs` has the identical shape, left untouched as a candidate for a future shared helper. **Closed 2026-09-19 (batch 3 integration, `b22a222`):** `filters.rs`'s `message_matches_search` now routes its five `.to_lowercase().contains(&query)` sites through `crate::components::data_table::includes_string` instead of its own manual trim+lowercase local, dropping the duplication this row's own text flagged — and fixing a latent bug along the way, found by the leftovers lane while proposing the refactor: `tag.label().contains(&query)` never lowercased the label side, so a mixed-case tag search silently under-matched. **Tag Group sizing** (`090d9f1`): confirmed NOT a token-migration regression (row 31b) — an audit of all 41 stylesheets row 31b touched found 363 exact-value matches and 130 equal-value unit rewrites, 0 mismatches, and upstream `bf007c1` renders the identical 20px tag today; the real, pre-existing gap is `text-sm` sizing plus `padding-block: var(--dx-space-1)` and `min-height: var(--dx-space-7)` under `border-box`, now rendering a 28px-tall tag (was 20px). **Sheet** (`12a8f86`): a shadcn/ui v4 translation pass — overlay tint moved onto the `<dialog>`'s own `::backdrop` on the web arm (the UA's `dialog::backdrop` opacity was stacking with the root tint, measuring ~55% instead of shadcn's 50%), `--dx-shadow-lg`, `sm:max-w-sm` gated to `@media (width >= 40rem)`, a 500/300ms slide, close-button `size-4`/`rounded-xs` — plus the Drawer-class centering fix, see row 78. **Resizable** (`47c6291`): the handle/grip were reading the wrong token pair (`--secondary-color-3/4`, a foreground-ish gray) instead of the border pair every other divider in this app uses (`--primary-color-6/7`); handle width corrected 4px → 1px (with a 4px `after:` hit area), grip 16×24px → 12×16px, demo container gained `max-w-md md:min-w-[450px]` (was unconstrained at ~269px against shadcn's 450px reference). Deliberate deviation, not a gap: shadcn's `h-[200px]` fixed content height is NOT translated, because this primitive's panels are pure-CSS flex-basis percentages over `overflow: hidden`, not react-resizable-panels' JS-measured layout — a fixed height clipped the nested 25% panel, caught by screenshot before it shipped. **Navigation Menu "width reflow"** (`08a14d8`): not a width transition — rAF sampling showed a constant width throughout; the panel-swap animation was dead, so switching between differently sized panels popped instantly, read by the reporter as a layout reflow. Two independent causes: (1) the `transition` rule, at the normal cascade tier, can never outrank the INLINE `transform: none` that `use_anchor_position_fallback` sets on every render (Navigation Menu sets no `data-side`/`data-align`, so the JS positioning fallback always engages, even on engines with native CSS Anchor Positioning); (2) the web arm's `use_popover_sync` calls `hidePopover()` synchronously on close, removing the content before any exit animation gets a frame. Fixed with `@keyframes`/`animation` (outranks an inline style) plus `use_popover_shown_while_mounted` (the same hook Tooltip/HoverCard/Popover/`SelectList`/`ComboboxList` already use for this exact reason, row 19) and `width: max-content`, gated by `prefers-reduced-motion`. The same commit adds the keyboard surface row 72 had deliberately left out (Home/End, panel Up/Down stopping at the ends, ArrowDown opening a closed trigger and focusing its first item) and fixes three latent bugs the keyboard work surfaced: content children mounting in reverse source order (now an explicit caller-supplied `content_index` prop), native Tab focus never syncing into the roving-focus collection (`collection.set_focus`, matching toolbar/tabs), and a stale-value no-op in the Escape-refocus handler. **Follow-up, outside this row's ownership:** `navbar.rs` pairs the identical `use_animated_open` + `use_popover_sync` combination, so it likely carries the same dead exit-animation defect (cause 2 above) — not investigated or fixed here. **Drawer, ×3 fixes, one shared construction** (`7310b89` + `2bf6489`): (1) the native `dialog`/`dialog:modal` UA stylesheet centers the panel as a floating card instead of anchoring it to its edge — the SAME class as Sheet's centering bug above, see row 78 for the shared construction and the sweep of the rest of `dialog.rs`'s consumers; the overdrag gap the fix leaves is covered by a solid `filter: drop-shadow` extension 40px past the edge (a `box-shadow`/`::after` would be clipped by the panel's own `overflow`). (2) `user-select: none` on the drag handle and on content while dragging, and the handle's `touch-action` corrected from `none` to a per-side `pan-*` value. (3) `DrawerClose` had no styling at all, so dark mode rendered the UA `ButtonFace` background at a measured 1.28:1 contrast — now styled like `.dx-button[data-style="outline"]`. `2bf6489` additionally makes the drag-continuation effect read `active_pointer_id` with `.peek()` (row 79) while keeping `dragging` under a tracked read as the effect's own wake switch. All six were verified live against the shared `:8080` server during batch-2 integration (per-lane table in the integration report, cited here only as session scratchpad, not re-run by this docs pass) |
| 78 | The native `<dialog>` un-freed-opposite-edge centering class | Landed 2026-09-18/19 (batch 2, Drawer + Sheet); swept clean elsewhere (batch 3) | S | **One construction, two instances this round, one clean sweep.** Both `Drawer` (`7310b89`) and `Sheet` (`12a8f86`) hit the identical bug shape independently: a per-side CSS rule overrides exactly ONE inset edge (e.g. `inset-inline-end: 0` on a right-anchored panel) while the UA's `dialog:modal` stylesheet's own `margin: auto` is left free to center the *opposite*, un-overridden edge — so the browser renders a floating, centered card instead of an edge-anchored panel. Measured on Sheet before the fix: a `[data-side="right"]` sheet's left edge at `x=440.5` against a 1280px-wide viewport (should sit flush right), and a `[data-side="bottom"]` sheet computing an 800px-tall box (should sit flush to the viewport's own height). Fixed identically in both: reset `margin: 0; inset: 0; width: auto; height: auto` as the shared base, then let each `data-side` variant re-open exactly the one edge it needs, so no UA default is ever left to fill a gap the component didn't ask for. **Swept for the same shape elsewhere, measured clean** — a concurrent batch-3 lane checked every other web-arm consumer of `primitives/src/dialog.rs` for the identical un-freed-opposite-edge shape (reported here as session scratchpad, not independently re-run by this docs pass): `Dialog` and `AlertDialog` both set `margin: 0` explicitly (killing the UA's auto-margin centering outright) and position via `top/left: <pct>; transform: translate(-50%, -50%)`, a self-contained technique with no dependency on the UA's un-set edges; `Command`'s command-palette dialog uses the same technique as `Dialog`; modal `Popover` uses `top_layer.rs`'s `ensure_anchor_positioning_styles`, which resets all four `inset` edges to `auto` at once, not one at a time. All four measured correctly centered/anchored at `:8080`, cross-checked against `getBoundingClientRect()` (every box's measured center matched its expected center exactly). One harmless, consistent artifact on all four: `getComputedStyle(...).right`/`.bottom` still report the dead UA-inherited value even though `getBoundingClientRect()` proves it unused in layout — an over-constrained `left`+`width`+`margin` box reporting a stale computed value, not a rendering bug, and not this class. **Subsumes:** Drawer, Sheet (both fixed by the shared construction above). **Does not subsume, because the shape never applied:** Dialog, AlertDialog, Command, modal Popover — swept and confirmed clean, a different centering technique entirely with no gap for this class to occur in |
| 79 | Reactivity guards: `check-hooks-in-closures.sh` + `check-self-subscribing-effects.sh` | Landed 2026-09-18/19 (batch 2) | M | **2026-09-26 (audit correction): RECONCILED** — `primitives/src/drawer.rs` (~lines 567-578) now explicitly cross-references the `NON_SELF_TERMINATING` allowlist entry ("Read this comment and that entry together"), closing the contradiction this row asked batch 3 to resolve. **Two new source-level guard scripts, joining the four already listed in `CLAUDE.md`** (`f11b98b`): `scripts/check-hooks-in-closures.sh` (no hook may be called from inside another hook's own initializer closure — the runtime-only panic class row 74 found only by execution, since neither `cargo check` nor clippy nor the doc tests see it) and `scripts/check-self-subscribing-effects.sh` (no `use_effect` may take a tracked read of a signal it also writes in the same run — the class row 73 found only by execution, hanging the main thread). The allowlisting was done anyway, per an explicit integration instruction that itself paraphrased that same reasoning before still directing the allowlist. Left unreconciled here on purpose: a human should decide whether `drawer.rs`'s comment gets a follow-up acknowledging the allowlist now exists, or whether the allowlist should instead be reverted in favor of a permanently-red, explained exception — a pass/fail script cannot represent that third option today. Batch 3 is asked to reconcile the two |
| 80 | CSS hot reload root cause: a compile-time embed, not the folder location | Landed 2026-09-18/19 (batch 2); supersedes row 72's "move stylesheets" bullet and row 76's number | M | **`238c8f4`.** Root cause: `dioxus_code::code!()` expands to `include_str!`, so a themed component's CSS text is compiled directly into the `preview` binary and lands in rustc's own dep-info; `dx`'s file watcher (`runner.rs::handle_file_change`) checks a changed non-`.rs` file against that dep-info list and, on a match, forces `needs_full_rebuild`, overriding that same file's own `asset!()` hot-reload path — the CSS is both compiled in and asset-served, and the compiled-in copy wins the watcher's decision every time. `build.rs`'s blanket `rerun-if-changed=src/components` was a compounding second cause, proven separately: narrowing it alone still produced a full rebuild, so it was not the sole mechanism. The same defect exists for `THEME_CSS` (`preview/assets/dx-components-theme.css`), which disproves row 76's original "CSS under `src/` is not cheap, `preview/assets/` CSS hot-reloads" theory — the mechanism is the compile-time embed, not which directory the file lives in. **Construction:** the `code!()` embed stays for release/SSG builds (`#[cfg(not(debug_assertions))]`, byte-identical output — not verified live in a browser, compile+lint only), so nothing changes for the shipped artifact; debug builds instead carry the `asset!()` URL and fetch-plus-syntax-highlight the CSS at runtime (`preview/src/main.rs`'s new `LazyCssCodeBlock`), so the watcher's dep-info list no longer contains the CSS text at all and the file's own `asset!()` hot-reload path is free to win. Measured: a `kbd/style.css` edit went from a 45.9s full rebuild to a 0.8s hot-reload, confirmed against a fresh, never-before-opened page load (not just an already-open tab, `dev-loop.md`'s own Trap 4). Checks: `fmt`, `check`/`clippy` in both debug and release, `cargo test --workspace` 23/23 (this component), all guards, stylelint, `preview.spec.ts` +1 ("style tab") 3/3. **Supersedes, in place:** row 72's "move component stylesheets so CSS edits hot-reload" bullet (the diagnosis there was directionally right about the symptom but named the wrong variable — moving files would not have fixed it, since `THEME_CSS` already lived in `preview/assets/` and had the identical defect) and row 76's "~28s" rebuild number (now historical: the same edit class is ~0.8s post-fix). `dev-loop.md` already carries this lane's own full write-up in its "CSS root cause, corrected" section — see there rather than duplicating it here |
| 81 | Fixtures-72 follow-ups, base-URL migration completion, and three full-suite-only test-timing fixes | Landed 2026-09-18/19 (batch 2) | M | **Row 72's two cheapest bullets, closed** (`8f3b10e`): `NavigationMenu` gets the identical 7-location oracle footprint `Navbar` already has in `top-layer.spec.ts` (Rule 1, axe, Rule 5, Rule 11 a/b + `CONFORMING_CASES` marked `supported: false`, Rule 13, Rule 14) via the same themed-wrapper fixture instance, and a raw-primitive `Select` fixture case with `scroll_lock: false` was added to `scroll-lock.spec.ts`, exercising row 9's opt-out prop for the first time; building the two cases found and fixed two methodology bugs along the way (the scroll-lock case's own `assertScrollIsUnlocked` helper, and an `evaluate(el => el.click())` call that bypassed real hit-testing). **Base-URL migration, complete** (`e0bb23f` + `2cb1b06` + `3cc0881`): every spec in the suite now honours `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL` (`playwright/base-url.ts`, row 76's own proof-of-concept). `e0bb23f` finished the 10 spec files the original migration (row 76) had deliberately excluded because their owning lanes hadn't landed yet, minus the 2 navigation_menu-owned specs, which had to wait for the navmenu lane; `2cb1b06` migrated 8 of those, and `3cc0881` finished the last 2 (`navigation_menu.spec.ts`, `oracle/tier1-apg/disclosure-navigation.spec.ts`) once navmenu landed. A repo-wide grep for the old hardcoded literal now turns up only `base-url.ts`'s own default/doc comment, one pre-existing prose mention in a header comment, and `ssg.local.config.ts`'s own unrelated doc comment — every executable occurrence is migrated. Recipe, one line (full account in `dev-loop.md`): set `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL` to point Playwright at any already-running server instead of the default `:8080`. **Three test-timing fixes found only by the contended full suite, not by any lane's own lighter-loaded run.** `3416551`: the shared `escapesClip` helper (`top-layer.spec.ts`, 11 call sites — native, Tooltip, HoverCard, Popover, DropdownMenu, ContextMenu, Menubar, Select, Combobox, Navbar, NavigationMenu) probed `document.elementFromPoint` immediately after `toBeVisible()`, with no wait for the probed element's own entrance animation — latent since the helper was written, because every one of those 11 subjects' entrance animations were themselves dead/instant until now; `NavigationMenu`'s own fix (row 77, `08a14d8`) was the first to make one genuinely run long enough to lose the race. Fixed in the helper itself, protecting all 11 callers: await `content.getAnimations({subtree: true})` before measuring. `f40c2b3`: Sheet's own new close-button test (row 77) measured the button's opacity and position immediately after `data-state` flipped, before the 500ms slide-in settled — the same fix, applied to the one caller that needed it directly. `4f05f75`: the drawer short-drag test's single `{ steps: 2 }` mouse move produced two internal samples with no timing guarantee, and when they landed under 1ms apart, `drawer.rs`'s own `elapsed_ms.max(1.0)` division guard (not a tuning knob) turned a near-instant step into an artificially explosive velocity, above the dismiss threshold — the file's own long-drag test already avoided this with an explicit per-step pause; the short-drag test now does too. `f7987aa`: `oracle/tier1-apg/sortable-table.spec.ts`'s DataTable R1 case read `sortableHeaders.all()` (a one-shot, non-retrying snapshot) before the CSR app's wasm bundle had rendered the table at all, unprotected by the auto-retrying `toBeVisible()`-style call every sibling test in the file happens to make first — fixed with one `expect(...).toBeAttached()` before the existing snapshot. **The lesson, worth stating plainly for the next round:** a lane's own green run against a lighter-loaded, single-purpose server is necessary but not sufficient evidence for a new Playwright test with any gesture or animation timing in it — full-suite contention is where these three surfaced, and none were flakes in the sense of being unfixable; each had a real, missing wait. The `getAnimations({subtree: true})` "wait for the entrance animation before measuring" idiom now exists in three independent places (`toast.spec.ts`, pre-existing; `escapesClip`; Sheet's close-button test) — worth extracting to a shared helper once a fourth caller needs it; not done here, since it would mean touching `toast.spec.ts`, an unrelated file with no defect of its own, for a pure refactor |

all cargo-gate evidence was already captured by that point). **New dx-serve trap, added to `dx-serve-hot-reload.md` as trap 6** (cross-referenced from `dev-loop.md`): near-simultaneous saves to two different files can make the watcher drop the rebuild entirely — no `Hotreloading:`, no `Build completed`, the process idles at ~2% CPU — rather than merely mis-time it (distinct from `dev-loop.md`'s existing traps 4 and 5). A trivial re-save of either file recovers it; observed once, on the shared dev server during integration — not restarted, per this task's own non-negotiable rule, but nudged with a trivial add/remove blank-line edit, which fired a rebuild ~20s later that picked up every pending change |
| 83 | DateRangePicker page hang — `DateElement`'s sync-down/sync-up effects form an unconditional two-way reactive cycle | Found and fixed 2026-09-19 (batch 3, date-picker + range-hang lanes) | S | **Same family as rows 73/74** (a reactive-cycle/hook-discipline defect that froze the whole tab, found live during a Playwright run, not by any static check). Two page hangs, confirmed to be ONE mechanism: (1) typing a complete year+month+day into a `DateRangePicker`'s own segment inputs (no popover involved) hangs the JS thread forever, moments after the demo logs the correct value; (2) opening a range popover, clicking a start day then an end day (completing the range) hangs identically. Root cause: `DateElement`'s sync-down effect (reads `props.selected_date`, writes the segment's year/month/day) and its sync-up effect (reads the three segments, calls `on_date_change`, which writes `start_date`/`end_date` back) form an unconditional two-way cycle, and `Signal::set` (dioxus-signals 0.7.9, `*self.write() = value`) marks dirty and reschedules subscribers **unconditionally** — so once the cycle completes, each hop re-triggers the next, forever. Evidence: the renderer measured at 97-125% CPU with RSS growing +4MB/s unbounded; CDP `Profiler`/`Debugger` commands never returned (the render thread never yields — a synchronous wasm loop, not a slow one). The single `DatePicker`/`RangeCalendar` alone never hung, because `DatePickerContext::set_date`/`DateRangePickerContext::set_range` already guarded against an equal write. Construction (`b66d00e`): one guarded choke point, `DateRangeInputContext::set_start_date`/`set_end_date` (`if *self.start_date.peek() != date { ... }`), every producer of a start/end write now routed through it, mirroring the equality guard the context-level setters already had one layer up — no change needed to `calendar.rs`/`popover.rs`/`top_layer.rs`. Verified (`c899951`): both of `date-picker.spec.ts`'s own `test.fail()` hang guards now pass; the full targeted 22-file batch finished in 3.9 minutes (a hang would have burned the whole per-test timeout); re-confirmed in batch-3 integration's own runs, every time. **Standing rule, proposed by this finding, not yet automated:** any write that closes a reactive cycle must be conditioned on the value actually changing, because `Signal::set` never is by itself. `scripts/check-self-subscribing-effects.sh` (row 79) does **not** catch this class — verified directly, it reports clean on the pre-fix file, because this is a multi-effect cycle closed through a `Callback`/method call, not one effect's own tracked-read-then-write the existing heuristic looks for. A wider check (whole-file read/write set plus `Callback` resolution plus guarded-vs-unconditional detection) is specified but **not built**: a narrower heuristic risks false-positiving on every legitimate parent-notification effect in the codebase — left as a review-only rule until a real construction is designed |
| 84 | Date picker — a11y/UX gaps found but not fixed this round | **Landed 2026-09-20 (round 4, `c03f021`)** — all six findings closed in one pass | S–M | Six findings from the same investigation that landed row 20's closure, recorded rather than fixed (out of that round's own scope): (1) `Calendar`'s unavailable days carry no `aria-disabled` (`calendar.rs`); (2) the month segment has no `aria-valuetext`, so a screen reader announces a bare number instead of a month name; (3) `aria-valuenow` on an untouched segment shows today's date before the person has chosen anything, rather than being absent/indeterminate; (4) Up/Down arrow wraps a segment's value (including the year segment, where wrapping is almost never wanted) instead of clamping; (5) the demo's own i18n language switcher is dead code — `main.rs`'s `i18n().set_language(id)` call is commented out; (6) the first Up/Down keypress on a segment that has never been touched commits that segment's default value with no visible change on screen, which reads as a missed keypress. None of these block row 20's own closure; filed together since one future pass on this primitive's remaining a11y surface can address all six at once. **Closed 2026-09-20 (round 4, `c03f021`):** findings 2, 3 and 6 turned out to be one construction, not three independent fixes — `aria-valuenow`/`aria-valuetext` now mirror `props.value`'s own `Option` faithfully (present once the segment actually holds a value, omitted while it doesn't) instead of substituting `props.default` the way the old `unwrap_or(props.default)` did; that alone fixes finding 3 (no more leaked default) and finding 6 as its direct corollary — an untouched segment's `aria-valuenow` was already reporting the default before any keypress, so the first Up/Down press committing that same default looked like a dropped keystroke, and once "no value" stops being reported as "= default" that same first press becomes a genuine attribute-absent-to-present transition. Finding 2's locale-aware month name rides the same "has a value" gate, via a new `on_format_month: Callback<Month, String>` threaded down to `DatePickerMonthSegment` (and the DateRangePicker's mirror path), defaulting to `Month::to_string()` — the same default `DatePickerCalendarProps` already uses for the grid. Finding 4 became an explicit per-segment wrap/clamp policy (`SegmentValueBounds.wrap`) rather than a blanket fix, calibrated against Chromium's own native `<input type="date">` and the APG spinbutton pattern: month/day stay cyclical (`roll_value`), the year segment now clamps at its bound (`clamp_value`) instead of jumping to the opposite end, since a year is not cyclical and Chromium's own year sub-field does not wrap either. Finding 1 is a direct fix, `aria-disabled` added to `calendar_day_attributes`, mirroring `data-disabled` exactly (same source value, same unconditional presence). Finding 5's i18n switcher is now wired rather than deleted — `main.rs`'s `i18n().set_language(id)` call, previously commented out, is live (a contained two-line fix: import `i18n`, make the call), per this task's own stated preference for a working control over a deleted one. Evidence: every finding got a Playwright test confirmed RED on the unmodified tree (via a git-stash round-trip, so RED is the real base commit, not reasoning) and GREEN after, plus new Rust SSR/unit tests in both `calendar.rs` and `date_picker.rs` (the wrap-vs-clamp unit test additionally verified red by temporarily reverting just that branch); one pre-existing test (the non-digit-keystroke guard) needed updating for the now-legitimate `aria-valuenow === null` case, no other collateral hits found. Left deliberately alone, unrelated: the two pre-existing row-21 popover-anchoring failures (Chromium's own anchor-freeze) |
| 85 | DropdownMenu/ContextMenu — a raw DOM `.focus()` on a sub-trigger closes the whole menu | **Landed 2026-09-20 (round 4, `3ba793b`)** — fixed by construction across DropdownMenu, Menubar, and both Sub levels | S to verify | Not an RTL defect — reproduces identically on the untouched `main`/LTR variant of both `DropdownMenu` and `ContextMenu`, found only because this round's own RTL oracle exercises submenus more thoroughly than any prior spec. Calling `.focus()` directly on a submenu's own sub-trigger element (as a test driving focus programmatically would, and as an assistive-technology focus-restoration path plausibly could) closes the entire menu and reverts focus to the ROOT trigger, rather than simply focusing the sub-trigger in place. Not investigated further or fixed this round (out of rtl-rust's own remit); worth a dedicated look at `menu_sub.rs`'s focus-out/blur handling the next time either menu family is touched. **Closed 2026-09-20 (round 4, `3ba793b`), root-caused as a class rather than the single instance this row named:** every affected site shared one shape — a synchronous `onblur` deciding "did focus leave" by reading a Rust-tracked roving-focus signal (`ctx.focus.any_focused()` / `menu_ctx.focus.any_focused()` / `sub.focus.any_focused()`) that only this crate's own click/keyboard paths ever write (state-first, DOM-focus-second), and that no item in this "menu button" pattern family reconciles via its own `onfocus` — so a focus move arriving any other way (a test's raw `.focus()` call, or an assistive-technology focus-restoration path) read stale "nothing focused" state and closed the whole menu. `DropdownMenuTrigger`'s root and, independently, `MenubarTrigger`/`MenubarItem` and the shared submenu level (`DropdownMenuSubTrigger`/`ContextMenuSubTrigger`, `menu_sub.rs`'s `SubMenuState`) all carried the identical shape — Menubar's was the most directly reproducible of all (arrow-down onto an item then `.focus()` on its own trigger closed the content unconditionally, no mouse-opened precondition needed). **`ContextMenu`'s own root — this row's original, named instance — could not be reproduced red in this pass**: it already dismissed via `use_outside_dismiss` (DOM-containment-based, immune to the same-tick blur-before-focus ordering hazard by construction), so this row's original claim of a ContextMenu-root defect does not hold up under direct testing, stated plainly rather than smoothed over. Fixed by construction anyway, for uniformity: `DropdownMenu`'s root and `Menubar`'s own per-menu wrapper now get the identical `use_outside_dismiss` construction `ContextMenu`'s root already had (a new `root_id` on each), plus a new two-id variant (`menu_sub::use_sub_outside_dismiss`, since a `Sub`'s trigger and content are two siblings, never one wrapped element) for the shared submenu level in both `DropdownMenu.Sub` and `ContextMenu.Sub`; every displaced `onblur` branch was removed rather than left as a second, still-fragile path to the same conclusion. The new `ContextMenu`/`ContextMenu.Sub` tests are documented in-file as construction-parity, not a reproduced defect. **`navigation_menu.rs` shares the identical underlying constraint** (its own module doc already cites the same `dioxus-html` 0.7.9 missing-`related_target` gap this fix cites) but already answers it with a different, correct construction of its own — a debounced close plus every element's own `onfocus` cancelling it, robust to an external `.focus()` call by construction — checked during this investigation and deliberately left untouched. Evidence: RED confirmed for `DropdownMenu` (plain item and sub-trigger) and `Menubar`, exact before/after `data-state`/DOM-count values in the commit body; full existing suites re-verified green (`dropdown-menu.spec.ts` 2/2+axe, `context-menu.spec.ts` 13/13, `menubar.spec.ts` 3/3+axe, `oracle/tier1-apg/menu-submenu.spec.ts` 16/16, `keyboard-matrix.spec.ts`+`menu-roles.spec.ts`+`focus-restore-reference.spec.ts` 87/88 with one pre-existing intentional skip); `cargo test --workspace` 433/0. **This row's own landing then introduced a regression, found and fixed the same round:** `3ba793b` gave `DropdownMenu`'s root `div` and `MenubarMenu`'s own wrapping `div` a literal `id: root_id.cloned()` (plus, on `MenubarMenu`, a literal `role:`) beside the unmerged `..props.attributes` spread already on that element, with a comment claiming a caller-supplied `id` "still wins (spread order)" — false: SSR emits both, a WHATWG duplicate-attribute parse error (`dev-docs/conformance-harness.md` hydration-parity Rule 4, extended). It shipped into the prerendered site as `docs/component/top_layer/index.html`'s `<div id="dxc-1091" ... id="clip-dropdown-menu-root" ...>`, caught by `playwright/oracle/hydration-parity.spec.ts`'s "Rule 4 (extended)" when the main loop ran it against a freshly regenerated site after round-4 integration. Fixed by `f2be1d7` (`fix(menu): dedupe the id/role duplicate 3ba793b introduced on DropdownMenu/Menubar roots`): both elements' full literal-attribute sets now route through `merge_attributes`, the same construction `context_menu.rs`'s own root already uses (`b35d671`). Two new Rust SSR tests (one per component) assert a caller's `id` renders exactly once — both proven RED on the pre-fix tree (2 `id="` occurrences, e.g. `<div id="dxc-0" ... id="my-menu-root">`) and GREEN after (1) — and the fix was re-verified end-to-end against the documented SSG recipe (`dx build --ssg --features fullstack --platform web`, no `--base-path`, served at `/` on :8090, `SSG_SITE_DIR` set): `oracle/hydration-parity.spec.ts` **11/11 green**, the fixture now carrying a single `id="clip-dropdown-menu-root"`. **Corrects this row's own evidence above:** the "full existing suites re-verified green" list this row cites (`dropdown-menu.spec.ts`, `menubar.spec.ts`, etc.) ran CSR-only against a live `dx serve` dev server and could never have caught this regardless of how thoroughly it was run — the browser DOM coalesces sequential duplicate `setAttribute` calls into one, so the second attribute is only ever visible in SSR/SSG's raw text output; the hydration-parity SSG lane is the only thing in the whole harness able to catch this class at all |

`component-backlog.md`'s Chart row for the full alternatives table. **Engine/layer seam (upstream-shaped by construction):** `primitives/src/chart/engine/{scale,curve,stack,geometry,table,data}.rs` is (`ChartContext`/`use_chart`) bridges that plain data into this crate's own reactivity and `components/{container,chart,tooltip,legend}.rs` are the Dioxus-facing pieces. **d3-derived math, ISC attribution, ported not copied:** `LinearScale::ticks`/`nice_domain` are built against `d3-array/src/ticks.js`'s actual `tickSpec` magnitude-threshold arithmetic (`e10=sqrt(50)`/`e5=sqrt(10)`/`e2=sqrt(2)` cutoffs), and the `Monotone` curve against `d3-shape/src/curve/monotone.js`'s Steffen (1990) tangent formula — both ISC-licensed (Mike Bostock), cited by file+SHA in `engine/scale.rs`'s and `engine/curve.rs`'s own module docs, algorithm ports (JS to Rust) with commit-message/doc attribution, never verbatim text (see `dev-docs/lifting-from-forks.md` §1's new ISC note). **The Steffen three-way-min precision finding** (`dev-docs/research/chart-forks-2026-09-19.md` §4.1, the single most load-bearing finding in that survey): d3-shape's real tangent formula clamps against *both* neighboring slopes, `(sign(s0)+sign(s1)) * min(abs(s0), abs(s1), 0.5*abs(p))` — a three-way min. `feral-dot-io/leptos-chartistry`'s own MPL-2.0 Rust port of the same 1990 paper silently drops the `abs(s1)` term (a two-way min), which can overshoot near a local extremum; this repo's `engine/curve.rs` implements the correct three-way-min directly against d3-shape's own source, not a from-memory/secondary-source version, for genuine shadcn/Recharts visual parity — a caution recorded for the next person tempted to port from a *sibling* library's Rust code instead of the *canonical* JS source. **A11y contract** (`dev-docs/research/chart-2026-09-19.md` §2.5, ahead of the field: the forks survey found ZERO accessibility semantics — no ARIA/role/tabindex at all — in every non-shadcn/Recharts-lineage chart library surveyed, `leptos-chartistry`/`yew-chart`/`dioxus-charts`/`rust-ui/dioxus-ui`'s ApexCharts DOM all included): `svg[role="img"]` + required `aria-label` + `<title>`/`<desc>` (WAI-ARIA Graphics Module 1.0's own `img` definition — a single indivisible graphic, no claim of internal navigability); a real, visually-hidden `<table>` mirroring the exact series/category/value data as the actual, free, natively-keyboard-navigable screen-reader path; legend swatches `role="graphics-symbol"` (the Graphics Module's atomic-repeated-glyph role) + `aria-label`; optional (default on) arrow-key/Home/End/Escape stepping of the active index, cited to Recharts' `accessibilityLayer` as a tier-3 opinion, additive only, never `role="application"` (which would strip screen-reader browse-mode navigation the hidden table makes unnecessary to sacrifice). Oracle: `playwright/oracle/tier3-radix/chart.spec.ts` (folder name deliberately KEPT, not generalized to `tier3-opinion/`, per the research's own §6.5b decision point — its header names Recharts' `accessibilityLayer` as the behavior/keyboard opinion source and the WAI-ARIA Graphics Module 1.0 as the role source, since Chart has no Radix primitive to calibrate against at all, confirmed by cloning `radix-ui/primitives` and finding zero chart files) plus `playwright/chart.spec.ts`'s own smoke+axe tests — 13/13 (8 rules across 10 tests in the oracle file, 3 in the smoke file), verified live against the integration server. **The `<title>` SVG-namespace note:** `dioxus-html` 0.7.9 has no SVG-namespaced `<title>` element, so `Chart`'s `<title>` (inside its `<svg>`) is built without the SVG namespace when Dioxus creates the DOM directly (confirmed by reading `dioxus-web`'s `create_template_node`) — a CSR-only-page-load discrepancy that does NOT affect this repo's actual deployed site (SSR + hydrate: the browser's own HTML parser correctly parses a `<title>` nested in `<svg>` *source text* as an SVG title per the HTML5 foreign-content spec regardless of Dioxus's per-element namespace table, and hydration reuses that already-correct node) — confirmed to NOT produce a hydration error in this round's own SSG execution (`hydration-parity.spec.ts` Rule 2/2-extended: PASS, part of an overall 11/11 against a real 72-route release build); documented in `Chart`'s own module doc, not fixed (not fixable from inside a primitive component). **Two integration-owned fixes, found live against a running server (neither caught by compilation):** (1) the hidden table's corner `<th>` was empty (`th {}`), which axe's `empty-table-header` rule correctly failed on every scan and regressed `preview.spec.ts`'s homepage a11y scan (the homepage renders every component's `main` demo) — fixed with a new `x_label: String` prop (default `"Category"`) rendered as the corner `<th scope="col">`; (2) the `main` demo's 90-datum view drew 90 overlapping one-per-day x-axis tick labels — fixed with a new `max_x_ticks: usize` prop (default 12), a pure `x_tick_step` stride function labeling only every `ceil(n / max_x_ticks)`-th datum (always the first), documented as MVP count-based thinning with the forks survey's width-aware alternative (`leptos-chartistry/src/ticks/gen/aligned_floats.rs`) cited as the stage-2 follow-up. **A third integration fix, found by direct inspection while writing this docs pass, not by any spec:** the themed `preview/src/components/chart/component.rs` wrapper reconstructs its inner primitive call field-by-field rather than spreading its own `ChartProps`, and that manual list predated `x_label`/`max_x_ticks` -- a caller's override of either compiled fine but was silently dropped before ever reaching the primitive (no spec asserts the corner header's specific text, and the primitive's own unit tests construct `Chart` directly, bypassing this wrapper). Fixed by adding the two fields to the forwarding list; verified live (`main`'s corner header now reads its own "Date" override, `bar`'s reads the default "Category"). **Out of scope, deliberately:** Pie/Radar/Radial (deferred to future lanes; `engine`'s file split leaves room for a `polar.rs` alongside `curve.rs` without disturbing this round's files — research's own §6.8 stages 3-5); tunable margins (`MARGIN_*`/`BAND_PADDING`/`GROUP_PADDING` in `chart.rs` are fixed MVP constants, not props — a themed-CSS-first precedent this repo's other multi-part primitives, e.g. Resizable, already follow); memoization (`Chart`'s scale/tick/geometry pipeline is plain, unmemoized Rust computed fresh on every render — confirmed by reading the component, no `use_memo` anywhere in `chart.rs`/`context.rs` — and since `active_index` is read at `Chart`'s own top level rather than behind a memo boundary, every hover-driven cursor move re-triggers the FULL pipeline, not just the cursor's own position; a real, not-yet-measured performance question for a future round, not a correctness gap). Full test/gate/oracle account: all 11 `CLAUDE.md` gates + rustdoc ×3 green (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`); `cargo test --workspace` 260 primitives (75 chart) + 31 preview + 142 doctests, 0 failed; targeted Playwright 13/13 chart + 3/3 `preview.spec.ts`; SSG execution (release+ssg+fullstack, isolated dir) 72 routes (up from batch-3's 71, +1 for Chart), 0 not-found shells, `hydration-parity.spec.ts` 11/11 against the real build; full suite (`:8090` kept up) 910 passed/7 failed/12 skipped, diffed programmatically against the batch-3 baseline: 1 fixed (a pre-existing intermittent flake, not this round's work), 5 still-red (all previously documented -- date-picker anchoring + ColorPicker Rule 8, row 21; main-thread combobox/command/date_picker, row 45), 2 new and both explained, not regressions (date-picker's own documented flip-flopping anchoring sibling; `main-thread.spec.ts`'s `select: open` crossing its 50ms/200ms thresholds under this run's measured, unusually high concurrent system load from unrelated lanes -- the identical pre-existing `use_anchor_position_fallback` mechanism row 45 already documents for combobox/select/date_picker, confirmed via a within-session A/B: the same test passed cleanly at low load right after this round's own rebuild and only failed once concurrent load spiked). Full account: this round's own integration report. Links: `dev-docs/research/chart-2026-09-19.md` (the approved engine/a11y/design research), `dev-docs/research/chart-forks-2026-09-19.md` (the fork-network + d3 survey, new this round), `component-backlog.md`'s Chart row |
| 89 | Selected/active state losing to :hover on click — a user-reported hover-over-state specificity class, swept and partly fixed (calendar-state lane) | Landed 2026-09-19 (calendar, tabs); **residue closed 2026-09-20 (round 4, `7fdfacb`)** — toggle/toggle_group hardened (was never actually broken), pagination confirmed benign, both honest non-fix outcomes | S | **User report:** "date picker: active state on selected date must be over hover state, otherwise active states are not visible after click" — a click leaves the pointer resting on the day it just selected, so hovering-what-you-just-selected is the common case, not an edge case. **Root cause, a real class, not one instance** (per this file's own "two or more is a class" rule): a component's plain `:hover` rule picked up extra specificity from a `:not([data-disabled="true"])` clause (raising it to (0,4,0)) and outranked a persistent-state selector like `[data-selected="true"]` or `[data-state="active"]` ((0,2,0)/(0,3,0)) that never anticipated needing to beat it — an accident of which selector happened to carry an extra clause, not a deliberate priority order. **Fixed by construction in two components**: every interactive-only `:hover` rule in `calendar/style.css` and `tabs/style.css` is now wrapped in `:where(...)`, which matches the exact same elements but forces its specificity to the lowest possible (0,0,0), so every persistent-state selector in the file outranks it unconditionally regardless of source order or accidental extra clauses — cheaper and more durable than reordering rules or reaching for `!important`. `:focus-visible` deliberately left un-wrapped in both files (it was never part of the bug and must keep winning on its own terms). Two real defects fixed: `calendar/style.css`'s dark-mode selected-day hover had drifted to different token values than its light-mode branch (a duplication that was the actual defect, not just a specificity accident) and a range selection's in-range "between" day had no hover companion at all, so the generic hover rule always won; `tabs/style.css`'s active-tab hover silently dimmed the active text color in both themes. **Swept, not fixed (recorded rather than silently dropped):** `pagination`'s own equivalent rule pair has a subtle token mismatch left on the watch list (not the same specificity accident, so not folded into this construction); `toggle`/`toggle_group`'s active-vs-hover tie is order-dependent rather than specificity-driven, a different shape of the same symptom, not fixed here. **Test-methodology trap found and worked around while writing the regression specs, worth recording for the next person:** clicking a real `<a href="#">` in a Playwright test navigates the page (breaking the rest of that test), and reading `getComputedStyle` mid-transition can catch an interpolated, neither-old-nor-new color value — both new tests wait past the false-signal cases before asserting. Evidence: `playwright/calendar.spec.ts`'s two new tests and `playwright/tabs.spec.ts`'s two new tests (light+dark) all confirmed RED on the unmodified tree with exact computed-color values, GREEN after the fix; full `calendar.spec.ts` 9/9, `tabs.spec.ts` 5/5, `date-picker.spec.ts` (the two pre-existing row-21 anchoring reds unrelated), `oracle/tier2-html/native-dialog.spec.ts` all re-verified green on the integration server. **Residue closed 2026-09-20 (round 4, `7fdfacb`), with two honest non-fix outcomes, not two more bug fixes:** `toggle`/`toggle_group` were diagnosed before reaching for `:where()`, per this lane's own discipline, and turned out NOT to be misbehaving — unlike calendar/tabs's accidental specificity bump, `.dx-toggle:hover`/`.dx-toggle[data-state="on"]` (identically for `toggle_group`) tie exactly at (0,2,0), and an ON control already keeps its "on" background while hovered on the unmodified tree, purely because `[data-state="on"]` happens to be declared after `:hover` and wins the tie by source order — fragile (order-dependent), not broken. Applied the same `:where(:hover)` construction anyway, scoped to only the `:hover` branch (`:focus-visible` untouched, since it was never part of the bug), so the win becomes unconditional (specificity, not accidental order) — confirmed byte-identical computed background-color values before and after, in both themes: a hardening, not a visible-bug fix, and the new Playwright tests in `toggle.spec.ts`/`toggle_group.spec.ts` document it as exactly that. `pagination`'s own watch-listed mismatch is confirmed benign, not fixed: the background token pair (`[data-active="true"]`'s own vs. its `:hover`'s) is indeed mismatched, real if subtle (exact RGB values captured, both themes) — but the actual "you are here" signal is the 1px border `[data-active="true"]` alone sets, which `:hover` never touches in either state and which no non-active link ever gets in any state; the CSS was left unchanged and the computed border-color/border-width evidence recorded in a new `playwright/pagination.spec.ts` (4 tests), per this row's own "genuinely benign is a legitimate outcome" allowance rather than a manufactured fix. Evidence: `toggle.spec.ts`/`toggle_group.spec.ts` full suites re-verified green (5/5 each, incl. axe); `cargo test --workspace` (433 passed, 0 failed), `check-css-literals.sh`/`check-css-logical-properties.sh`/`check-dx-class-prefix.sh`, and `cd preview && npx stylelint "src/**/*.css"` all re-run and clean after the CSS-only change, per this repo's own "every gate before every commit" rule rather than assumed unaffected |

a family-agnostic tooltip-anchor construction so Radar's tooltip stops falling back to dead-centre; two verbatim CSS blocks for `chart/style.css` (tooltip indicators/icons/total-row from `s2-tooltip`, radar grid rings from `s2-radar`) that also need reconciling against `03927e3`'s own tooltip/legend grid-layout rewrite, which none of the seven lanes has seen. **Nothing here is pushed** — every lane lives only in this container's own git worktrees (`.claude/worktrees/agent-<id>`), and `s2-radar`'s gallery only in its scratchpad. Full per-lane commit lists, verbatim code/CSS, the ownership map, and the exact preservation commands are in `dev-docs/issues/chart-stage2-handoff.md` — this row is a pointer, not a substitute for it. **LANDED 2026-09-25 (round 8, `2d77f2b`..`6a9f936` plus the cross-lane construction commit `b6806f5`):** all seven parked lanes integrated onto one tree — per-type galleries now match shadcn's own inventory exactly: Area 10/10, Bar 10/10, Line 10/10, Tooltip 9/9, Radar 14/14, Pie 11/11, Radial 6/6 (70 demos total). All five cross-lane constructions from §4 above landed: (a) the struct-update-spread fix, applied to `ChartTooltip`/`ChartLegend`/`ChartContainer` (and confirmed still correct on `Chart` itself, which `s2-refactor`'s own `b1cc636` had already fixed); (b) `ChartKind::has_full_table()`, decoupling the hidden-table full-vs-reduced choice from `is_cartesian()` so Radar gets its correct categories-×-series table; (c) a `StackMode`-aware `components::layout::build`, so a percent-stacked chart's shared grid/axis/tooltip-anchor y-domain agrees with its own marks, not just the marks alone; (d) a family-agnostic `ChartLayout::anchor_percent` construction, so `ChartTooltip` positions correctly for Radar's sinusoidal vertex geometry instead of falling back to dead-centre; (e) both pending CSS blocks (tooltip indicator/icon/total-row rules from `s2-tooltip`, radar grid-ring rules from `s2-radar`) reconciled into `chart/style.css`, including a new polar block, against `03927e3`'s own tooltip/legend grid-layout rewrite none of the seven lanes had seen. `RadialBar`'s primitive was built out from the empty stub `s2-polar` left behind (`RadialOptions{inner_radius, outer_radius, start_angle, end_angle, corner_radius, grid, labels, stacked, center_text}`); single-series Pie/RadialBar legends key per datum, matching shadcn's own behaviour; two of the drafted specs' stale `?variant=` navigation calls were fixed. Evidence: 207/207 chart-specific tests green on the integration lane's own tree; all `CLAUDE.md` gates green on the fully integrated tree (`cargo test --workspace` 445 primitives + 38 preview + 160 doctests, 0 failed). **Browser + SSG validation now DONE 2026-09-25 (round 8b; was: reported in progress at the first round-8 docs pass, above):** on `972b32e`, a targeted chart-only Playwright run was 266 tests / 262 passed / 4 failed, and the full suite was 1252 / 1200 passed / 34 failed / 18 skipped, with 30 of the 34 matching the already-settled baseline list and the remaining 4 (`pie_chart.spec.ts:77,116,186`, `radial_chart.spec.ts:47`) root-caused as a test race, not a component defect — a non-retrying `.count()`/`.evaluateAll()` called immediately after `goto` on the default `load` event, before the gallery's own client-side work had settled — fixed in `f468f78` by giving every `goto` in both spec files `waitUntil: "networkidle"`, verified 102/102 with `--repeat-each=2` (the same class as this file's new row 109, under a weaker fix — see that row's own follow-up note).
