| 91 | Carousel landed — CSS scroll-snap engine, tier-1 APG oracle vendored, three live-verification fixes | **Landed 2026-09-20 (round 4; `d64b014`, `9a301b9`, `01b7437`, `2046136`, `5b8c21a`, `2ec1b70`)** | L | **User decision (row 86's engine question, closed):** a CSS scroll-snap engine written in Rust — matches the 2026-09-19 approved defaults in full: `loop` deferred (still the single largest scope lever), autoplay/rotation-control (and its `aria-live` region) deferred to a fast-follow (a manually-paged carousel is fully APG-conformant on its own, per `carousel-2026-09-19.md` §1.2), the tablist-picker variant deferred by one iteration. Root-level `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight` shipped, RTL-swapped per Radix's convention (not shadcn's own unswapped one); vertical orientation shipped in v1 too, alongside horizontal, since it fell out of the same construction for free rather than being its own decision point. **Construction** (`primitives/src/carousel.rs`, new, ~1262 lines — `Carousel`/`CarouselContent`/`CarouselItem`/`CarouselPrevious`/`CarouselNext` plus `CarouselApi`/`use_carousel()`): zero new Cargo/npm dependency — paging calls each target slide's own `scrollIntoView()` rather than hand-rolled offset math, so there is no manual pixel calculation and no RTL sign-flip to get wrong, and a second, independent `document::eval` bridge (`use_carousel_scroll_tracking`) keeps the selected index correct after native drag/wheel/trackpad scrolling by comparing `getBoundingClientRect()` positions — never `scrollLeft`, which disagrees across browsers under RTL — via `scrollend` where supported and a debounced `scroll` listener otherwise. a11y: `role="region"` + `aria-roledescription="carousel"` on the root (`tracing::warn!` when no accessible name is supplied), `role="group"` + `aria-roledescription="slide"` + a default "N of M" label per item, SSR-stable by construction (the label is empty pre-hydration on both server and client, never a leaked "1 of 0"); `CarouselPrevious`/`Next` are genuinely `disabled` (native, not `aria-disabled`) at the ends, since v1 has no loop. Themed package: `preview/src/components/carousel/` (5 variants — main, multiple, indicators, vertical, rtl — each with its own non-colliding `aria-label`, per the known "same page, same visible name" strict-mode collision class `oracle/tier3-radix/rtl.spec.ts`'s own header already documents). **Three defects live verification caught that the unit/SSR suite alone missed entirely:** `CarouselContent` had no way for a keyboard-only user to reach the scroll region directly (axe `scrollable-region-focusable`; fixed with `tabindex="0"`, matching `ScrollArea`'s own already-documented advice); the `rtl` demo's own `aria-label` ("RTL carousel demo") literally violated the "name must not contain the word carousel" rule the library's own oracle tests for — a self-inflicted fixture bug, renamed to "Right-to-left slideshow demo"; and a chevron icon inside Previous/Next kept pointing a fixed physical direction under `dir="rtl"` even though the button's own position correctly mirrored via `inset-inline-*` — fixed by construction, not by picking a different icon in the demo, with one `.dx-carousel[data-direction="rtl"] .dx-carousel-previous/-next svg { transform: scaleX(-1); }` rule scoped to horizontal+RTL, so any consumer's icon choice auto-mirrors, not just this demo's own. **Oracle:** a sixth vendored APG reference page, `playwright/oracle/reference/7e4034b/content/patterns/carousel/`, the same pinned commit `7e4034b` every other reference page in this tree already uses (`conformance-harness.md`'s own Layout section has the updated tally; the tablist example deliberately not vendored, since that variant is itself deferred); `playwright/oracle/tier1-apg/carousel.spec.ts` (11 tests, dual-subject like `disclosure-navigation.spec.ts`: region role/name, slide role/roledescription/"N of M" label, native button semantics + Enter/Space paging, tab order via a `compareDocumentPosition`-based `precedes()` helper rather than simulated Tab presses) plus `playwright/carousel.spec.ts` (30 tests, smoke+a11y+paging across all 5 variants) — 41/41, three times in a row on a live server. `CarouselPrevious`/`Next`'s genuine `disabled` state at the ends is deliberately its own, non-dual-subject `describe` block: the reference *loops* and never disables either button, so a dual-subject rule for that would fail the reference itself, which this doc's own Calibration policy says means the rule is wrong, not the component. **Two calibration gotchas the vendored reference surfaced, worth recording for future vendored-reference work:** it autoplays by default, rotating the fixture away from slide 1 mid-test — handled with `page.emulateMedia({ reducedMotion: "reduce" })`, the reference's own documented mechanism for forcing autoplay off; and it shows/hides one slide at a time (a `.active` toggle) rather than keeping all of them simultaneously ARIA-reachable, unlike this library's own always-in-DOM scroll-snap strip — a real engine difference, not a defect in either, so the oracle asserts each slide's `aria-label` attribute directly rather than assuming simultaneous `getByRole` visibility. **Not verifiable here, stated plainly:** touch-drag/swipe feel (this sandboxed environment has no way to test that honestly — native scroll-snap dragging via touch/trackpad is inherited from the browser) and raw wheel/trackpad gestures specifically (every Playwright test pages via button or keyboard, which round-trips through the same live signal the scroll-tracking bridge updates, but nothing drives a raw wheel/trackpad gesture distinctly from those paths — Playwright has no reliable, portable way to simulate one). Evidence: 31 Rust unit+SSR tests + 2 doctests, all 7 mechanical `check-*.sh` gates, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --tests --examples -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (291/0 primitives incl. this row's 31, 144/0 doctests incl. this row's 2, 31/0 preview), `cd preview && npx stylelint "src/**/*.css"` (auto-fixed 6 pre-existing property-order findings in this row's own new `style.css`, no rule changes), and rustdoc (`-Dwarnings --document-private-items`, plain and `--features web`) all clean. **Supersedes row 86's own "execution deferred" note and `component-backlog.md`'s prior "plan approved 2026-09-19, execution pending" status for Carousel** — see that file's own Carousel row for the deferrals restated in status form. **Fast-follows LANDED 2026-09-25 (round 8b, `bfcff32`, `4bd8816`, `59902a2`, `b557dfa`; was: in progress at the first round-8 docs pass, per this row's own now-superseded note above):** `loop` ships rewind-style — Previous at the first slide wraps to the last, Next at the last wraps to the first, the root-level arrows likewise, all RTL-aware via `Direction::resolve_horizontal`; `CarouselPrevious`/`Next` are never `disabled` once loop is on; no cloned slides, per invariant 5 in `dev-docs/research/carousel-overscroll-2026-09-23.md`; drag/wheel still rubber-band at the physical edges regardless. Autoplay ships as a behaviour-only `CarouselAutoplay` (`delay_ms`, `stop_on_interaction`, `stop_on_mouse_enter`, `default_playing`, mirroring `embla-carousel-autoplay`'s own prop shape) driven by `spawn` + `dioxus_sdk_time::sleep`, using `menu_sub.rs`'s own cancel-before-reschedule idiom; it stops at the last slide when not looping, hover pauses and resumes, focus pauses and deliberately does NOT self-resume, and a one-shot post-mount `prefers-reduced-motion` check forces it off entirely. `CarouselRotationControl` is a real button (no `aria-pressed`; its label toggles "Start/Stop automatic slide show") with its own focusin/focusout stop-propagation — added after a real bug found by execution: without it, clicking the control to start rotation immediately re-paused what it had just started. `aria-live` goes `off`/`polite` on `CarouselContent` only when autoplay is present. The tablist variant is `CarouselTabList`/`CarouselTab`, composing `crate::collection` directly rather than `Tabs` — `Tabs`'s own `TabContent` hides inactive panels and `TabTrigger` is manual-activation-only, both wrong for APG's tabbed-carousel pattern (tried first, reverted); `CarouselItem`'s role switches group→tabpanel once a tablist mounts. The APG tabbed example is vendored at the same pinned `7e4034b` every other reference page in this tree already uses (`playwright/oracle/reference/7e4034b/content/patterns/carousel/examples/carousel-2-tablist.html` plus its css/js). Evidence: Rust unit+SSR carousel tests 52 (was 37), `playwright/carousel.spec.ts` 85 (was 65), tier-1 APG oracle 23 (was 11), axe clean on the new `looping`/`looping_rtl`/`autoplay`/`tabs` demo variants. Scope cuts, stated plainly: `stop_on_interaction` is not wired to native drag/wheel interaction (only to explicit Previous/Next/tab activation); no dedicated RTL tablist demo; the `tabs` variant inherits the tab buttons' own padding reservation (cosmetic). See `component-backlog.md`'s Carousel row and `plan.md`'s round-8 status paragraph for the same update. **Fixed 2026-09-25 (lane `carousel-no-page-scroll`): "autoplay scrolls the main page" (owner report, live site).** The class: every paging path (Previous/Next, root arrow keys, `CarouselTab`, `CarouselApi::scroll_to`, each `CarouselAutoplay` tick, and the pointer-drag release settle) called `target.scrollIntoView()` on the newly-selected slide — `scrollIntoView` walks and scrolls *every* scrollable ancestor between the target and the viewport, including the document itself, so any of those six call sites could silently drag a reader's page back to the carousel from wherever they had scrolled to; `overflow: clip` on the viewport wrapper (this row's own round-8b addendum) does not stop this, since `scrollIntoView` was called on the slide, not that wrapper. The construction: both call sites replaced with a new `CAROUSEL_SCROLL_TO_JS`/inline `endDrag` helper that computes the physical `getBoundingClientRect()` delta between the target slide and the carousel's own scroller and calls `scroller.scrollBy({ left top: delta })` directly on the scroller — `scrollBy`, unlike `scrollIntoView`, only ever writes the element it is called on, so there is no ancestor for it to reach; RTL stays sign-flip-free (invariant 1, `carousel-overscroll-2026-09-23.md` §6) since the delta is physical either way. Two latent bugs surfaced and fixed by execution while building this, neither present in the `scrollIntoView` original: (a) `scrollBy({ behavior: 'smooth' })` on a `scroll-snap-type: mandatory` container truncates a multi-slide jump at the first intervening snap point (measured: a bare 672px request landed at 336px) — fixed by suspending `scroll-snap-type` for the scroll's duration and restoring it on `scrollend`/a timeout fallback, the same construction `CAROUSEL_DRAG_JS`'s own release settle already used, with the pending-restore handoff stashed on the scroller element (`__dxCancelSnapRestore`) since independent `document::eval` calls share no closure; (b) that same suspend/restore, applied naively, fired mid-drag too (`use_carousel_scroll_tracking` reports a changing `selected` continuously while dragging) and re-enabled mandatory snapping before the gesture ended, sending drags flying 2 slide-pitches past the release point — fixed by skipping the discrete-paging bridge entirely while the scroller carries `data-dragging`, deferring to the drag's own release settle. `primitives/src/carousel.rs`'s own `CAROUSEL_SCROLL_TO_JS` doc comment has the full account and the live measurements. Not touched: the wheel/trackpad bounce bridge (`CAROUSEL_WHEEL_BOUNCE_JS`) already never wrote the scroll position at all (THE RULE, §3), so it was never part of this bug. **Investigated same session, unrelated:** the owner's second, ambiguous "trackpad not working with loop" report, interpretation A (wheel/trackpad scrolling broken specifically under `loop`) — found NOT reproducible: `use_carousel_wheel_bounce` is wired identically regardless of `loop`, and a live repro (wheel-paging through the middle slides, then pushing past both physical ends) behaved identically on `looping` and `main` (mid-slide paging advanced the same way; both rubber-banded at both ends without wrapping). No code change made for this half; interpretation B ("swiping past the last slide should wrap") is a design decision explicitly left to the owner, out of this lane's scope. **Fixed 2026-09-26 (lane `carousel-a11y`): only visible slides reachable, per `dev-docs/research/carousel-loop-2026-09-25/loop-a11y-guidance.md` §7's contract and `loop-libraries.md`'s cross-library convergence.** Every `CarouselItem` outside the selected slide (or, for a multi-per-view layout, outside `CarouselContext::visible_range`, the `[start, end]` indices actually inside `CarouselContent`'s own viewport at rest) now renders `inert` — one attribute removing it from both Tab order and the accessibility tree, the construction every a11y-layer-having carousel library (Embla, Swiper, Splide, Slick) and the Chrome "make accessible carousels" recipe independently converge on. `visible_range` defaults to `(0, 0)`, matching `selected`'s own forced-`0`-until-registration value, so SSR/pre-hydration first render is deterministic (only slide 0 non-`inert`) without a special case. Focus safety: each `CarouselItem`'s own plain `onfocusin`/`onfocusout` tracks which slide holds focus (`CarouselContext::focused_item`); a new effect redirects focus to `CarouselContent`'s own always-focusable scroller the instant `selected` changes away from that slide, closing the HTML spec's own "an element becoming inert while it holds focus falls back to `<body>`" behavior — confirmed live this would otherwise happen (Tab into slide 1's own button, then ArrowRight, landed on `<body>` before this fix). **Two defects live verification caught that the unit/SSR suite alone missed:** (1) `dioxus-interpreter-js` 0.7.9's `set_attribute.ts` hardcodes which HTML attributes are boolean (`isBoolAttr`) and `inert` is not on that list, so a raw `inert: bool` field rendered `inert="false"` as a *present* attribute on the web target — every slide came back inert, including the selected one; fixed by typing it `Option<&'static str>` (`None`/omitted vs. `Some("true")`), which routes through Dioxus's `remove_attribute` mutation instead of that allowlist — the same construction `default_label`/`aria_controls` already use, and the fix for any future boolean HTML attribute this crate adds that isn't on that same hardcoded list, not just this one. (2) the first visible-range measurement raced `CarouselContent`'s own external stylesheet load (measured live: a `multiple`-variant demo read a 544px scroller box before its wrapper's `padding-inline` reservation applied, vs. 448px correct after), permanently over-widening the visible set by one slide for any carousel whose `default_value == 0` never scrolls on its own — fixed by moving all visible-range measurement into `use_carousel_scroll_tracking`'s own long-lived bridge and adding a `ResizeObserver` on the scroller, whose first callback establishes the initial range and whose later callbacks self-correct on any subsequent genuine size change. Also found and worked around: this session's installed Playwright build's own `getByRole`/`ariaSnapshot` do not treat `inert` as excluding a node at all (a minimal `<div role="group" inert>` still matched both), while Chrome's own CDP `Accessibility.queryAXTree` (the real platform tree) correctly omits it — new a11y-tree-exposure tests use the latter, scoped per demo frame via `DOM.querySelector`, since several variants share the same "k of N" label shape. Evidence: 6 new Rust unit/SSR tests (58, was 52), `playwright/carousel.spec.ts` 108 (was 96) + 12 new in a dedicated "only visible slides are reachable (inert)" block, tier-1 APG oracle unchanged at 23 — 131/131 red-then-green on a live `dx serve`; all 8 mechanical `check-*.sh` gates, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --tests --examples -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (466 primitives+preview, 160 doctests), `cd preview && npx stylelint "src/**/*.css"` all clean; SSG release build + `oracle/hydration-parity.spec.ts` + this row's own suite against the prerendered site on :8090/:8080 — 171/172, the only red being the pre-existing, red-by-design `selftest:unfixed:id` (unrelated to this change). Deferred, stated plainly: the "via autoplay" focus-redirect scenario the task asked for turned out unreachable by construction — `AutoplayContext::rotating` is gated off the instant `focus_within` becomes true (a plain Rust reactive effect, no JS round-trip), so autoplay cannot itself strand focus in an about-to-be-inert slide; a regression test instead confirms that existing pause-on-focus invariant still holds with slides now `inert`. **Landed 2026-09-26 (lane `carousel-virtual`): data-driven, virtualised content with a seamless loop, per the two approved research surveys (`dev-docs/research/carousel-loop-2026-09-25/*.md`, `carousel-overscroll-2026-09-23.md`).** New `CarouselVirtualContent<T>` composes inside the existing `Carousel` root in place of `CarouselContent`+`CarouselItem`s: `items: ReadSignal<Vec<T>>`, `render_item: Callback<(usize, T), Element>`, `radius` (default 2), `virtualize: Option<bool>` (auto-decides once `N > 2*radius+1`); publishes its count into `CarouselContext` exactly like `CarouselItem`'s own registration effect, so every existing consumer (Previous/Next, tablist/tab, autoplay, rotation control, root arrow keys, `use_carousel()`) works completely unchanged. Non-virtualised (small N, or `virtualize: Some(false)`): real per-data-index ids published into `item_ids`, so the existing built-in mount/selected paging effect drives it with zero bespoke code — loop stays today's rewind, unchanged. Virtualised+loop: an internal `anchor: isize` lets logical `position` run arbitrarily far past `[0, N)` (mapped to a data index only via `rem_euclid` for rendering/labels), so paging past the last data index slides physically forward instead of rewinding across every intervening slide; a `selected` change resolves to the shortest signed delta mod N (`shortest_signed_delta`, unit-tested standalone) — a ±1 step animates the existing scroller-only `CAROUSEL_SCROLL_TO_JS` helper to the already-rendered target position, anything else re-anchors instantly. The window re-renders keyed by `position` (surviving slides keep their DOM node), with a dedicated effect re-aligning the scroller to the new centre by one more instant `scrollBy` once a physical scroll settles — a **known, recorded deviation** from the bench prototype's `MutationObserver`-microtask correction (this lane's simpler plain-`use_effect` version was validated empirically via Playwright rAF-sampling instead, see below). SSR/first client render always renders the full list, true order, no window (mounted-gated), switching to the window only in a later, client-only effect — identical markup both times. Generalised every DOM-child-to-slide JS bridge in this module to read `data-index`/`data-position` off each slide rather than a child's array position (`CarouselItem` now renders both, equal to its own index) — the fix `loop-libraries.md`'s own cross-library finding calls for, and what lets the windowed content's repeating data indices (N < 2R+1) share the exact same tracking bridge as the plain children API. **Two real bugs found live against a running dx-serve server, not merely reasoned through, both now closed by construction rather than a timing patch:** unbounded anchor drift at mount (a settle's own report was being re-interpreted as a fresh navigation request against stale prior state, an unbounded feedback loop measured drifting continuously with zero user interaction) — fixed by skipping the paging effect whenever a new `intended` position-tracker already agrees with the incoming selected value; and a dropped step under back-to-back paging (autoplay ticks, or two clicks closer together than one settle's own latency) — the animate-vs-jump branch was measuring the wrong distance (step size from `intended`) instead of distance from `anchor` (the actually-rendered window), so a second step could target a `data-position` id with no element behind it yet and silently no-op, with the first step's own later settle then overwriting `intended` back down, erasing the second step outright; fixed by branching on distance from `anchor`. Preview: themed `CarouselVirtualContent` wrapper (`preview/src/components/carousel/component.rs`), two new demo variants (`virtual_loop` — 12 items, loop on, autoplay + dots; `virtual_many` — 200 items, loop off), one registration line, and a "Virtualised content" `docs.md` section (API, when to virtualise, the find-in-page/browse-mode trade-off). Evidence: 16 new Rust unit tests + 7 new SSR tests, 15 new `playwright/carousel.spec.ts` tests (all green) plus the full existing 147 carousel.spec.ts+tier1-oracle suite green, plus the repo's full baseline Playwright suite (1217 passed, 18 pre-existing skips, 0 failed) against a live `dx serve`; `cargo test --workspace` (700 across all suites), all 8 mechanical `check-*.sh` gates, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --tests --examples -- -D warnings`, `cd preview && npx stylelint "src/**/*.css"` all clean. **Deferred, stated plainly:** the bench's own `MutationObserver`-microtask re-centre correction was not ported 1:1 (a plain `use_effect` substitutes, validated empirically rather than by construction-equivalence — an upgrade path is named on `CarouselVirtualContent`'s own doc if a real engine ever shows the substitution isn't safe); `aria-controls` from a `CarouselTab` to its matching slide is absent in virtualised+loop mode (a data index can label more than one position at once, so there is no single DOM id to point at); no dedicated tier-1-oracle-style reference comparison for the virtualised/seamless-loop behaviour specifically, since no APG reference implementation virtualises or loops seamlessly to compare against (the existing tier-1 oracle suite, unmodified, stays green throughout). **Landed 2026-09-26 (lane `carousel-parity`, 8 commits `d410269`..`c300a8f`): shadcn-parity pass across 7 owner-approved items, per `shadcn-carousel-parity.md` and `shadcn-ui/ui@98a1fe6`.** (1) **Gap model**: real flex `gap` was additive to each slide's percentage `flex-basis`, so a "3 per view" layout showed ~2.5 slides — replaced with shadcn's own idiom, gap lives *inside* the slide (`padding-inline-start`/`padding-block-start` on the item, `margin-inline-start`/`margin-block-start: -<gap>` on the content), ported to logical properties for RTL correctness and driven by one `--dx-carousel-gap` custom property; no JS geometry formula needed changing, since every bridge measures live `getBoundingClientRect()` boxes. (2) **Sizes**: default is now whole-slide-per-view; multi-per-view and edge-peek are both opt-in via `--dx-carousel-per-view`/`--dx-carousel-peek`; the old `multiple` demo became the Sizes demo, peek got its own small demo. (3) New `align` option (`Start`/`Center`/`End`, default `Start`) on `Carousel`, threaded through paging, native-scroll tracking and drag-settle via one `anchorOf` formula duplicated into each independent `document::eval` bridge (this module's own established no-shared-JS convention), covered for per-view-2 and peek across all three values. (4) **Per-demo parity**: container sizing for sizes/vertical/api demos matches shadcn; orientation demo got correct height+basis; a new `api` demo shows a live "Slide x of y"; autoplay demo's caption now states it pauses on hover/focus rather than shadcn's stop-and-reset. Demo slides compose `Card`+`CardContent` everywhere except `main` (kept as a deliberate, documented exception — it protects a fragile box-model-matching fixture a pointer-drag test depends on; Card itself was not touched, per this lane's ownership boundary). (5) **Indicators**: `CarouselTabList`/`CarouselTab` renamed to `CarouselIndicators`/`CarouselIndicator` (no deprecated alias — pre-1.0, owner-approved); the old `tabs`+`indicators` demos merged into one Indicators demo with a CSS fade transition on the active dot (instant under `prefers-reduced-motion`); the custom dot-picker behaviour lives on as the `api` demo; `docs.md` states when to reach for `Tabs` instead. (6) **Loop config**: `CarouselVirtualContent`'s seamless loop is unaffected (already the only sensible behaviour there); for the children API, rewind-style wraparound is now an explicit opt-in new `LoopMode` (`Seamless` default / `Rewind`), formalised as `CarouselContext::loop_wraps() = loop_enabled && (loop_mode == Rewind \|\| virtualized_loop_active)` and a standalone-unit-tested `is_rewind_wrap(old, new, count)` boundary detector (documented ambiguous, by construction, at `count == 2`); a Rewind wrap is a genuine instant jump, not a smooth scroll-across — verified by sampling scroll position through the transition. The `looping`/`looping_rtl` demos are retired (`virtual_loop` is the loop demo); a `rewind` demo (+ RTL row) was added since it was cheap. (7) **Previous/Next disabled**: re-verified at both ends — already a real native `disabled` HTML attribute, not a data-attribute; no code change needed. **Two regressions live verification caught that reasoning alone missed:** the gap model's new content-box shift broke both rubber-band JS helpers' edge-clearance math (`axisSize()` was reading the gap-widened scroller's own `clientWidth`/`clientHeight` instead of the viewport wrapper's) — fixed by reading `el.parentElement`'s box in both `CAROUSEL_DRAG_JS` and `CAROUSEL_WHEEL_BOUNCE_JS`; and vertical orientation alone (never horizontal — margin collapsing is a block-axis-only phenomenon in every writing mode) lost its top clearance because `overflow: clip` on the viewport wrapper did not, in this Chromium build, establish a strong enough containment to stop the wrapper's block-axis margin from collapsing with its negatively-margined child — fixed with an explicit `display: flow-root` on both viewport-wrapper style strings, confirmed via an isolated `page.setContent()` margin-collapse experiment before and after. Evidence: Rust unit+SSR carousel tests 98 (was 74), `playwright/carousel.spec.ts` + tier-1 APG oracle 150/151 on a live `dx serve` (the 1 red is the pre-existing parallel-worker-contention flake this spec file's own comments already document as non-blocking, confirmed by an isolated `--workers=1` rerun passing), an SSG release build (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, no `--base-path`, per this row's own round-8/backlog-row-98/100 incident history) served on :8090 and :8080 reproduces the same 150/151 plus `oracle/hydration-parity.spec.ts` 40/41 (only the by-design red `selftest:unfixed:id`); all 8 mechanical `check-*.sh` gates, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --tests --examples -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace`, `cd preview && npx stylelint "src/**/*.css"` all clean. **Deferred, stated plainly:** container-width/height parity was only pursued where the parity research doc named a specific mismatch (sizes, vertical, api); other demos keep their pre-existing static widths. |
| 92 | New-component landings can leave `preview`'s `OUT_DIR` stale — `cargo clippy`/`test`/`doc` fail until `build.rs` is touched | Found 2026-09-20 (round 4 integration, Carousel landing onto a pre-existing `target` dir); recurs for any future new component landed onto a `target` dir that predates it — remedy known, no construction fix pending | S | **A real trap this integration hit, not a defect in Carousel or in any component's own code:** landing Carousel — this round's brand-new component — into an *existing* `target` dir (one that predated the carousel commits) made `cargo clippy`/`cargo test`/`cargo doc` all fail with `couldn't read .../out/carousel/description.txt` (or equivalent), even though `preview/src/components/carousel/**` was itself complete and correct. **Root cause, read directly from `preview/build.rs`'s own comment, not assumed:** the script deliberately does not watch `src/components` recursively — its own comment explains why (a blanket recursive watch forced a full rebuild on any component's `style.css` edit, compounding the same CSS-hot-reload defect row 80 already root-caused) — so it only ever emits `cargo:rerun-if-changed` for the exact markdown/`component.json` files it already found on some *prior* run; a component it has never seen is invisible to it until something else forces a rerun. **The comment's own "self-healing in practice" framing does not hold the way it reads:** it cites "adding a new component always also means editing `components/mod.rs`, a `.rs` file cargo already tracks directly" as the mechanism — but cargo recompiling `preview` because one of its `.rs` files changed (which it always does) is a different mechanism from cargo *rerunning the build script*, which is governed solely by the script's own declared `rerun-if-changed` list; `mod.rs` is not on that list, so editing it recompiles the crate against the SAME stale `OUT_DIR`, not a fresh one — the comment is not silently wrong (its very next clause already names the real remedy), just optimistically titled for what is actually a loud, blocking failure with a known manual fix, not literal self-healing. **Remedy:** `touch preview/build.rs` or `cargo clean -p preview`, either of which forces cargo to see the build script itself as needing a rerun. **Why none of round 4's three lanes hit this themselves:** each lane built in its own fresh `$CARGO_TARGET_DIR` per this round's own environment brief, so there was no pre-existing `OUT_DIR` to go stale — this trap only appears when an existing target dir predates the new component, exactly the shape of a later integration step, not a lane's own isolated build. **Not fixed here, by design:** the main loop is separately correcting `build.rs`'s own comment (rewording the "self-healing" framing, not this crate's actual behavior); this row exists so the next person who hits the identical `couldn't read .../out/<name>/description.txt` failure after landing a new component finds the remedy in one place rather than re-deriving it |
| 93 | Literal attribute beside an unmerged props spread with no typed field claiming it — the duplicate-attribute class row 85's own fix swept for but deliberately left unfixed | Found 2026-09-20 (round 4, `f2be1d7`'s own `..props.attributes`/`..attributes` sweep of `primitives/src/**` and `preview/src/components/**`); **awaiting the repo owner's go-ahead** — the proposed fix-by-construction (a new static `check-*.sh` gate) touches dozens of files across primitives and themed wrappers, so it is deliberately not bundled into this round's PR. **CLOSED 2026-09-25 (round 8)** — the gate (`scripts/check-attr-spread-collision.sh`) had already landed (`ed0ea61`, `f8c2dec`); this round burned its debt register down 788 → 1, see this row's own dated addendum below | S–M for the check script itself (mirrors the existing seven guards' shape — a source-level scan, no full parser needed once attribute/typed-field names are enumerable per component); separately, M–XL to fix every site the gate then flags, exact count unknown until it is written and run — at minimum the five named `id` instances below, plus an unenumerated "dozens" of `class`/`role`/`data-*`/`type`/`tabindex` sites | **The distinguishing factor is not "a literal attribute beside a spread" but "a literal attribute beside a spread where the props struct has no typed field claiming that name":** a typed field reconciles the collision before it ever reaches `attributes` — `popover.rs`'s `PopoverRoot` `id: root_id` is not an instance because `PopoverRootProps` has a typed `id` field; `context_menu.rs`'s identical root shape was already fixed by `b35d671`; `carousel.rs` (landed this round) has no `id`-literal-beside-spread shape anywhere. On that criterion, `f2be1d7`'s own sweep found **five more real, currently-unexercised `id` instances** (verified directly against source for this row): `alert_dialog.rs`'s `AlertDialogTitle`/`AlertDialogDescription` (`h2`/`p { id: ctx.labelledby` / `ctx.describedby, ..props.attributes }`; neither props struct has a typed `id`); `virtual_list.rs`'s container (`id: container_id`, plus `role`/`tabindex`, all beside `..attributes`; `VirtualListProps` has none of the three); `resizable.rs`'s `ResizablePanel` (`id: id()`, plus `data-panel`/`data-collapsed`, beside `..attributes`); `toast.rs`'s `ToastTitle`/`ToastDescription` (`id: ctx.label_id` / the toast's own `description_id`, beside `..props.attributes` — not this same file's `ToastRegionRendered`, which is already fixed: it explicitly strips any caller `id` from `attributes` before merging, the construction `dev-docs/conformance-harness.md`'s Rule 4 section cites as the fix pattern); and `drag_and_drop_list.rs`'s `DragAndDropInstructions` (a hardcoded `id: "dnd-instructions"` literal, beside `..props.attributes`). **None fails today only because no demo page exercises the collision yet** — precisely the state `3ba793b`'s own bug was in until the `top_layer` fixture's caller-supplied `id` exercised it (row 85's extended Notes above). Beyond those five, a wider version of the same shape — a literal `class:`/`role:`/`data-*`/`type:`/`tabindex:` beside an unmerged spread, no typed field claiming the name — recurs across dozens of themed wrappers in `preview/src/components/**` and several more primitives, including `Menubar`'s own outer root (`role`/`data-disabled`/`data-direction`/`tabindex`, confirmed against source), `calendar.rs`'s `CalendarGridRoot` (`role`/`dir`/`data-direction` — its own `id` is fine, typed), all five of `carousel.rs`'s components (`Carousel`/`CarouselContent`/`CarouselItem`/`CarouselPrevious`/`CarouselNext`, each mixing some of `role`/`data-orientation`/`data-direction`/`data-selected`/`tabindex`/`type` beside its own unmerged spread), `popover.rs`'s `PopoverRoot` `data-state` (its own `id` is likewise fine, typed), and `DropdownMenuContentRendered`'s `role`/`popover`. **This is a class needing a fix by construction, not a fourth reactive pass:** `b35d671` (5 sites), `5fc1439` (`label.rs`'s `class`), and now `f2be1d7` each caught only the instances some page happened to exercise — three successive manual sweeps, three different survivors, each found only by whichever demo page happened to get built and exercised first. **Proposed construction:** a new `scripts/check-*.sh` static gate flagging a literal attribute name rendered beside a raw props spread where no typed field of that name exists, joining the seven guards `CLAUDE.md` already lists (`check-preview-composition.sh`, `check-cfg-axis.sh`, `check-dx-class-prefix.sh`, `check-css-literals.sh`, `check-hooks-in-closures.sh`, `check-self-subscribing-effects.sh`, `check-css-logical-properties.sh`) — not attempted this round, per the Trigger column, since it touches dozens of files across `primitives/src/**` and `preview/src/components/**` rather than the handful this round's own PR already carries  **ROOT CAUSE, 2026-09-20 (lane `deconstruct-attr-dup`, read-only; every load-bearing claim below re-verified in the main loop against the vendored crate sources, not taken on the lane's word):** the class lives two layers below where the three reactive passes were patching. `dioxus-core`'s VNode data model defines a dynamic-attribute slot as an ordered, concatenated `Box<[Attribute]>` with no per-name uniqueness invariant — `dioxus-core-0.7.9/src/nodes.rs:57-88`'s own doc comment states the contract verbatim (`[static named attributes, remaining dynamically named attributes]`, i.e. literal first, spread second, duplicates permitted) — and the one mount path every renderer funnels through (`diff/node.rs:822-825`) replays every entry with an unconditional `write_attribute`, no dedup, for every renderer, always. Everything else follows from that single absent step: `rsx!` is *structurally* incapable of fixing it (a spread's runtime contents are unknowable at expansion time, so `dioxus-rsx-0.7.9/src/element.rs:228-301`'s own `Element::merge_attributes` can only merge literal-vs-literal pairs it sees in the token stream, never literal-vs-spread), and `dioxus-ssr-0.7.9/src/renderer.rs:146-159` special-cases exactly three things (`dangerous_inner_html`, `namespace == Some("style")`, and `BOOL_ATTRS`) and writes everything else straight through — a `grep '"class"' dioxus-ssr-0.7.9/src/` returns zero hits, so there is no class-joining anywhere. This also closes `dev-docs/issues/drafts/dioxus-ssr-duplicate-attributes.md`'s own unchecked "confirm the exact function/file before filing" TODO: it is `render_template`'s `Segment::Attr` arm plus `write_attribute` (`renderer.rs:146-159`/`:482-498`), now traced by source rather than by observation. **New finding that raises the severity:** hydration does NOT repair the SSR'd value either — `dioxus-web-0.7.9/src/mutations.rs:181` gates `set_attribute` behind `skip_mutations()`, which is `true` throughout hydration (`lib.rs:100`, `hydration/hydrate.rs:162`), and `hydrate.ts` never touches attributes at all. Chaining that with the slot ordering, the two renderer modes resolve a collision to **opposite values**: local non-hydrating `dx serve --web` calls `setAttribute` literal-then-spread so the *caller's* value wins, while production SSG+hydrate leaves the browser's HTML-parse resolution (first occurrence = the *library's literal*) in place permanently. So a consumer writing `Alert { class: "mine" }` sees their class applied in dev and silently dropped in production. That is the full reason three separate authors each missed this locally — not "no demo page happened to collide" but "the dev loop's own renderer mode structurally cannot show it"; only the SSG-backed hydration-parity oracle can. **Size of the class:** a guard prototype (`$S/round4/deconstruct/check-attr-spread-collision.py`, iterated live with two real scanner bugs found and fixed mid-run — multi-line fn signatures, and non-`pub` Props structs — each confirmed to have caused a genuine false positive) found **623 sites across 66 files** (543 primitives / 80 preview): `role` 92, `data-state` 48, `data-disabled` 48, `tabindex` 36, `class` 29, `aria_label` 24, `data-direction` 23, `type` 19, `data-slot` 18, … `id` 8 (the five named above plus the three `b35d671`/`f2be1d7` already fixed). CSS shorthands are excluded via a 407-ident denylist extracted mechanically from this lockfile's own `dioxus-html-0.7.9/src/attribute_groups.rs` — those genuinely do fold into one `style="…"`. Disclosed false-negative: single-line element bodies are invisible to the indentation heuristic, so 623 is a floor, not a ceiling. **Recommendation put to the repo owner 2026-09-20, awaiting the call:** harden the prototype into a `syn`-based `scripts/check-*.sh` gate **paired with** extending `hydration-parity.spec.ts` to synthesize the collision per component rather than waiting for an incidental demo (the static check catches authoring, the oracle catches the dev-vs-prod divergence); file the already-drafted upstream issue regardless, now that it has the missing file:line trace; treat the API-level construction (make post-merge the only expressible form for a spread) as the follow-up once the hardened guard's output sizes the cleanup. **What none of that subsumes:** a typed field per name cannot reach the 223 string-literal `data-*`/`aria-*` findings at all (Rust field names cannot contain hyphens), and the `5fc1439`-shaped entry point (an ad-hoc `#[props(extends)]` key plus a separately-threaded `attributes:` forwarded to a *child* component, not a same-element `..spread`) is outside the prototype's literal-scan pattern. Full deconstruction, 642 lines with file:line evidence: `$S/round4/deconstruct/ROOT-CAUSE.md`. **CLOSED 2026-09-25 (round 8):** the gate itself had already landed as a pre-commit check (`ed0ea61` builds `scripts/check-attr-spread-collision.sh` and its `syn`/`dioxus-rsx` analyzer; `f8c2dec` wires it in alongside the other guards) — what this round did was burn the resulting debt register down from **788 sites (687 primary + 101 component-forward, per `dev-docs/issues/duplicate-attribute-findings.md`'s own count at that time) to 1**, across four disjoint lanes: overlay/menu primitives (216 sites), the remaining primitives (310 primary + 4 component-forward), preview wrappers (122 primary + 101 component-forward), and carousel (21, landed alongside row 102's own carousel work); chart's own count came down separately, via stage 2's struct-update construction (row 90's own (a) above) rather than a dedicated attr-spread lane. **One construction used everywhere, not one per component:** `attributes!()` + `merge_attributes(vec![defaults, caller, owned])`, where `owned` (component-managed state, `role`/`aria-*` wiring, managed `tabindex`, `popover`, positioning `style`, an aria-referenced `id`) always wins — preserving each component's pre-fix production behaviour exactly, since those are the values the component itself was relying on the browser's first-wins parse behaviour to keep; `defaults` (a base `class` that still concatenates with the caller's regardless of order, `data-slot`, a default `aria-label` text, `type="button"`) are caller-overridable; event handlers are never merged by this construction at all. **The single remaining entry in the debt register** is `PopoverTrigger`'s `id` — verified a false positive by direct read: the caller-supplied attribute vec is filtered of any `id` before the merge (`popover.rs`, around line 837), so there is no actual collision left to fix. Baselines were regenerated three times over the round (`chore(scripts): lock in …` commits), once per major sweep. **Evidence:** all 12 `CLAUDE.md` gates green; a full Chromium Playwright run at `51a96a6` was 1015 passed / 31 failed / 18 skipped, with every one of the 31 settled by execution as either pre-existing on `f103bbd`, a CPU-contention flake that passes in isolation, or by design — zero regressions attributable to this round's own changes; an SSG release build showed zero duplicated attribute names across all 73 prerendered pages (an ad-hoc tokenizer scan, not a sampled one); `hydration-parity.spec.ts` against that same SSG build was 40 passed / 1 failed, where the one failure is the permanent, deliberately-red-by-design `selftest:unfixed:id` case — every real Rule 4c collision case is green. **A side effect worth recording for anyone editing tests going forward:** `merge_attributes` sorts its output by attribute name, so rendered attribute *order* changed wherever this construction was applied — this broke two pre-existing string-slicing tests that located content by slicing rendered HTML from a specific attribute-string match (a `calendar.rs` unit test that sliced starting from `aria-label="…"`, and chart's `tooltip.rs`'s own `tooltip_fragment()` helper, which sliced from a literal match instead of the enclosing tag's own `<`); both were re-anchored on the tag's start rather than on any specific attribute, and `dev-docs/issues/duplicate-attribute-findings.md` carries its own dated status header noting the gate's closure. See `backlog.md` row 108 (new, added this round) for the proposed by-construction fix to this test-fragility class itself, not built this round. |
| 94 | Demo wrappers that set `max-width` + `margin: 0 auto` never reach that width — they shrink-wrap instead, so every affected demo renders narrower than its author intended | Found 2026-09-20 while bringing Carousel to shadcn visual parity (round 5). Surfaced by a parity lane, but its proposed cause and fix were both wrong and are recorded here so they are not retried: it blamed `.dx-component-preview-frame`'s `align-items: center` and proposed `align-items: stretch` | S per wrapper (add `width: 100%`); **all DONE 2026-09-26 (`650db75`)**: the carousel's five in round 5, the other 12 in the audit batch, guarded by `check-demo-wrapper-width.sh` | **2026-09-26 (audit batch): FIXED** in `650db75` -- measured on every page: 12 affected (chart_tooltip ×9 via one shared rule, combobox disabled/controlled/dynamic), each now exactly its max-width; `form`/`toolbar` fixed too (latent: content happens to fill the cap today). Guarded by construction: new `scripts/check-demo-wrapper-width.sh` (in CLAUDE.md's gate list), zero false positives today; limits stated in its header. **The real mechanism, measured rather than reasoned:** `.dx-component-preview-frame` (`preview/assets/main.css`) is `display: flex; flex-direction: column`, so its cross axis is horizontal. A wrapper's own `margin: 0 auto` makes BOTH cross-axis margins `auto`, and per flexbox auto cross-axis margins take precedence over `align-self: stretch` — so the item's cross size stays `auto` and it shrink-wraps its content. **`align-items: stretch` therefore does not fix it**: measured on the carousel page, the five wrappers stayed at 176/250/137/22/137px both before and after injecting that rule. An explicit `width: 100%` does fix it — the same five went to 320/448/320/192/320px, each exactly its own `max-width`. **And `align-items: stretch` would have been actively harmful:** probing all 66 component pages (123 preview frames) with that rule injected widened **80 direct children that have no `max-width` at all**, e.g. `alert_dialog`'s bare `<button>` 159px -> 682px, `button`'s demos 113px -> 682px, `chart`'s `.dx-chart` 300px -> 682px. Evidence: `$S/round5/align-probe.json` plus `align-probe.js`/`fix-probe.js` beside it. **Scope of the class:** 24 direct children across the 66 pages carry a `max-width` and are candidates; carousel's five are fixed (each variant's wrapper now leads with `width: 100%`, with the mechanism documented in `variants/main/mod.rs` so it is not "cleaned up" later as redundant). The other ~19 (`combobox` confirmed: three wrappers at 200/200/236px against a 320px `max-width`) were NOT fixed as of round 5 (fixed 2026-09-26, `650db75`, see the first sentence) — each needed its own author to confirm the intended width is really the `max-width` value rather than the shrink-wrapped one that has been shipping. **Why this matters beyond tidiness:** the carousel's main demo was rendering at 176px against shadcn's 320px, which was a larger visual difference from the reference than the Previous/Next placement this round set out to fix. |
| 95 | A debug SSG build serves fine but traps at runtime, so interaction specs run against it fail like real regressions | Hit 2026-09-20 (round 5) running `carousel.spec.ts` against a local static serve of `dx build --ssg` output | DONE — warning added directly to the recipe in `dev-docs/conformance-harness.md`, where it is actually read | The documented local SSG recipe omits `--release`, and the resulting debug artifact prerenders correct markup (so `hydration-parity.spec.ts` and any static-geometry check are happy) but its wasm traps the moment the app does anything: `RuntimeError: memory access out of bounds` in the console, plus a failed `ws://127.0.0.1:8080/_dioxus?build_id=0` handshake because a debug build still expects a `dx serve` hot-reload socket. HTTP 200, page renders, clicks do nothing. **13 tests across `carousel.spec.ts` and `oracle/tier1-apg/carousel.spec.ts` failed on known-good code** — paging, keyboard, drag — while every geometry assertion in the same run passed, which is exactly the pattern that makes it look like a targeted regression rather than an environment fault. Same family as row 92 (stale `OUT_DIR`) and the `--base-path`-served-at-root trap: an artifact that returns 200 is not evidence that the artifact is the one you meant to test. |
| 96 | `scroll-snap-stop: always` caps a browser-driven scroll but NOT this crate's own pointer-drag release | Landed 2026-09-20 (round 5) after the user chose a CSS-only no-skip guarantee over matching embla's velocity model | DONE as far as it goes; the residual gap is deliberate, not outstanding work | **2026-09-26 (owner re-evaluating):** the owner is re-evaluating this row's non-loop edge-overscroll framing in the bench (`dev-docs/research/carousel-overscroll-bench.html`; the live bench artifact is newer than the committed 2026-09-23 write-up) — approach E ("native room": real scroll room past the ends that the browser itself scrolls into, with a compensating transform following WebKit's own stretch curve, and the browser's own snap releasing at lift) is under consideration to replace B3 and the owner said it "feels right"; a clip fix for E is being chosen. This row's drag-release/flick framing is therefore under re-evaluation, not settled — see row 102's matching addendum. The user's call was explicit: "the exact velocity is not important, since we use native css and there is some difference expected, we can only set no overscroll of slide css property if the browser supports it", and then "lets not go overboard with parity where it does not matter and where the basis is different". So `scroll-snap-stop: always` is set on every slide behind `@supports`, and no JS velocity model was built. **What it does cover, measured twice independently:** a browser-animated scroll is capped to one slide pitch — `scrollBy({left: 1000, behavior: 'smooth'})` travels 1008px (3.00 slides) without the property and exactly 336px (1.00 slide) with it, at a 333px pitch. **What it does not cover:** our own drag release. The drag path holds `scroll-snap-type: none` for the whole gesture (so per-move instant `scrollBy` calls don't fight snapping), so there is no in-progress scrolling operation for the property to constrain; restoring the type afterwards is a static re-evaluation of an already-stopped position, and the settle lands on whichever slide is numerically nearest — a 2.5-pitch drag settles at 2 pitches, identical to the property being absent. A same-tick zero-distance nudge and a 1px smooth nudge after restoring the type were both tried and neither routed the correction through the constrained path. **Also not covered:** the short-fast-flick symptom that originally motivated the lane (a quick flick settling back to the origin instead of advancing) is inherently velocity-shaped — no CSS property can observe release speed, so this is unreachable CSS-only by construction. Closing either gap means a JS distance-or-speed threshold, i.e. the velocity model under another name, which the user declined. Reopen only if the user asks for it.  **CORRECTION, same day: the first implementation of this was inert and the row above described an intent, not a shipped behaviour.** It set the property via a scoped `<style>` tag rendered from `CarouselContent`. Against a real release SSG build, `getComputedStyle(slide).scrollSnapStop` read `normal` on every slide and a 1000px smooth `scrollBy` travelled 1008px (3.03 slides) — completely uncapped. The `<style>` element was present with the correct id, and the id matched the live element pre- and post-hydration, but `styleElement.sheet.cssRules.length` was **0**: Dioxus emits hydration marker comments *inside* the element's text (`<!--node-id413-->@supports (…) { … }<!--#-->`), and in CSS `<!--`/`-->` are CDO/CDC tokens, so the ident between them begins a qualified rule whose prelude swallows the real `@supports` rule and the browser drops the lot. Injecting the byte-identical CSS without markers gives `cssRules.length === 1` and computes `always` immediately. It would have worked in pure CSR, where no markers are emitted — which is why it looked correct in a dev loop. **Fixed** by declaring `scroll-snap-stop:always` inline on `CarouselItem` beside its existing `scroll-snap-align:start`; the `@supports` guard was deleted rather than relocated, because an unrecognised property in a `style="…"` attribute is dropped by the parser by definition, which satisfies the user's "if the browser supports it" requirement without a stylesheet at all. Verified live post-hydration: computed `always` on all five slides, and 1000px / 2.5-pitch / 3-pitch scrolls all cap to 336px. **The test that shipped with the broken version was a false green:** it scrolled 2.5 pitches and asserted slide 2, which is the nearest snap point with or without the property; it passed 54/54 against an inert build. Replaced with a 3-pitch scroll, where capped (slide 2) and uncapped (slide 4) diverge. The two limitations recorded above (drag release uncovered; short-fast-flick velocity-shaped) remain true and unchanged.  **UPDATE 2026-09-21, round 6.** The drag release now animates: it is routed through the same `scrollIntoView` paging path `CarouselPrevious`/`CarouselNext` use, and the `scroll-snap-type` restore is deferred until that scroll finishes (`scrollend`, with a timeout fallback), with a fresh `pointerdown` cancelling any pending restore so it cannot fire mid-gesture. Measured: 18 samples across a release, **11 distinct `scrollLeft` values** from 228 to 336, against exactly 2 before. **The cap-to-one-slide gap persists, for a restated reason.** It is no longer "restoring snap-type is a static re-evaluation"; it is that the release picks the nearest slide by `getBoundingClientRect` geometry and that search has no "cap from the gesture's origin" concept, so a long drag can still land several slides away. Closing it still means a JS distance-or-speed threshold, i.e. the velocity model the owner declined, so it stays open by choice. The short-fast-flick case is likewise unchanged and remains unreachable from CSS. Note the release now performs one `getBoundingClientRect` read per gesture — deliberately not a `scrollLeft` read, which is the RTL hazard this file avoids everywhere, and outside the pointer-move hot path. |
| 97 | A second, un-fragmented rsx sibling renders on the first pass and is silently dropped by the next one | Found 2026-09-20 (round 5) adding a scoped `<style>` tag beside `CarouselContent`'s track | DONE for this instance (explicit `Fragment`); the class is NOT swept | The `<style>` tag rendered correctly in SSR markup and then vanished on the second render, which in a real mount happens almost immediately via this component's own effects — so the served HTML looked right while `getComputedStyle` reported `scroll-snap-stop: normal` in the live browser. Reading the SSR output alone would never have caught it; only a live computed-style check did. Fixed by wrapping the siblings in an explicit `Fragment`, mirroring the pattern `drag_and_drop_list.rs` already uses, with two unit tests pinning it (present on first render, still present after `render_immediate` flushes effects). **Not swept:** no audit was done for other multi-sibling rsx returns in `primitives/src/**` that could have the same shape. Worth a guard script if a second instance turns up — per CLAUDE.md, two occurrences would make it a class.  **Update, same day: this instance no longer exists** — the `<style>` tag it was keeping alive turned out to be unparseable anyway (row 96) and was deleted along with the `Fragment` that wrapped it. The underlying Dioxus behaviour the row describes is still real and still worth knowing, so the row stays: a second, un-fragmented rsx sibling renders on the first pass and is dropped by the next. Note the sequence though — the `Fragment` fix was correct *and* insufficient, because it made the element survive while the CSS inside it was still being discarded by the parser. Two different failures with the same visible symptom (`scroll-snap-stop` computing `normal`), and fixing the first one made the second one look fixed too. A live `getComputedStyle` read distinguishes them; the served HTML does not. |
| 98 | `dx build --ssg` fails with `No such file or directory (os error 2)` when `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` is a relative path | Hit 2026-09-20 (round 5) by a lane following this repo's own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target-lane-<name>` convention | Workaround is one word: make the path absolute. ~20 min lost across two failed builds before it was traced | Traced into `dioxus-cli-0.7.9/src/build/builder.rs`'s `pre_render_static_routes`: it runs `Command::new(&server_exe).current_dir(server_exe.parent().unwrap())` while `server_exe` is still a **relative** `PathBuf`. `current_dir` chdirs the child before exec, so the still-relative program path is then resolved against the new cwd rather than the original one, and the binary can never find itself. **This is a direct hazard for `CLAUDE.md`'s own lane discipline**, which tells every lane to set an isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and shows it as a bare relative name — any lane that then does an SSG live-check walks into this. Passing the same directory as an absolute path fixes it with no rebuild cost, since the relative-path attempt has already populated the cache. Upstream-reportable: the fix is to canonicalise `server_exe` before setting `current_dir`. |
| 99 | `anchor_name_style` set as a bare literal beside a spread — a duplicate-`style` class across the anchored-trigger family, and a second, quieter bug at the sites that looked safe | Found 2026-09-21 by Rule 4c on its first run (`NavigationMenuTrigger`), then widened to the whole family before fixing | DONE — all 9 sites now route through one construction | **Two distinct defects, and the second is the one that would have been missed.** (a) Four sites set `style: anchor_name_style(...)` as a bare literal on an element that also spread caller attributes — `hover_card.rs`, `menubar.rs`, `navbar.rs`, `navigation_menu.rs` — so a caller supplying `style` produced two `style` attributes. `navbar.rs`'s own comment claimed "Merged (caller-wins, deduped)", which is true for its `id` and false for its `style`; the comment is why this looked like a single-site bug. (b) The five sites that were safe from the duplicate (`context_menu.rs` sub, `dropdown_menu.rs` ×2, `popover.rs`, `tooltip.rs`) were silently **losing the anchor binding** whenever a caller supplied `style`, because `merge_attributes` folds only `class` (`join_class`) and replaces every other name outright, later-list-wins. That failure is invisible: no duplicate attribute is emitted, so no oracle can see it. **This is also why the obvious fix was wrong.** The initial brief proposed moving the literal into the merged base list, mirroring `TooltipTrigger` — which would have fixed (a) at the four sites while giving them (b), a worse trade, since (b) fails every time a caller sets `style` and leaves nothing to detect. The lane established the replace-not-fold behaviour with a new permanent test (`style_attribute_is_overwritten_not_folded`) before proceeding, and built `top_layer::anchored_trigger_attributes` instead — the trigger-side mirror of the existing, proven `anchored_content_attributes`, using `fold_style_attributes` so the anchor name is always kept and the caller's declarations still apply after it. Applied at all nine sites, including retrofitting the five that were only affected by (b). Evidence: static analyzer agreed with a full source read on which four were acutely defective; 7 new synthesized `style` cases in the Rule 4c oracle; full hydration-parity run 40 passed / 1 failed (the deliberate `selftest:unfixed:id`); an independent quote-aware re-tokenisation found 0 duplicate-attribute tags across 93,175 start tags over the home page and all 65 component pages; row 93's guard baseline shrank by exactly 4. |

| 102 | Port the carousel edge rubber-band into `primitives/` as mode B3 (edge transform) — **LANDED 2026-09-25 (round 8), then SUPERSEDED 2026-09-28 by mode D**; was: the component still behaves as mode A (clamped) | **LANDED 2026-09-25 (round 8), SUPERSEDED 2026-09-28 by mode D (`863f8ac`)** -- Owner reported twice that you cannot drag past slide 1 or the last slide and get a rubber-band; five mechanisms benched side by side 2026-09-22/23. **LANDED 2026-09-25 (round 8, `188e50b`, `c59dc8b`, `51a96a6`)** | M (done: the port landed, then mode D replaced it) | **2026-09-28: SUPERSEDED by mode D** (`863f8ac`) -- after the owner benched approaches D and E on real hardware (bench revs 33-40, Firefox 156, MacBook trackpad), D ("platform-aware band") was chosen and replaces B3's wheel path in `primitives/src/carousel.rs` (`CAROUSEL_WHEEL_BAND_JS`). The owner's tuned knob values became named constants. Differences from the bench, all approved: axis gating (vertical page scroll over a horizontal carousel never draws a band), notched wheels release after 140ms of silence, vertical orientation and RTL, no band on the seamless virtual loop or under reduced motion, telemetry only behind `localStorage["dx-carousel-debug"]="1"`. Verified by 21 rewritten tests in `carousel.spec.ts` (13 fail on B3) and replays of 9 of the owner's real recorded bursts plus 2 synthetic ones (`playwright/carousel-wheel-replay.spec.ts`; the same recordings also pass against the bench's own D). **Open:** Safari (and possibly Firefox on some platforms) may bounce a scroll container that is at rest at its edge, stacking with D's band (double bounce); needs a real-device check (see the carousel's docs.md). The pointer-drag rubber-band is unchanged. **2026-09-26 (owner re-evaluating):** see row 96's matching addendum — approach E ("native room", benched in `dev-docs/research/carousel-overscroll-bench.html`, whose live artifact is newer than the committed write-up) is under consideration to replace this row's own B3 mechanism, per the owner's "feels right"; a clip fix for E is being chosen, and row 112 now depends on this decision. **Design closed; implementation LANDED 2026-09-25 (below), later superseded by mode D.** Full write-up in [`dev-docs/research/carousel-overscroll-2026-09-23.md`](./research/carousel-overscroll-2026-09-23.md), with the bench checked in beside it. Root cause of every glitch across ~20 bench revisions is ONE thing: during a wheel/trackpad gesture the scroll position has **three would-be writers** — the compositor's momentum animation (where `preventDefault` is only advisory), the snap engine on its own schedule, and our code — and two of the three are neither observable nor sequenceable from JS. The negative evidence is decisive: the *mouse-drag* path stayed clean for 20+ revisions untouched, because a mouse drag has no native scrolling and our code is then the only writer. **THE RULE:** where the browser is already the scroller, never write the scroll position and never disable snapping — express the overdrag as a transform only; where there is no native scrolling, owning it is safe. Nuance for the port: the contest exists only while momentum is live, so a spring-back ease may own the offset once a burst is spent — detecting that end accurately is the remaining difficulty. **B3 is the choice and the reason is arithmetic, not taste:** B2 (damped margin) cannot resist, because damping means slowing the scroller, which means owning its position, which is exactly the fight the rule removes — with the fight gone its room fills 1:1 with raw travel, measured as a 25px/event gesture consuming the whole 260px room while 145 and 121px/event gestures at the other edge produced 3px and 0px. B3 has no wall: depth is bounded by the platform curve's asymptote (`L = track width / 0.55` = 582px on a 320px track) instead of by a `padding` value. Mode C felt closest to native and is subsumed by B3 — same mechanism on the track rather than the root, which is what dissolves C's z-index problem. Eight binding port invariants are listed in the research doc §6, each one the generalisation of a named bench regression (never `scrollLeft`; edge test synchronous from layout gaps; no smooth `scrollIntoView` for spring-back; never restart a spring-back in flight; no spacer children; suspend `scroll-snap-type` once per gesture; never change measurement and timing in the same revision; no pixel threshold below the device quantum). **LANDED 2026-09-25 (round 8):** ported as mode B3, per the rule above. The pointer-drag path keeps owning `scrollBy` (there is no native scrolling to contest there: it requests the full delta and treats any refusal as overdrag). A new, separate wheel/trackpad bridge, `use_carousel_wheel_bounce`, never calls `scrollBy`/`scrollLeft`/`scrollTop`/`preventDefault`/touches `scroll-snap-type` — edge detection is synchronous from `getBoundingClientRect` gaps, and overdrag is expressed only as a transform on `CarouselContent` itself (not the root — this is what dissolves mode C's z-index problem, per the research doc). Both paths write `style.transform` via `eval` outside the vdom, so hydration parity is unaffected; `prefers-reduced-motion` disables the bounce entirely; invariant 4 is enforced via a token counter (`bounceToken`) rather than the bench's own boolean flag. Tests were written red-first: 7 of 8 new tests failed on the pre-port code; after the port, `playwright/carousel.spec.ts` is 65/65 and the tier-1 APG oracle is 11/11. **Test-methodology finding worth keeping on record:** `page.mouse.wheel(0, deltaY)` does not scroll a horizontal scroller in headless Chromium at all — `deltaX` does; a test asserting wheel-driven behaviour on a horizontal carousel needs the X axis regardless of how a real trackpad gesture would be expressed. **Open, stated plainly, not silently dropped [2026-10-05: the feel-test happened for mode D -- the owner benched D and E on real hardware and chose D -- so only the Safari double-bounce real-device check from the first note stays open]:** the owner still needed to feel-test this on real hardware (mouse and trackpad, both axes, both edges) — synthetic wheel events cannot reproduce momentum, so this is not something Playwright can close out; the cross-browser rubber-band constant used is WebKit's own curve (row 103's measurement), unverified against Chromium's or Gecko's actual constants; arrow-paging while a bounce is already in flight is unexercised by any test; and there is no extra clipping wrapper around `CarouselContent` (it self-clips), which still wants a real-device visual check. **ADDENDUM 2026-09-25 (lane `carousel-rb-clip`), both flagged risks above materialized on the owner's real hardware, exactly as this row's own "still wants a real-device check"/"unverified against Chromium's or Gecko's" language warned they might.** (1) The "no extra clipping wrapper — `CarouselContent` self-clips" claim was wrong: an element's `overflow` clips relative to its OWN box, and a `transform` moves that box (clip region included) as one unit, so translating the scroller moved its clip boundary right along with it — a trackpad overdrag pushed the whole visible slide track ~750px past the carousel, unclipped, overlapping the rest of the page. Fixed by giving `CarouselContent` a stationary, presentational `div[data-slot="carousel-viewport"]` wrapper (`overflow: clip`, inline in the primitive itself, not `hidden` — a `hidden` box is still a scroll container, which would make the paging path's `scrollIntoView` scroll the wrapper too and permanently offset the content); both bridges now translate the scroller inside a clip boundary that never moves. (2) The port's own wheel-path curve constant was mis-transcribed: `L·x/(x+L)` with `L=d/c` is NOT algebraically identical to Apple's `x·c·d/(d+c·x)` as this row's own §4 source table claimed — they differ by a factor of `c` throughout (slope 1 vs `c` at `x=0`; asymptote `d/c`≈1.8·`d` vs `d`), so the shipped curve was roughly twice as sensitive and reached roughly twice the depth of the curve it was meant to port. Fixed in `CAROUSEL_WHEEL_BOUNCE_JS` only (`CAROUSEL_DRAG_JS`'s pointer-drag curve is untouched — the owner's report was trackpad-specific, and "mouse drag feels natural" as shipped). Both fixes and the corrected algebra are recorded in `dev-docs/research/carousel-overscroll-2026-09-23.md` §9's own dated entries. A separate, still-open concern from the same report — trackpad momentum overshoot needing damping beyond the curve fix — is deliberately NOT part of this addendum; the owner is deciding between that heuristic and a different construction, so it is being tracked and implemented separately rather than bundled in here. |
| 103 | Native sub-scroller rubber-band is real but **composited**, so it is invisible to `getBoundingClientRect`/`scrollLeft` — an instrumented observer records nothing and reads as "no bounce" | Measured 2026-09-23 with a script-free scroll-snap control added to the bench; the owner corrected two wrong conclusions I drew from its silence | N/A — a measurement limit to know, not work to schedule | I reported first "leave the wheel to the platform, macOS does this natively" (wrong — macOS gives elasticity to the *document*, not to an arbitrary sub-scroller) and then "there is no native rubber-band here at all" (also wrong). The owner settled it: *"there is an end rubber-band if there is still momentum coming from slide 2 to slide 1 and over, even if there is none from slide 1 to end."* The bounce exists when momentum arrives at an edge; it just never reaches a JS-readable layer. **The consequence is constructive:** it cannot be *measured* from inside the page, but it can be *modelled*, because the WebKit curve is published — `b(x) = (1 - 1/(x*c/d + 1))*d` with `c ~= 0.55`, algebraically identical to `L*x/(x+L)` with `L = d/c`. Other platform numbers from the same control, all reusable: rest after the last wheel event **64-78ms** typical (outliers 210/526/609ms on deliberately gentle gestures), device delta quantum **1px**, momentum decay **~0.96x per event**. All from one engine on one machine — Chromium and Gecko have their own curves, and a wrong constant degrades gracefully (stiffer or looser band) rather than breaking. |
| 104 | Instrumentation that keys off the wrong event, or records a threshold after it is reset, lies exactly where the bug is | Two telemetry defects found 2026-09-22 (bench v22) while chasing a B2 glitch that the data appeared to show nothing about | Done for the bench; worth remembering as a class | (a) Burst tracking keyed off **pad installation** rather than off the gesture, so a 49-second driving session recorded exactly **one** B2 burst — blind precisely in the region under investigation. (b) `releaseThreshold` was recorded **after** the peak it derives from had been zeroed, so every burst reported a flat `1.6` regardless of what was actually used. Both were fixed and the session re-driven. The general shape: instrumentation is code, it can be wrong, and a quiet dataset is evidence of nothing until the recorder itself has been checked against a case whose answer is already known. Related and separately instructive: the owner flagged *"1.6px floor might be wrong"* before any data did — it sat **below** the device's own 1px quantum, so it could never fire. It is now `deviceMinDelta * 2`, learned from the stream, with 1.6 as a fallback only until the device speaks. |

| 108 | Tests that locate rendered content by slicing an HTML string from a specific attribute-string match break when attribute order changes — the same class showed up twice this round (`calendar.rs`'s unit test, chart's `tooltip.rs::tooltip_fragment()`), both caused by row 93's `merge_attributes` construction sorting its output by attribute name | Found 2026-09-25 (round 8) while landing row 93; two occurrences in one round makes it a class per `CLAUDE.md`'s own rule, not two coincidences | **BUILT 2026-09-26 (`dab751c`)**; was: proposed, not built (that lane owned `dev-docs/**` only) | **2026-09-26 (audit batch): BUILT** in `dab751c` -- `primitives/src/test_support.rs` (`#[cfg(test)]` start-tag parser, `find_elements`/`inner_html`); migrated both named instances plus a third latent one in `tooltip.rs`'s hydration-parity test. Proven with `merge_attributes` temporarily reversed: migrated tests still pass. **Root cause:** a test that does something like `html[html.find("aria-label=\"...\"")..]` is implicitly assuming a stable attribute order that nothing in Dioxus's own contract actually guarantees (`dioxus-core`'s own VNode data model has no ordering invariant on its dynamic-attribute slot to begin with — see row 93's root-cause account) — `merge_attributes` sorting by name was simply the first thing to make that latent assumption visible, and any future change to attribute construction order could do it again. **Proposed fix by construction, not per-instance:** a shared test helper (e.g. in a `playwright`/Rust-test-support module already used across specs) that parses a rendered start tag into an attribute name→value map — a real, if minimal, tag-attribute parser — rather than any test slicing a string match out of raw HTML. Every call site that currently slices from an attribute match would switch to `attrs.get("aria-label")`-style lookups, which are order-independent by construction and can never break this way again, regardless of what future attribute-ordering change lands. Two instances subsumed by this construction: `calendar.rs`'s unit test (was slicing from `aria-label="…"`) and chart's `tooltip.rs::tooltip_fragment()` (was slicing from a literal match instead of the tag's own `<`) — both were re-anchored on the tag start as an interim fix, not rewritten onto the proposed helper, so they remain individually correct but do not yet demonstrate the construction. Not built this round: this lane owns `dev-docs/**` and the four playwright config files only, not test-support code [BUILT 2026-09-26, see the first sentence] |

| 110 | Playwright's own actionability pre-scroll (`.hover()`/`.focus()`/`.click()`'s auto-scroll) animates under the site's global `html { scroll-behavior: smooth }` (`preview/assets/main.css:54`) — a class, confirmed at a second instance | Instance 2, 2026-09-25 (round 8b): `carousel.spec.ts`'s "hovering the carousel stops rotation…" failed roughly 3-4 times per 5-10 runs on the SSG build. Instance 1, 2026-09-18 (`daebe40`, confirmed by reading that commit: its own message states verbatim "html gets scroll-behavior: smooth so the jump animates"): `oracle/tier2-html/top-layer.spec.ts`'s `pinNearTop` helper (~line 1212) | Diagnosis M (two hypotheses tried and refuted before the real one); the by-construction fix is **BUILT 2026-09-26 (`a8849a7`)**; was: proposed, not built | **2026-09-26 (audit batch): BUILT** in `a8849a7` -- `playwright/fixtures.ts` overrides the worker `browser` fixture so every context gets `html { scroll-behavior: auto !important }`; every spec imports `test`/`expect` from it, enforced by the new `scripts/check-playwright-fixture-import.sh` (it caught `navbar.spec.ts` the same day). The two ad-hoc patches were simplified onto it. **Two hypotheses refuted, in order, before the real mechanism.** The main loop's own first read — a mid-transition autoplay tick counted as an extra advance — was disproved by a MutationObserver + `performance.now()` probe: `mouseenter` fires, a genuine `mouseleave` follows ~250-300ms later, and rotation resumes on a fresh ~1200ms cadence. The actual cause: the whole page was still smooth-scrolling (~900px) from `.hover()`'s own `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded` pre-check — an unqualified scroll defaults to `behavior: "auto"`, which itself defers to the ancestor's CSS `scroll-behavior: smooth` — dragging the carousel out from under the still-resting cursor. Confirmed two independent ways: forcing `scroll-behavior: auto` site-wide dropped it to 0/15 failures; an instant pre-scroll also gave 0/15. No `carousel.rs` change — component logic was correct throughout. **Fixed for instance 2** with a `scrollIntoViewInstant()` call before `.hover()` (and `.focus()`, as defence-in-depth) in `62183b3`. **Instance 1** (`top-layer.spec.ts`'s `pinNearTop`, per that helper's own inline comment dated 2026-09-18) predates this round and carries the identical fix, arrived at independently: `behavior: "instant"` on its `scrollIntoView`/`scrollTo` calls, opting out of the same CSS property for the same reason. **Per `CLAUDE.md`'s own "two occurrences is a class" rule — proposed here, not built [BUILT 2026-09-26, `a8849a7`, see the first sentence]** (this lane owns `dev-docs/**` only): a shared Playwright fixture, or an `addInitScript`/`page.addStyleTag` in the local + CI configs, setting `html { scroll-behavior: auto !important }` for the test run. This subsumes both instances above and any future actionability pre-scroll, without a per-call-site fix each time. It does **not** touch a test's own *explicit* `scrollIntoView({behavior: 'smooth'})` call — verified by reading `carousel.spec.ts`'s "paged transition animates" test, which asserts on exactly such a call: an explicit `behavior` argument overrides the ancestor CSS default regardless of what that CSS says, so this construction leaves animation tests working. **Weaker alternative:** scope `main.css`'s rule under `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` — subsumes both instances too, but only for a runner that emulates `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, which this repo's configs do not do by default (confirmed: no `reducedMotion` project setting in any of the four `*.local.config.ts` files or in `playwright.config.ts`), so it would need that setting added as well to actually take effect |
