| 138 | Toast depth parity: loading type, action button, promise helper, update/close by id, stacking and swipe-dismissal are missing (shadcn rebuilt its toast on Base UI Toast) | **BACKLOG** -- recorded 2026-10-04 from the catalog audit (§6.4); depth, not a catalog gap (`toast` is present); **scheduled in the next-session order (2026-10-05)**; the Base UI drift from the shadcn comparison is folded in below | M-L (was M; the folded-in items add an S and an M) | **2026-10-05 fold-in (shadcn comparison, "where our port has fallen behind"):** shadcn rebuilt `toast` on Base UI Toast on 2026-07-23 and ours has four types and none of the new behaviour. The comparison's list, all "Add later": (0) **close or update a toast by id** (S; `show` returns nothing to hold on to; the prerequisite of the promise step), (1) a **`loading` type** (S; `ToastType` stops at `Info`), (2) the **action button** (`actionProps`, e.g. Undo; M), (3) **`promise`** (one toast moves from loading to success or error; M, needs (0)), (4) **swipe to dismiss** (M), plus the **stacking** below. A sensible order: (0), (1), (3), (2), stacking, (4). The four numbered steps below are the original 2026-10-04 plan and still hold. shadcn's base `toast` (Base UI Toast, release note 2026-07-23) documents types, an **action button**, **promise** (loading -> success/error), **stacking** and **swipe dismissal**. `primitives/src/toast.rs` has the four types and `ToastOptions { description, duration, permanent }` but no action, promise helper, swipe or stack (grep of `toast.rs` and `toast/component.rs` finds none). Take them as four small steps, each with its own spec: (1) an `action: Option<(label, callback)>` on `ToastOptions` rendered as a `Button` inside the toast, keyboard reachable, which dismisses on activation; (2) `use_toast().promise(future, {loading, success, error})` that updates one toast in place and announces the outcome (`role="status"`, not a second toast); (3) stacking: collapsed pile with hover/focus expand, `prefers-reduced-motion` honoured; (4) swipe dismissal with pointer events (threshold + velocity, RTL-aware direction). Base has no `sonner` page, so this is the toast's own parity target. |
| 139 | PR #83's "all gates green" was wrong: `main` was clippy-red from `d9bcfda` (`primitives/src/navbar.rs:655`, `clippy::redundant_closure`) | **FIXED 2026-10-04, merged** (`a17983c`, PR #84 / `001a5f0`: `unwrap_or_else(&*is_open)`, one line, no behaviour change; was uncommitted at time of writing) | S | **Correction to the record.** The 2026-10-04 addenda of rows 111 and 116 and PR #83 (merge `44a103e`) quote "all gates green (... clippy `-D warnings` ...)" as measured "on `20e2fa4`". `20e2fa4` is an ancestor of `d9bcfda` ("open on click so a held tap cannot light-dismiss its own menu", row 40), which added `.unwrap_or_else(\|\| is_open())` to `NavbarTrigger`'s `onclick` (`git log -S` names it as the introducing commit; line 655 sits outside every `cfg` block of the file, so the default clippy run flags it). The quoted green was therefore taken on a tree that did not contain the line that went red; the merged tip's clippy was never what was run. **Same class as row 67** (a gate red on `main` because nothing ran it on the merged tree), different cause: not a missing workflow but a claim about a tree the claimer did not run. **Construction:** `scripts/run-gates.sh` (row 116, round-2 addendum): one entry point, the summary prints HEAD plus a hash of the uncommitted diff so a green names its own tree, exit 0 only when every gate ran, an unlisted `check-*.sh` fails the run. **Honest limit:** that makes a mismatched claim visible, it cannot stop anyone quoting a green from another commit, so a "gates green" in a commit message or addendum should carry the tree line the script prints. **A separate hole found the same day, not the cause of this miss:** `cargo clippy --workspace` never enabled primitives' `web` or preview's `server` feature (104 `feature = "web"` cfg sites and the shipped SSG server code unlinted), which is why the script runs three clippy arms. The earlier addenda are left as written; this row is their correction. |
| 140 | Menubar: a pointer selection leaves DOM focus on `<body>`; the keyboard path restores it to the trigger | **LANDED 2026-10-05 (round 3)** -- recorded 2026-10-04 from the checkable-item lane (`playwright/menubar.spec.ts`, "by default (Radix onSelect default) ... by keyboard focus returns to its trigger"); deliberately not asserted there | S | Keyboard `Enter` on a Menubar item closes the menu and hands focus back to the menu's own trigger (APG menubar rule; asserted by that spec). A pointer selection does not: `MenubarItem` commits on `pointerdown` (`primitives/src/menubar.rs`, the `MenubarMenu` roving-focus collection's doc), the menu then unmounts, and the click that follows focuses an item that is being removed, so focus ends on `<body>` (as reported by the lane). The spec's comment records that this is the plain `MenubarItem`'s behaviour and that the new `MenubarCheckboxItem`/`MenubarRadioItem` inherit it, so the pointer half is not asserted. **Next:** reproduce by execution first (click an item, read `document.activeElement`; also on DropdownMenu and ContextMenu, whose pointer paths are unverified here), then restore focus to the trigger on a pointer close unless the click landed outside the menu bar (Radix `onCloseAutoFocus` semantics), and add the pointer case to `oracle-focus-restore.spec.ts` red-first. **Landed 2026-10-05 (round 3):** reproduced first, as asked. Only Menubar had the gap (its items commit on `pointerdown` and close the menu right there, so the mousedown that follows focused the item being unmounted); DropdownMenu and ContextMenu commit on click/pointerup and were already fine. **Construction:** `menu_root::keep_focus_on_pointerdown` (`primitives/src/menu_root.rs`) is the single pointerdown guard (it cancels the default so the refocus the commit made is the last focus change) for every menu content wrapper (Menubar, DropdownMenu and the other hosts), so the class cannot reopen in one host only; the `context_menu` copies were migrated onto it later in the round. **Proof:** pointer cases added to `playwright/oracle-focus-restore.spec.ts` for Menubar, DropdownMenu and ContextMenu ("returns focus to its trigger after a pointer selection"); they pass. |
| 141 | Dialog, AlertDialog, Sheet and Drawer exit animations play only where `transition: overlay allow-discrete` exists (Chromium); a deferred close in `use_dialog_open_driver` would make them cross-browser | **LANDED 2026-10-05 (round 3; the construction below was built, on a wider root cause)** -- recorded 2026-10-04 from the overlay-scrim construction | M | The shared scrim block's own comment states the limit (`dialog/style.css`, the identical block in `alert_dialog`, `sheet`, `drawer`): `close()` drops a `<dialog>` from the top layer synchronously and its `::backdrop` goes with it, so the exit only plays through `overlay ... allow-discrete` on the dialog itself, and "engines without `overlay` transitions keep the old snap-out". Firefox and Safari snap today (per the scrim lane; Chromium animates). The modal Popover already has the cross-browser form: `use_popover_modal_driver` (`primitives/src/popover.rs`) keeps the dialog modal until the content and scrim fades have both ended and only then calls `close()`, where `use_dialog_open_driver` (`primitives/src/lib.rs`) calls `close()` at once. **Construction:** give `use_dialog_open_driver` the same deferred close (wait for the panel's and the scrim's `animationend`/`transitionend`, bounded by a timeout, immediate under `prefers-reduced-motion`), so all five modal surfaces ride one driver and `overlay allow-discrete` becomes a refinement instead of the mechanism. Watch: reopen while closing, `use_dialog_close_sync` (Escape and form-method closes), `playwright/assert-backdrop-fade.ts` still green, and the proof needs Firefox and WebKit (row 4). **Landed 2026-10-05 (round 3).** Owner report 2026-10-05: Dialog close "glitches", a side Sheet "changes size" while closing, the Drawer "glitches". **Root cause (measured):** `use_dialog_open_driver` called `dialog.close()` the instant `open` went false, so the panel left the top layer in one frame, and on the HOME page a `position: fixed` panel is then sized and clipped by `.dx-component-card` (`content-visibility: auto`, added in round 2): Sheet 384x800 -> 384x256 and Drawer 1265x346 -> 1105x205 mid-slide. The component pages have no such ancestor, which is how the earlier verification missed it. **Construction:** the shared driver keeps the dialog open until its own and its `::backdrop`'s animations settle (bounded at 1.5 s, `DIALOG_EXIT_TIMEOUT_MS`), then calls `close()`; a generation counter guards a reopen; `transition: overlay allow-discrete` is gone from the stylesheets, so Firefox and Safari get a real exit by construction (executed on Chromium only: this sandbox has no Firefox or WebKit, row 4); `dialog:not([open]) { display: none }`; the modal Popover's web arm now calls the same driver. **Gates:** oracle `playwright/modal-exit.spec.ts` with `modal-consumers.ts` (consumers derived from source: dialog, alert_dialog and popover call the driver, sheet, drawer and command compose `DialogRoot`; a new modal component with no entry fails the first test) and `assert-modal-exit.ts` (a closing modal is never rendered outside the top layer, keeps one layout box on every rendered frame, and `close()` waits for the longest exit animation). **Found by the same pass:** the scroll lock released at unmount, ~250 ms after `close()`, not at the `open` flip as assumed, and now releases on the dialog's `close` event (rows 149 and 159); an upward Drawer drag closed the drawer because a handle press that ended over the backdrop read as a backdrop click, so backdrop dismiss now requires the press to have STARTED on the backdrop (every dialog); Drawer flick velocity now uses the events' `timeStamp`s (100 ms window, at least 10 px, a 25% distance rule, a rubber band, `pointercancel` snaps back). **Landed with it:** an `overlay` prop (default true; Popover default false) on DialogRoot, AlertDialogRoot, Drawer, CommandDialog and the styled wrappers, through a theme rule `:where(dialog[data-dx-overlay="off"])::backdrop { ... !important }`, `overlay` demos and `playwright/overlay-switch.spec.ts`. Measurements and the spec invariants: [round-3 note](./research/round3-2026-10-05.md), section 1. |
| 142 | The phone theme picker is missing on `/demos` and `/charts` (no docs sidebar there) | **LANDED 2026-10-05 (round 3)** -- recorded 2026-10-04 with the picker landing (row 111, round-2 addendum) | S | The header's popover trigger is hidden at <= 760px (`main.css`) and the phone entry, `theme::ThemePickerSidebarFooter`, is rendered inside the docs `Sidebar` (`preview/src/main.rs`, under the scrolling nav), so a route that does not render that sidebar has no picker at phone width. Reported for `/demos` and `/charts`; confirm on a 390px viewport and list every route that renders without the docs sidebar before fixing. **Fix by construction:** render the phone trigger from the shared site chrome (the header) instead of a per-layout sidebar, so a route cannot lose it by choosing another layout, while keeping exactly one trigger visible per width (the "exactly one panel mounted" rule in `theme::ThemePicker`'s doc). Spec: a phone-viewport sweep asserting the trigger is visible on every route type. **Landed 2026-10-05 (round 3):** a phone trigger rendered from the shared site chrome, as proposed: the header's own `theme::ThemePicker` renders `ThemePickerPhoneTrigger` when, and only when, the route has no `SidebarCtx` (`/demos`, `/charts` and its tabs, the not-found pages), and it opens the SAME single `ThemePickerDrawer` (one panel mounted, one trigger visible per width). The header overlap at 390 and 320 px is fixed. **Spec:** `playwright/theme-picker-phone.spec.ts`, a sweep over every route type asserting exactly one Theme entry is reachable at phone width, that it opens the same drawer, that the header still fits, and that desktop is unchanged. |
| 143 | `VirtualList`: add a content-visibility mode (default up to ~5k rows), quantize the scroll signal, drop the O(N) per-resize clone | **LANDED 2026-10-05 (round 3; owner-approved)** -- from the virtualization survey (`dev-docs/research/virtualization-survey-2026-10-04.md` sections 2, 7.1, 8) | M | Evidence (code reading, not benchmarked): every scroll event re-renders the whole `VirtualList` (bridge `virtual_list.rs:126-170`, `virtualizer.rs:257-258`); `onresize` does `measurements.peek().clone()`, O(N), per row resize (`virtual_list.rs:208`); `compute_measurements` rebuilds an O(N) `Vec` per newly measured row (`:105-117`); `item_size_cache` never evicts (`virtualizer.rs:186`); SSR renders 0 rows (`virtualizer.rs:260`) and find-in-page sees ~30 of 2000. Probe (Chromium 141, loaded box, trust ratios): cv is 3-5x faster to first layout and 2.5-3.5x lighter than rendering every row (10k rows +72 MB vs +194 MB) but stays linear in N (+445 MB at 100k vs +12 MB windowed), so windowing stays for > ~10-50k rows. **Plan:** (1) a `ContentVisibility` mode mirroring `message_scroller`'s `Virtualization::ContentVisibility`, default for N <= ~5k, windowing above; (2) quantize the scroll signal to the visible range so a scroll event re-renders only when the range changes; (3) remove the per-resize clone, and ignore ResizeObserver entries from skipped rows (`checkVisibility({contentVisibilityAuto: true})`), which would otherwise cache a skipped row's `contain-intrinsic-size` as a real measurement. Specs: find-in-page and a 10k-row case; none exist. **Not a grid:** we have no 2-D virtualization; the survey recommends CSS grid + cv (two-value `contain-intrinsic-size`) before any 2-D windowing engine. **2026-10-05 addendum -- Owner-approved 2026-10-05; in progress in this session.** Engine-level helpers this row needs (content-visibility mode helper, quantized range signal) belong in `primitives/src/virtual/` (row 154); find-in-page for the windowed mode above the cv threshold is its own research row (153). **Landed 2026-10-05 (round 3):** `virtualize: Auto, ContentVisibility or Windowed` (`VirtualListMode`) on `VirtualList`, same prop name and `data-virtualize` spelling as `message_scroller`. **`Auto` (default) is content-visibility while `count <= auto_threshold`, windowed above; `auto_threshold` defaults to 1000, not the ~5k this row planned**, because mounting every row is one main-thread task proportional to the count (~90 ms at 1,000 rows, 205 ms at 5,000, one 147 ms task). **Chunked content-visibility:** `primitives/src/virtual/cv_chunks.rs` groups 20 consecutive rows per skippable element (the browser does per-frame work for every element it may skip), keyed by absolute position; per scroll frame (release): 1,000 rows 7.7 ms one-per-row against 1.1 ms chunked, 5,000 rows 31 against 1.5 (windowed 1.4). `message_scroller` uses the same module: its 2,000-row demo went 20.3 -> 3.7 ms per frame (debug numbers, heavily loaded machine). **Windowed engine:** a Fenwick tree, `primitives/src/virtual/size_index.rs`, replaces the per-measure O(N) rebuild and the per-resize clone, and the scroll signal is quantized (the mounted range keeps hysteresis): 100k rows, 1000 px scroll, 53 -> 3 renders and 275 -> 48 ms. Plan item 3's second half (ignoring ResizeObserver entries from skipped rows) is moot: content-visibility mode has no measurement at all. **Specs:** `playwright/virtual_list.spec.ts` (the cv mode with find-in-page, the windowed trade-off stated as a test, renders per scroll, the unmount listener count, and a release-only per-frame assertion under 4 ms at 1,000 rows) and `message_scroller.spec.ts`; the per-frame thresholds only mean something on a release build. Find-in-page above the threshold stays row 153; the shared engine outline is row 154. Numbers and the table: [round-3 note](./research/round3-2026-10-05.md), section 5. |
| 144 | Opt-in `hidden="until-found"` for Collapsible, Accordion and Tabs | **LANDED 2026-10-05 (round 3; owner-approved)** -- from the virtualization survey (sections 1.9 and 6) | M | Closed content is unmounted in all three (Collapsible unless `keep_mounted`, Accordion after the close animation, Tabs renders an empty `div[hidden]`), so Ctrl+F cannot find it, a `#fragment` cannot open it, and SSR/SSG HTML omits it. `hidden="until-found"` is the one native member of the content-visibility family that adds a user-visible capability rather than speed (probe: fragment navigation fires `beforematch`; `window.find` finds `until-found` text and not `display: none`). **Sketch, default unchanged:** Collapsible keeps children mounted under `hidden="until-found"` (its `keep_mounted` today sets nothing and the styled layer would show them -- a latent bug with no spec); Accordion sets it after the close animation instead of unmounting; Tabs renders inactive panels and must delete the author `display: none` at `tabs/style.css:118`, which defeats the UA rule; a `beforematch` listener (no typed `onbeforematch` in Dioxus 0.7: a small `document::eval` bridge or `onmounted`) opens the panel. Cost: every panel mounted and hydrated, which is why it is opt-in. Support: Chrome 102, Firefox 139 (events; 148 full), Safari 26.2 partial (reveals without scrolling, WebKit 304174); older engines treat it as plain `hidden`, so there is no regression path. Headless `window.find` did not fire `beforematch`: verify the Ctrl+F path by hand. **2026-10-05 addendum -- Owner-approved 2026-10-05; in progress in this session.** The windowed-list analogue (searchable text placeholders carrying `hidden="until-found"`) is row 153. **Landed 2026-10-05 (round 3):** a `hidden_until_found` prop (default false) on Collapsible, Accordion and Tabs, sharing `hidden_attribute`, `use_beforematch` and `PanelHidden` from `primitives/src/collapsible.rs`; `until_found` demos and a docs section with the browser-support table. Collapsible keeps closed content mounted as `hidden="until-found"`; Accordion sets it after the close animation instead of unmounting; Tabs mounts inactive panels the same way, and `tabs/style.css` no longer applies its author `display: none` to `hidden="until-found"` panels (it would defeat the UA rule) and takes those panels out of flow at zero size so the themed card padding does not show. **Side finding, fixed:** Collapsible `keep_mounted` closed content used to be shown (the latent bug this row named); it now carries `hidden=""`, and `hidden_until_found` overrides `keep_mounted`. **Specs:** `collapsible.spec.ts`, `accordion.spec.ts` and `tabs.spec.ts` drive the reveal by a real `#fragment` navigation and a synthetic `beforematch`, because headless Chromium's `window.find` finds until-found text but does not fire `beforematch`. **Still unverified, by hand only:** the real Ctrl+F path in a headed browser (not done in round 3). |
| 145 | Unification refactors, top 3: theme baseline, floating-surface recipe, state recipes (incl. Combobox and Textarea missing a focus ring) | **OPEN, OWNER-APPROVED 2026-10-05 for the next new session** (second in the chain 135 -> 145 -> 111; step 0 is the instance-list recount) -- audit 2026-10-04 (`dev-docs/research/unification-audit-2026-10-04.md`; static read at `44a103e`, nothing executed, hypotheses H1-H4 in its section 6) | L (three lanes) | The audit found 15 concerns implemented ad hoc per component; this round's scrim and menu-item-indicator constructions are two already done, and its section 2 lists what is still incomplete in them (the Command dialog still paints its own 30% wrapper scrim over the UA `::backdrop`; `--dx-overlay-*` and `--dx-menu-item-indicator-*` are defined in no theme file, only as per-use fallbacks). **Take in this order:** (1) **Theme baseline**, serial and first (owns `dx-components-theme.css`): zero-specificity `:where([class^="dx-"])`-keyed rules, the row-111 border-box precedent -- `font-family: inherit` (5 sheets hard-code a stack, 11 button sheets set none), a reduced-motion block (14 sheets lack one), `touch-action` (95 declarations in 33 files), half-step spacing tokens (`0.375rem` x20 ...), the shared `::backdrop` block, the overlay and indicator tokens; near-zero visual change except fonts and the Command scrim. (2) **Floating-surface recipe** (12 surfaces: popover, select, combobox, dropdown, context menu, menubar, navbar, navigation menu, hover card, tooltip, color picker, date picker): 6 edge/shadow idioms, open durations 100/150/200 ms on 5 curves, 66 `@keyframes` of which 46 sit in byte-identical clusters that collapse to 14; one `data-dx-surface` rule, shared keyframes, `--dx-floating-*` tokens. | 149 | `scroll_lock.rs` installs non-passive `wheel`/`touchmove` listeners on `window` permanently after the first lock | **LANDED 2026-10-05 (round 3; widened into and fixed by row 159)** -- found 2026-10-04 by the scroll-jank investigation (`dev-docs/research/scroll-jank-2026-10-04.md` section 7.3); not fixed there because the file belonged to another lane | S | `scroll_lock.rs::ensure_scroll_block_listeners_installed` adds both listeners at the first lock (the first Dialog, AlertDialog, Popover, DropdownMenu or ContextMenu open) and keeps them for the life of the page, gated afterwards by a `__dxScrollLocked` flag. After any overlay has opened once, every wheel and touch on the page again waits for the main thread before the compositor may scroll -- the cost this round removed from `#main` (Chromium `DidHandleInputEventSentToMain` 40/40 wheel ticks vs 0; wheel latency max 388 vs 143 ms with a busy main thread). **Fix:** add on lock, remove on unlock (refcounted if locks nest). `scripts/check-blocking-scroll-listeners.sh` allowlists `scroll_lock.rs` with a reason today; tighten that entry when this lands. The oracle (`oracle/tier2-html/scroll-main-thread.spec.ts`) inspects `#main` only because Playwright's own capture listeners pollute `window`: add an open-then-close-an-overlay step that reads the init shim's listener inventory, so "no permanent blocking listener" becomes testable. **Landed 2026-10-05 (round 3), through row 159's helper rather than a one-off fix:** `scroll_lock.rs`'s four listeners are installed by the outermost lock through the shared helper (`JsListeners`, passive unless the call says otherwise) and are torn down when the lock lets go, so no permanent blocking `wheel`/`touchmove` listener is left on `window` after an overlay has been opened. The lock now releases on the dialog's own `close` event: it released at unmount, ~250 ms after `close()`; the premise that it released at the `open` flip was wrong (row 141). **The oracle step this row asked for exists:** `playwright/oracle/tier2-html/listener-inventory.spec.ts` asks Chromium (CDP `DOMDebugger.getEventListeners` on `window`, `document`, `<html>`, `<body>`) for the listeners left after every modal, popover and menu consumer has been opened and closed twice, compared with a baseline taken BEFORE the first open, and asserts no non-passive `wheel`/`mousewheel`/`touchstart`/`touchmove` remains; it also pins the lock timing (a dialog whose exit is slowed to 1.2 s does not scroll and cancels the wheel until its real `close()`) and that the page width does not shift under a forced classic scrollbar. `scripts/check-blocking-scroll-listeners.sh` now also sees `listen(.., { passive: false })`; `scroll_lock.rs` keeps reasoned `blocking-ok` markers (the lock cancels the wheel by design). |
| 150 | Home-page blocks registered their hooks on the parent scope: `MasonryCard` called `component.call(())`, so `BlockPlayer` grew a new never-cleared interval every ~1.2 s | **FIXED 2026-10-04, merged** (`b83640a`, PR #84 / `001a5f0`, SCROLL-FIX lane); the candidate `.call(())` gate in the notes stays OPEN (measure false positives first) | M | Found by the scroll-jank investigation (`dev-docs/research/scroll-jank-2026-10-04.md` section 7.1; its section 3 had the mechanism wrong). `Callback::call` runs its closure with the scope it was CREATED in on the scope stack, so a block that calls hooks directly (`BlockPlayer`, `BlockColorPalette`, `BlockCommand`, `BlockComposer`) pushed them onto `WidgetMasonry`'s hook list; the hook index resets only when that scope renders, so every later re-render of the card allocated a FRESH set (new signal, memo, effect and, for the player, a new `setInterval`) beside the old unreachable one: 128 live intervals after ~100 s, 1,326 timer wakeups/s at 155 s, 387 layouts during one scroll vs 0-1. Visible side effects, probed: player readout stuck at 1:24, pause did nothing, composer typing lost, colour-palette arrow keys no-op. **Construction (covers the class):** blocks mount as real components (`let Block = entry.component; rsx! { Block {} }` as `MasonryCard`'s children), each owning a scope, with the rule in `MasonryCard`'s doc; plus `dioxus_primitives::interval::use_interval` (a task spawned in the caller's scope and dropped with it, no JS timer) and `scripts/check-uncleared-intervals.sh` (bans `setInterval`/`set_interval`/`Interval::new`/`spawn_forever` unless marked `// interval-ok: <reason>`). **Not gated, and this row says so:** `check-self-subscribing-effects.sh` would not have caught it (the effect never read the signals; the spawned task is excluded by design) and `check-hooks-in-closures.sh` sees a hook lexically inside another hook's closure, not a hook reached through the `.call(())` of a callback created in another scope. **Candidate gate, not built:** flag `.call(())` of a component `Callback<_, Element>` inside rsx in `preview/src`, allowlisting render props that provably call no hooks; a `Callback<_, Element>` render prop is legitimate and common and a hook inside one is invisible to grep, so measure the false-positive rate over `preview/src` before deciding between a gate and a doc rule. Runtime backstop: `oracle/tier2-html/scroll-main-thread.spec.ts` tests 1 and 2 (live intervals constant; readout advances and pause works). |
| 151 | A `document::Link` stylesheet can be recorded as present and never inserted: a subtree torn down in the turn it first renders drops its head elements, then every later `Link` for the same href is skipped as a duplicate | **FIXED 2026-10-04, merged** (`66b19d3`, PR #84 / `001a5f0`; `preview/src/eager_head.rs`) | M | **A class, third occurrence:** row 46 (`main.css` after `ComponentBlockDemo`'s redirect; its fix moved `GlobalHead` into `AppLayout`, sound for those sheets only), an earlier page with no `Button` stylesheet, and now `/component/X/` rendering without the header `LanguageSelect` and the theme picker's `Popover` stylesheets (the picker's panel computed `border 3px / radius 0` instead of `1px / 10px`). **Mechanism, read from dioxus 0.7.9 source:** `Link` records `href\|rel` in a root-context de-duplication set at render (`dioxus-document elements/link.rs:123-137`) but the web document appends the `<link>` from `queue_effect` on the Link's own scope (`dioxus-web document.rs:160-164`); `Runtime::remove_scope` drops a removed scope's queued effects (`dioxus-core runtime.rs:190-193`), and effects only run once no scope is dirty. The trigger is an ancestor's first-run `use_effect` that dirties a scope unmounting the subtree: the legacy `/component/?name=X` shell renders `Navbar`, then `nav.replace(..)`s from its own effect. **Construction:** `EagerHeadDocument` wraps the platform `Document` and leaves `create_link/meta/script/style` at the trait defaults (`create_element_in_head` through `eval`, which `WebDocument` runs synchronously), so the `<link>` is in `<head>` before `Link` returns, in the same breath the set records it; `create_head_component` (the hydration handshake) is delegated untouched; installed once in `App` via `use_eager_head_document()` on every client build and never on the server, where `ServerDocument` must keep collecting the SSR head. One install covers every component, including the wrappers `dx components add` copies out. **Guards:** `scripts/check-eager-head-document.sh` (static: the wrapper exists, never uses `queue_effect`, `App` installs it) and `playwright/oracle/tier2-html/stylesheets-present.spec.ts` (behavioural: every rendered component's stylesheet is applied on fresh loads of every route type). The script's header cites this finding by name rather than by row; this row is its anchor. |
| 152 | Test-suite review: revisit the Playwright suite (~1,786 tests, ~21 min on a release build with 4 workers) for slimming and optimisation without losing still-relevant coverage | **OPEN** -- owner note 2026-10-05; nothing was run for this row, the evidence below is read from the round-2 full-suite report | M | **Starting evidence (all from `/home/user/tgt/r2/pw-full2.json` and `.log`, the 2026-10-04 round-2 full run: release no-base-path SSG build, Chromium only, 4 workers, `fullyParallel`; wall 1,255 s = 20.9 min, 1,761 passed / 12 failed / 13 skipped).** **Size:** 103 spec files; `grep -c "test("` finds 1,213 static `test(` calls but 1,786 tests run, because 54 spec files generate tests in loops (`main-thread` 3 -> 58, `dx-class-migration` 1 -> 52, `touch-double-tap` 12 -> 57, `touch-focus-zoom` 13 -> 58, `top-layer` 52 -> 99, `hydration-parity` 13 -> 42, `top-layer-ink` 1 -> 30, `carousel` 110 -> 144), so a slimming pass must count run tests, not source lines. **It is total work, not scheduling:** summed test time is 4,916 s; 4,916 / 4 workers = 1,229 s against a 1,255 s wall, so the 4 workers are ~98 % packed and only less work or more shards shortens the run. **Where the time is** (summed seconds, share of 4,916 s): carousel family 255 tests / 836 s (17 %; `carousel.spec.ts` 144 / 414 s, `carousel-virtual-wheel` 38 / 247 s, `carousel-wheel-replay` 22 / 80 s); chart family 289 tests / 938 s (19 %; radar 44 / 175 s, pie 36 / 151 s, `charts_gallery` 29 / 122 s, `chart_tooltip` 35 / 112 s, `chart_tooltip_follow` 33 / 83 s); `oracle/` 663 tests / 1,445 s (29 %; tier2-html 402 / 959 s, tier1-apg 173 / 309 s, tier3-radix 46 / 96 s, hydration-parity 42 / 81 s); `message_scroller.spec.ts` 25 / 171 s; theme-preset specs 51 / 257 s. **The cost is a per-test floor, not a few giants:** median test 1.9 s, 77 % of tests (1,366) take <= 3 s and make up 50 % of the time (2,469 s), while the 28 tests over 10 s are 525 s (11 %) and the slowest 50 are 722 s (15 %), so the lever is the ~1.5-2 s navigate-and-hydrate each test pays, multiplied by the number of tests, more than any single slow test. **Duplicated-coverage candidates (each needs a per-test check that the guard is really redundant before anything is cut):** (a) *per-component axe*: 181 tests titled "has no automatically detectable a11y issues" in 70 files = 956 s (19 % of all test time), 141 `expectNoAxeViolations`/`AxeBuilder` call sites in 72 files, about 55 of them the at-rest "loaded" scan; `preview.spec.ts` scans only the home page and `component/calendar`, so there is no global sweep today (hydration-parity already enumerates every prerendered route through `SSG_SITE_DIR`, the same list a sweep would use). Open-state scans (menus, dialogs, calendar focus) cannot be folded into an at-rest sweep. The saving is unmeasured: the slowest scans (`message_scroller` 23.6 s and 23.3 s, `preview.spec.ts` 19.7 s and 13.5 s, `calendar` 11.6 s) are dominated by axe's analysis, not navigation. (b) *repeated route matrices*: seven specs each walk (nearly) every component route and run one cheap probe per route, 312 tests / 614 s (12 %): `main-thread` (58), `dx-class-migration` (52), `touch-focus-zoom` (58), `touch-double-tap` (57), `top-layer-ink` (30), `hydration-parity` (42), `stylesheets-present` (15), so each component page is navigated ~7 times; a visit-each-route-once fixture running all the probes would keep every assertion. (c) *light/dark and preset matrices*: ~95 tests whose titles say light, dark, preset or theme = ~348 s; `theme-preset-contrast.spec.ts` (24 tests, 158 s, base x accent x light/dark swept in one in-page pass per probe) is already the efficient shape, the ones to compare it against are `carousel.spec.ts`'s 6-viewport x light/dark frame-escape matrix (12 tests, each a fresh navigation) and the `toggle` and `toggle_group` slate/gray x light/dark background tests (6 each). (d) *oracle vs component overlap*: the overlay family is exercised by its own spec plus up to ~9 oracle files (`dropdown_menu`, `dialog` and `popover` are named in 16, 24 and 30 spec files respectively: `top-layer` 99 tests / 239 s, `native-dialog` 18, `main-thread`, `top-layer-ink`, `auto-popover-opening-gesture`, `touch-*`, and five separate "Scrim" tests built on `assert-backdrop-fade.ts` for alert-dialog, dialog, sheet, drawer and popover); which of those guard different rules (every oracle header cites its source rule and backlog row) and which re-prove the same one has not been audited. **Long-timeout specs** (`test.setTimeout`): `charts_gallery.spec.ts:151` 240 s; `carousel-virtual-wheel.spec.ts` 11 calls of 120-180 s (lines 527-1191); `anchor-landing.spec.ts` 3 x `SLOW_TEST_MS`; `hydration-parity.spec.ts:931` 300 s; `computed-style-snapshot.spec.ts:312` 20 min (a tool, skipped unless `DX_SNAPSHOT`); config defaults are 120 s (`playwright.config.ts`) and 90 s (local configs). They are headroom, not cost: the slowest carousel-virtual-wheel test in the last run took 11.7 s; the only long real runs are `hydration-parity` Rule 2 extended (67.8 s) and `charts_gallery` Copy (24.4 s). **Known flaky or red specs:** the last run's 12 reds were 6 known (`calendar.spec.ts:364`, row 113, a 90 s timeout on every run = 1.8 % of all test time; `date-picker.spec.ts` x2 and `top-layer` Rule 8, rows 21 and 106, CSS-anchor geometry this sandbox's Chromium cannot pass; `drag_and_drop_list.spec.ts:300`, row 114; the by-design Rule 4c selftest) and 6 load flakes that were green on a `--workers=1` rerun (`alert-dialog` and `dialog` Scrim, `avatar`, `carousel.spec.ts:3389`, `message_scroller.spec.ts:169`, `main-thread` drawer drag); rows 112 (carousel sampling across async gaps, both instances fixed), 115 (tooltip, hover-card, popover, combobox:134, menu-submenu:307, carousel timing, `sampleCloseFade`) and 121 (`navigation_menu.spec.ts:128`, still marked OPEN, not among the last run's reds) are the rest of the family. **Consequence for any sharding or extra-worker plan:** the flakes are load-sensitivity, so more parallelism will raise them unless rows 112-115 are cleared first. Also: `main-thread` (58 tests / 138 s) and `scroll-main-thread` (8 / 56 s) are release-only timing specs, a natural separate tag. **Approach options (not decided):** tag a smoke subset (one at-rest axe, one behaviour spec, one hydration pass per component) against a full run; `--shard=i/n` across machines once the load flakes are fixed (4 shards x 4 workers would be ~5 min of wall time at today's total); collapse the seven route matrices (b) into one visit-each-route-once fixture and fold at-rest axe (a) into one sweep while keeping open-state scans; per-PR targeted runs through a changed-files map (a component directory -> its own spec plus the oracle subjects that name it; the oracles already hold explicit subject lists such as `OVERLAY_SUBJECTS` and `COMPONENTS`) with the full suite on demand, nightly or before a merge to `main` (all workflows are `workflow_dispatch`-only under the CI freeze, row 33, so today the full suite is the only run there is); a cheap first pass on the 90 s calendar test and the flakes. **Guard against losing coverage:** for each removal name the rule or regression it guarded (spec headers cite them), and keep the cheapest instance that still goes red against the bug it was written for (the repo's red-first rule, `plan.md`). |
| 153 | Native find-in-page for WINDOWED virtual lists: keep per-item text in the DOM as `hidden="until-found"` placeholders, then sync the virtualizer to where the browser found the match | **OPEN (research)** -- owner idea 2026-10-05; nothing prototyped | M | **Problem:** a windowed `VirtualList` (above the content-visibility threshold of row 143) keeps only ~viewport + 2 x buffer rows in the DOM, so Ctrl+F sees about 30 of 2,000 rows (survey section 1.1) and SSR/SSG renders none. **Owner idea:** keep the off-screen items' text in the DOM before and after the rendered window so the browser can find it, then move the virtualizer to where the browser scrolled. **Refined design:** per-item lightweight text placeholders carrying only a proposed per-item `text_value(i)` (a callback returning the searchable text, no markup; not an existing prop) inside an element with `hidden="until-found"`. Such an element costs no layout until revealed and is searchable in Chromium 102+, Firefox 139+ (events; 148 full) and Safari 26.2 (partial, reveals without scrolling, WebKit 304174); older engines treat it as plain `hidden`, so they fall back to not-findable, with no regression. When a match is found the browser reveals the placeholder and fires `beforematch` on that exact element (survey section 6), which names the **item index directly**. That is better than reading the scroll position after the find, which is ambiguous because unrevealed placeholders have no height, so the scroll offset cannot be mapped back to an index. The virtualizer then scrolls its window to that index, renders the real row, and re-hides or re-uses the placeholder. **Open risks to prototype:** (1) "find next" continuity: the browser's active match lives in a DOM node that is swapped when the real row replaces the placeholder, so the next Enter may restart from the top or skip; measure per engine. (2) DOM and memory cost: one text node per item (about 1 node) against about 9 for a full row, but at 100k items that is still ~100k nodes (survey probe: +445 MB at 100k with content-visibility rows, +12 MB windowed); measure, and consider chunked placeholders (one element per N items, an IntersectionObserver or a second `beforematch` step picking the item after the reveal). (3) Accessibility: `until-found` content is hidden from the accessibility tree, so screen-reader browse history still stops at the rendered window; no worse than today, but not a fix for it. (4) Unsupported browsers (and Safari 26.2's no-scroll reveal) fall back to not-findable. (5) Headless `window.find` did not fire `beforematch` in the survey's probe: the Ctrl+F path has to be verified by hand, and by the keyboard in a real browser. **Applies to:** `VirtualList` windowed mode now and, if windowing is ever approved for it (row 146 defers it), `message_scroller`. **Related:** row 144 (the same attribute on Collapsible, Accordion and Tabs, owner-approved), row 143 (the cv mode this complements), row 147 (the Safari 18-25 gap on `content-visibility: auto`, which this design does not have: `until-found` is not skipped content). Lives in the shared engine per row 154. **2026-10-05:** stays a dependent row of 154. In 154's outline this is the Windowed + find-in-page strategy (layer 2): `beforematch` names the item index, and the core's `scroll_to(index, align)` (layer 1) moves the window to it. It waits for 143 and for 154's engine, and is not in the next-session order. |
| 154 | Shared virtualization engine: ONE engine in `primitives/src/virtual/` (core, strategy, DOM bridge) that every virtualized or windowed component consumes, rather than merging the components | **DESIGN NOTE** -- first note recorded 2026-10-05 from the owner question whether the virtualized components should share an implementation; the **owner's three-layer outline was added the same day**. Not scheduled: it waits for row 143 to land, and its first consumer is `message_scroller` (with row 146). No code change here. **Owner decision 2026-10-05: stays BACKLOG, with a REQUIRED first step: research the impact on Carousel before any migration (first paragraph of the notes)** | S (note); the build is not estimated | **REQUIRED FIRST STEP (owner decision 2026-10-05): research the impact on Carousel before any migration onto the engine.** Carousel is not a plain windowed list. Besides the virtual loop it carries trackpad and wheel behaviour no other consumer has: `playwright/carousel-virtual-wheel.spec.ts` (and its sibling `carousel-wheel-replay.spec.ts`, which replays real MacBook trackpad recordings), the wheel/trackpad gesture handling in `primitives/src/carousel.rs`, and the virtual loop itself. In the source: a browser-idle gate (`CAROUSEL_SCROLL_TRACKING_JS`, `CAROUSEL_INPUT_QUIET_MS`) that counts every wheel event as input and treats `scrollend` as NOT idle while a trackpad still delivers momentum (~`:1112-1160`); trusted-wheel handling of the pinned state; the mode-D edge band and the `window.__dxCarouselWheel` trace flag; the virtual window's re-align deferred until input has gone quiet (~`:796-802`); and a carousel-owned bridge that registers `scroll`, `wheel`, `touchstart`, `touchend`, `pointerdown`, `keydown` and a `dx-carousel-rest` listener on the container (~`:1296-1304`), against the engine bridge's planned one scroll listener and one ResizeObserver. **The research must answer, in writing and in this row, whether the engine's DOM bridge (one passive scroll listener plus one ResizeObserver, scope-owned, a render-signal write only when the range changes) can host that behaviour, or whether Carousel stays a consumer of only the core `window()`.** It should: list each carousel input path (wheel, trackpad momentum, touch pan, drag, keyboard, `scrollend`) and say for each whether it is a scroll-bridge concern or a carousel-physics concern; check whether the engine's range-change write can be held until input is quiet (the carousel's window moves wait for idle, a list's do not) without a carousel-only branch inside the bridge; and run `carousel-virtual-wheel.spec.ts`, `carousel-wheel-replay.spec.ts` and the tier-1 carousel oracle against a throwaway prototype before any verdict. **Decision rule:** if hosting needs carousel-specific branches inside the bridge, Carousel stays on the core `window()` alone (as today), the consumer list below loses its Carousel entry, and the order below skips the Carousel step. No consumer migrates before this verdict is recorded. **Design note (owner outline, 2026-10-05).** **Today:** `primitives/src/virtual/` has `window()`, the `Virtualizer` and the new Fenwick `size_index.rs` (this round, uncommitted at time of writing); `VirtualList` uses all of it. Carousel uses only `virtual::window` for its virtual loop. `message_scroller` has its own JS anchoring; its content-visibility moves to the shared `virtual/cv_chunks.rs` this round. Select, Combobox and Command render every item. There is no 2D grid. **Proposal: one engine, three layers.** **(1) Core,** pure Rust, DOM-free, unit-tested: `SizeIndex` (estimates plus measurements, O(log n) offset <-> index), `window(offset, viewport, overscan)`, anchoring (keep an item's on-screen position when sizes above it change or items are prepended), and `scroll_to(index, align)`. One axis; a grid is two indices, which gives x/y virtualization. **(2) Strategy,** i.e. how out-of-window items exist: Windowed (a range plus spacers), ContentVisibility (all rendered, chunked cv), Auto (a threshold between the two). Later: Windowed + find-in-page (row 153: `hidden="until-found"` text stubs, with `beforematch` calling `scroll_to`). **(3) DOM bridge,** one `use_virtualizer` hook: a passive scroll listener, one ResizeObserver, scope-owned cleanup (the construction of row 159), and a render-signal write only when the range changes. **Consumers:** VirtualList (done), MessageScroller (a reverse list; stick-to-bottom is an anchor policy), Carousel (**only if the required Carousel research says so**; it keeps its own snap, drag and wheel/trackpad physics either way, and the loop would become a modular index map over the core), long Select, Combobox and Command lists, DataTable rows, and a future Grid. **By construction:** measurement, anchoring, listener hygiene and performance live in one place, so every consumer gets the Fenwick and cv-chunk wins, and a new consumer cannot reintroduce the jank and leak classes (rows 143, 149, 159). **Not covered:** carousel physics, drag-and-drop autoscroll and sticky headers. **Order:** the Carousel research first (nothing migrates before its verdict), then `message_scroller` (with row 146), then the Carousel's loop only if the verdict says the bridge can host it, then the list components when a long-list need appears. Row 153 (windowed find-in-page) stays a dependent row. **First note (2026-10-05, kept; its "do not merge the components" conclusion stands, the outline above says how the shared part is layered):** **What is already shared:** `primitives/src/virtual/` (`window()` in `window.rs`, `Virtualizer` in `virtualizer.rs`, `types.rs`, `utils.rs`). `VirtualList` uses the full engine (scroll offset, measured sizes with prefix sums, `get_virtual_items`). Carousel's `CarouselVirtualContent` uses only `crate::r#virtual::window` (pure index windowing with loop rotation, `carousel.rs:4655-4661`: `window(n, anchor(), anchor(), radius, loop_now)`, and `window(n, 0, n - 1, 0, false)` when not virtualized). `message_scroller` uses none: its stage 1 is `content-visibility: auto` (row 146). **Recommendation:** keep one engine and put the new capability there as engine-level features: the content-visibility mode helper and the quantized range signal (row 143), and the `until-found` search placeholders (row 153), so `VirtualList` and any later windowed consumer inherit them. **Do not merge the components:** their scroll models differ in kind (free scroll with variable heights and a measured size cache, versus snap pages with loop rotation and a fixed slide pitch, versus stick-to-bottom with follow-the-stream, prepend preservation and a jump button), so a single component would be a union of three state machines. **What this subsumes:** the duplicated windowing math (already one `window()`), and any second implementation of a content-visibility or find-in-page mode, which would otherwise be written once per component. **What it does not:** the per-component scroll handling and focus behaviour, which stay where they are. **2026-10-05 (round 3):** row 143 built the first engine pieces under `primitives/src/virtual/`: `cv_chunks.rs` (chunked content-visibility, shared with `message_scroller`) and `size_index.rs` (a Fenwick tree for item geometry, O(log N) measure and lookup), and row 159's `use_js_listeners` is the listener helper a bridge should use. Read them before outlining; the REQUIRED Carousel research is unchanged. |

| 158 | Theme follow-ups: accept shadcn's `.dark` class as an alias of `data-theme="dark"`, and suppress transitions during a theme switch | **PART (a) OWNER DECIDED 2026-10-10: APPROVED (by construction); PART (b) BACKLOG, conditional (cold, not scheduled)** -- proposed 2026-10-05, together with the evaluated-and-rejected `light-dark()` option (below). (a) stays on the next-session list; (b) waits on its trigger | S + S | **(a) `.dark` as an alias.** shadcn themes key dark mode on a `.dark` class (`:root` + `.dark` blocks, toggled by next-themes) and Tailwind's `dark:` follows it. Ours keys on `html[data-theme="dark"\|"light"]` (`theme.rs` sets it from the cookie before first paint) plus `prefers-color-scheme`, so a pasted shadcn theme block or a `dark:` utility does nothing here. **Proposal:** `preview/assets/dx-components-theme.css` accepts `.dark` as an alias of `data-theme="dark"` (the same `--dark`/`--light` toggle and `color-scheme`), and the theme switch sets both, so pasted shadcn themes and Tailwind `dark:` work unchanged. A host that sets only `.dark` gets dark; if the switch is the only writer the two cannot disagree. Spec: set `.dark` alone, expect the dark tokens and `color-scheme: dark`. **(b) No transitions during a switch,** like next-themes' `disableTransitionOnChange`: while the mode (and a preset) flips, a transient rule disables `transition` everywhere and is removed after a forced reflow, so every component's colour transition does not animate at once. Lives next to the switch in `theme.rs`. **Evaluated and rejected: migrating the tokens to CSS `light-dark()`.** (1) The values would be identical, and shadcn itself does not use it (`:root` + `.dark` blocks + next-themes), so it would diverge from the system we track and from pasted themes. (2) It is Baseline only since 2024, and an engine without it DROPS the colour declaration instead of falling back: a risk for Dioxus desktop on older Linux WebKitGTK and for the native renderer. (3) Our `data-theme` attribute already drives the `--dark`/`--light` space toggle and `color-scheme` from the same selectors, so there is no second source of truth to remove. **OWNER DECISIONS 2026-10-10 (the row is split in two).** **(a) APPROVED: shadcn's `.dark` class, built by construction.** One `apply_mode(mode)` writer, shared by `preview/src/theme.rs` and the pre-paint script in `preview/index.html` (the existing `PREPAINT_JS` drift test covers the pair), is the ONLY code allowed to change the mode. It sets `data-theme` and toggles the `.dark` class together. "System" mode is resolved from `prefers-color-scheme` inside it, with a page-lifetime change listener (marked `listener-ok: lives as long as the page`, per `check-js-listeners.sh`). The theme CSS treats `html.dark` and `html[data-theme="dark"]` as one selector list. **Gate:** a `check-*.sh` that fails if any other code writes `data-theme` or the `.dark` class (add it to `scripts/run-gates.sh` and CLAUDE.md's gate sentence). **Spec:** paste a real shadcn theme (`:root` + `.dark` blocks) and assert the light, dark and system colours. **Why this matters today:** a pasted shadcn theme's dark block never activates (nothing sets `.dark`), and its `:root` light values win even in dark mode, because our tokens read the shadcn variables first; so the paste is wrong in dark, not merely inert. This supersedes the "alias" proposal in (a) above, which is the same goal without the single-writer guarantee. **(b) BACKLOG, conditional: transitions off during a theme switch** (next-themes' `disableTransitionOnChange`), via a "switching" flag that disables transitions for one frame inside `apply_mode`. **The owner wants to KEEP the colour transition for now, so (b) is NOT scheduled and stays cold.** Trigger: if the switch wash (every component's colour transition animating at once) causes problems. Build it only inside `apply_mode`, never as a second writer. |

The inventory table and the oracle's four assertions: [round-3 note](./research/round3-2026-10-05.md), section 4. |

they must move to `inherit` or `var(--dx-font-sans)` in this row, or the new token never reaches dialogs, sheets, drawers and the calendar; (2) `preview/src/main.rs:2840-2849` and `drag_and_drop_list`'s demo set `monospace` stacks inline: fold them into `--dx-font-mono`. **shadcn, for reference:** the font is a per-project choice. `DEFAULT_CONFIG` is style `nova` + font `inter`; the Nova preset (`radix-nova`) uses Geist, Vega/Mira/Luma use Inter, Maia uses Figtree, Lyra uses JetBrains Mono. shadcn apps load fonts through `next/font`, which downloads them at build time and SELF-HOSTS them, so there are no runtime requests to Google. **Options for the docs site:** (a) self-host Geist + Geist Mono (~52.5 KB; matches the Nova look); (b) self-host Geist only and use the system monospace stack for code (~29 KB, one file); (c) the system font stack only (0 KB; looks different on every OS). **Recommendation: (b)**, plus library tokens `--dx-font-sans` and `--dx-font-mono` defined in `preview/assets/dx-components-theme.css` with system-stack defaults, so an app picks its fonts by overriding two variables; the docs site overrides `--dx-font-sans` with Geist in `main.css`. Components read the tokens (or inherit), never a literal family. **Mechanics:** the woff2 under `preview/assets/` through `asset!` (hashed, base-path-safe), `@font-face` with `font-display: optional` kept (the CLS fix), a preload of the self-hosted Latin file with `crossorigin`, and no Google `preconnect` or stylesheet link left in `main.rs` or `eager_head.rs`. Latin subset only: characters outside it fall back to the system stack (Google's CSS also served latin-ext on demand; adding it costs bytes not measured here). Geist is SIL OFL: ship the licence text beside the files. **The gate, by construction:** widen `scripts/check-demo-remote-assets.sh` from images to fonts, stylesheets and scripts (a remote `@font-face` `url()`, a `<link rel="stylesheet">` or `document::Link` with a remote `href`, a `<script src>` with a remote URL), so nothing remote can return after this row; the script's header says fonts are "Not flagged" today and must be reworded, and the first run will list whatever else is remote (allowlist only with a stated reason). **Done when:** the SSG build makes zero requests to Google hosts (a Playwright network assertion on the home page and one component page), CLS stays 0 on `/component/form/`, the gate fails on a seeded remote font, and the five hard-coded stacks are gone. **Owner:** pick (a), (b) or (c); the recommendation is (b). **Owner decision 2026-10-05 (later the same day): option (a), self-host Geist + Geist Mono (~52.5 KB, about 23 KB of it the mono face); it stays on the next-session list (step 4).** The mechanics above stand, now for both files: `--dx-font-sans` and `--dx-font-mono` in `dx-components-theme.css` with system-stack defaults (a library user picks fonts by overriding two variables), the docs site overriding both with Geist and Geist Mono in `main.css`, `@font-face` with `font-display: optional` and a `crossorigin` preload of the two latin files, no Google `preconnect` or stylesheet left (the links are still in `preview/src/main.rs` at ~`:2060-2073`), the OFL licence text beside the files, and the widened `check-demo-remote-assets.sh`. The inline `monospace` stacks (`preview/src/main.rs:2840-2849` and `:2967`, and the `drag_and_drop_list` main demo) fold into `--dx-font-mono`. **Landed 2026-10-05 (round 3), the first of the two exceptions found while writing this row:** `dialog`, `alert_dialog`, `sheet`, `drawer` and `calendar` no longer hard-code a system sans stack (they inherit, so the app font reaches overlays and the calendar), and `scripts/check-component-fonts.sh` (in `run-gates.sh`) makes the class inexpressible: a component `style.css` `font-family` may only be `inherit` or `var(--dx-font-<name>)` (an `inherit` fallback allowed), `font` only `font: inherit`, and `@font-face` is flagged. So "the five hard-coded stacks are gone" in this row's done-when is already true, and 162 no longer edits those five sheets. Related and landed: the font CLS fix (`display=optional` plus latin preloads: `/component/form/` 0.117 -> 0.000, `/component/carousel/` 0.048 -> 0). Facts and numbers: [round-3 note](./research/round3-2026-10-05.md), section 6. |
| 163 | Wrap hover styles in `@media (hover: hover)`, by codemod plus a gate: 80 `:hover` blocks in 39 stylesheets leave a sticky hover on touch | **OPEN** -- recorded 2026-10-05 (audit); scheduled late in the next-session order (step 4, after the parity wave and row 135's hover pass, because it edits the same stylesheets) | M (one mechanical pass over 39 sheets, plus the gate and a touch spec) | **The defect.** On a touch screen a tap fires a compatibility `:hover` that stays on the tapped element until something else is tapped, so a pressed Button, Tab, menu item or Card keeps its hover look after the finger is gone (a sticky hover). The standard fix applies hover styles only where the primary input can hover: `@media (hover: hover) { .x:hover { ... } }`. The audit counted 80 `:hover` blocks in 39 sheets without it; re-count at step 0 (a plain `grep -c ':hover'` over `preview/` also hits comments, `:not(:hover)` and `main.css`, so use the codemod's own list). **Construction (a class, so no per-sheet patching):** (1) a codemod that wraps each `:hover` rule in `@media (hover: hover)` and keeps the rule where it is in source order (its place in the cascade is what lets it beat the base rule and lose to `:active` and `[data-state]`), run once; (2) a gate `scripts/check-hover-media.sh`, added to `scripts/run-gates.sh` and to CLAUDE.md's gate sentence, failing on any `:hover` in `preview/src/**/*.css` and `preview/assets/*.css` that is not inside an `@media (hover: hover)` block and carries no reasoned allowlist marker (the shape of `check-css-logical-properties.sh`); (3) a touch spec: tap a Button on an emulated phone and assert its computed `background-color` is the rest colour. **Proof it changes nothing on desktop:** row 135's hover pass (the computed-style snapshot with hover applied, A/A byte-identical) before and after; without 135 there is no cheap proof, so 163 follows 135. **Watch:** Chromium's mobile emulation may report `(hover: none)`, so a spec that asserts a hover style on a touch project changes meaning; find those first (`grep -rn "hover()" playwright/`). **Sequencing:** it edits the 39 sheets that phase C and the parity wave repaint, so it runs after them, as one lane that owns every stylesheet while it runs and is never dispatched beside a lane that edits CSS. |
| 164 | A fixtures `goto` that waits for hydration (49 spec files lack the wait), and migrate the 362 legacy `?name=` spec routes | **OPEN** -- recorded 2026-10-05 (audit); scheduled in the next-session order (step 4, just before row 152, which touches the same specs) | M (the fixture is S; the migration is mechanical but touches ~50 spec files) | **The class.** Row 109 fixed the hydration race one spec at a time: `gotoHydrated` (`playwright/hydration.ts`) waits for `html[data-hydrated="true"]`, and the 2026-09-26 sweep moved the 24 interacting SSG specs onto it, but choosing between plain `page.goto` and `gotoHydrated` is still a per-spec decision, so any new or untouched spec can click prerendered markup before wasm has attached its listeners. The audit found **49 spec files that never wait for hydration** (re-count at step 0; some are legitimately render-only or vendored W3C reference pages). **Construction:** make the `page` fixture in `playwright/fixtures.ts` (the file every spec must import from, enforced by `check-playwright-fixture-import.sh`) wrap `page.goto` so a navigation to a preview URL waits for `html[data-hydrated="true"]` by default, with an explicit opt-out (`{ hydrate: false }`) for render-only, vendored and no-wasm pages; `gotoHydrated` then becomes a thin alias, and "a spec forgot the wait" cannot recur. Row 109 confirmed the signal on both `dx serve` and the SSG serve, so the default wait does not hang on either. **Second half:** 362 spec routes still use the legacy form `/component/?name=X&`. Row 46's closure keeps those links resolving client-side, but an SSG build serves them as the not-found shell until hydration corrects the page, so a spec on a legacy route that does not wait reads the wrong page; migrate them to the path form `/component/<name>/` the SSG build prerenders (the form the site and the README link use). Do both in one pass over the same files, in an order that keeps the suite green at each step: the fixture default first, with the legacy routes still working, then the route migration. **Subsumes:** row 109's remaining per-spec follow-ups and row 90's `networkidle` remedy for `pie_chart`/`radial_chart`. **Overlap:** row 152 (the suite slimming review) edits the same files, so 164 goes first; the parity wave adds specs, so take 164 after it or let those lanes write their new specs against the new fixture. **2026-10-05 (round 3) evidence and a trap:** debug builds showed Playwright hydration timeouts under 4-worker load (a spec acting before wasm attaches; release builds did not), which is this row's class appearing as flakes: the fixture default fixes it by construction, and until it lands run functional specs with fewer workers on debug. Round 3's new specs (`all-routes.spec.ts`, `modal-exit.spec.ts`, `first-paint-styles.spec.ts`, `theme-picker-phone.spec.ts`, `listener-inventory.spec.ts`) already use `gotoHydrated`; the 49-file count has not been re-taken. **Trap from row 113:** block demos have no prerendered path form on the SSG build (`/component/block/calendar/range/` answers 404 there), only the query form `/component/block/?name=<c>&variant=<v>&`, so the route migration applies to component pages (`/component/<name>/`), not to block pages. **2026-10-05 (round 3 final verification) sub-item, base-path-naive spec selectors:** `modal-exit.spec.ts` (14 cases) and `listener-inventory.spec.ts` (2 cases) match links with `a[href^="/component/…"]` and `a[href="/?"]`, which hold only when the site is served from the root. They PASS on the root-served verification build and FAIL on the base-path deploy build (`/dioxus-components/…`), the same class as row 100's base-path leak but on the spec side. Fix in the same pass over the same files: a shared helper that builds the link locator from the page's base (or match on a base-path-insensitive suffix such as `a[href$="/component/<name>/"]`), and run the suite once on a `--base-path` build to prove it. |
| 165 | A generated props reference table on every component's docs page, produced in `preview/build.rs` (~100 public props are undocumented today: select 31, calendar 18, date_picker 14) | **OPEN** -- recorded 2026-10-05 (audit); scheduled in the next-session order (step 4, after the site-shell rows 161 and 162) | M (the generator is S-M; writing ~100 doc comments is the bulk) | **The gap.** A component's docs page (`preview/src/components/<name>/docs.md`) describes usage, but nothing lists the props a component takes: name, type, default, what it does. The audit counted ~100 public props with no doc comment, concentrated in `select` (31), `calendar` (18) and `date_picker` (14); a reader has to open the source. **Construction (so a prop cannot ship undocumented):** `preview/build.rs`, which already turns each component's `docs.md` and `component.json` into `docs.html` and `description.txt` in `OUT_DIR`, also reads the `#[derive(Props)]` structs of the component's primitive (`primitives/src/<name>.rs`) and its styled wrapper (`preview/src/components/<name>/component.rs`) and emits a props table (name, type, `#[props(default)]` value, the field's doc comment), appended after the examples. Fail the build, or a `scripts/check-*.sh` gate (added to `scripts/run-gates.sh` and CLAUDE.md's gate sentence), when a public prop field has no doc comment, so the ~100 get written once and the class stops. **Cautions:** `build.rs`'s only parser dependency today is `pulldown-cmark` (no `syn`), so decide in the lane between a small scan of the derive structs and `syn` as a new build-dependency; register `cargo:rerun-if-changed` per source file (row 92's stale `OUT_DIR` class); `#[props(extends = ...)]` spreads and `ReadSignal<T>` need display rules (show the inner type, say "plus the global attributes"); and `build.rs` must be REWRITTEN, not touched, for the `dx serve` watcher to notice (CLAUDE.md). **Sequence:** write the doc comments first for the three big components (one docs lane per component, disjoint files), the generator after, or the gate fails on day one. **Does not cover:** events and parts (the table lists props; a component's parts stay in `docs.md`). |
| 166 | HoverCard and Tooltip `delay` / `close_delay` props (Radix and Base UI parity) | **OPEN** -- recorded 2026-10-05 (audit); scheduled in the next-session order (step 4, in row 138's batch: disjoint files) | S | `primitives/src/hover_card.rs` (~`:57-60`) and `primitives/src/tooltip.rs` (~`:60-62`) already say in their module docs that delays are "a possible future prop" and that the component "opens and closes without one today": a hover card or tooltip opens the instant the pointer enters and closes the instant it leaves. The libraries underneath shadcn both have them: Radix `HoverCard` `openDelay`/`closeDelay`, Radix `Tooltip` `delayDuration`, Base UI `PreviewCard.Trigger` and `Tooltip.Trigger` `delay`/`closeDelay`. Without a delay, sweeping the pointer across a toolbar or a card list flashes every surface in turn, and a pointer travelling from the trigger to the content can close the card on the way. **Fix:** `delay` and `close_delay` (milliseconds) props on the two roots, defaults taken from the current Base UI source at build time (do not copy numbers from this row), timers owned by the scope and cleared on drop (the `check-uncleared-intervals.sh` class; a `setTimeout` through `document::eval` is a row 159 leak, so use the Rust-side timer the other components use), keyboard focus opening without the delay (check shadcn's tooltip), the two "possible future prop" paragraphs turned into prop docs, and specs: nothing opens before `delay`, the close waits `close_delay`, and re-entering the content cancels the close. Check the existing `hover-card.spec.ts` and `tooltip.spec.ts` expectations, which assume instant open. |

| 168 | The disk-full class: lanes running raw `cargo` with incremental compilation on regrow the shared gates target dir by ~10-13 GB per hour | **OPEN (proposal)** -- recorded 2026-10-05 (audit); scheduled in the next-session order (step 1, a harness fix; the CLAUDE.md note is the main loop's edit) | S | **Evidence (audit, 2026-10-05):** the shared gates target dir regrew by ~10-13 GB per hour while lanes ran raw `cargo` (check, clippy, test) with the default `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=1`; the growth is incremental-compilation state. `scripts/run-gates.sh` already defaults it off (`export CARGO_INCREMENTAL="${CARGO_INCREMENTAL:-0}"`, `:117-118`), which is why the gates run is not the grower: the growth comes from lanes outside it. **Construction (not a reminder):** `scripts/lane-target.sh create` already prints the single `export CARGO_TARGET_DIR=<abs path>` line every lane `eval`s; make that output also export `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` (opt-out: `LANE_CARGO_INCREMENTAL=1`), so a lane that follows the documented setup gets both and the class needs no discipline; plus one sentence in CLAUDE.md next to the `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` rule (the main loop edits CLAUDE.md, this row's lane does not). **Trade-off, stated:** a lane's own edit-build loop gets slower (a changed crate recompiles whole instead of incrementally) in exchange for a bounded disk footprint; a lane that truly needs the speed opts back in and runs `scripts/lane-target.sh prune` afterwards. **Does not cover:** a caller that exports `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` by hand without `lane-target.sh` (the CLAUDE.md sentence is for them) or `dx` builds, which set their own profile flags. **Done when:** a lane created through the script has `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` in its environment, and a before/after `scripts/lane-target.sh du` over one lane-hour shows the growth gone. |
| 169 | **Open owner question:** should Popover be non-modal by default (shadcn / Radix parity)? | **OWNER DECIDED 2026-10-10: APPROVED (non-modal by default)** -- the owner's answer to the question below; was AWAITING OWNER ANSWER (recorded 2026-10-05). Stays on the next-session list (joins the parity wave as group 10, after 145's floating-surface lane); nothing started | S to flip the default; M with the dependent tests and docs | **Today:** `PopoverRoot`'s `is_modal` defaults to `true` (`primitives/src/popover.rs:114`): focus is trapped, the page behind is inert, a click outside dismisses, and the scroll lock is taken while it is open (`is_modal() && open()`, `:399`); `overlay` (default `false`, like shadcn) controls the scrim separately. shadcn's Popover (Radix underneath) is non-modal by default: the page stays interactive and light dismiss closes it. **What depends on modal (the theme lane's list; not re-verified here):** 8 popover tests, the native-dialog oracle's Rules 6 and 8 (`playwright/oracle/tier2-html/native-dialog.spec.ts`), the main-thread and top-layer-ink entries, and the scroll lock. **Cost of flipping:** every modal-dependent test and demo must pass `is_modal: true` explicitly or be rewritten for the non-modal arm, and it is a behaviour change for existing users (a breaking-change note). **Cost of not flipping:** a shadcn Popover example pasted into a Dioxus app behaves differently (page inert, scroll locked), the surprise parity work exists to remove. **No recommendation recorded: the owner decides.** If yes, it joins the parity wave (row 155) as a tenth group, after 145's floating-surface lane; if no, record the reason here and close the row. **2026-10-05 (round 3):** the scrim half landed separately and does not answer this question: Popover has an `overlay` prop (default false, like shadcn) and a `variants/overlay` demo, through the theme's `data-dx-overlay="off"` rule (row 141). Modality (focus trap, inert page, scroll lock, light dismiss) is untouched, and the modal Popover's web arm now calls the shared dialog driver, so a flip of the default would not need its own close logic. **OWNER DECISION 2026-10-10: APPROVED.** Make Popover non-modal by default, like shadcn/Radix/Base UI. The target behaviour: (1) focus moves in on open and returns to the trigger on Escape; (2) the page stays interactive and scrollable (no inert, no scroll lock); (3) one outside click closes it AND reaches its target (no swallowed first click); (4) modal stays available as `is_modal: true`. **Consequences to do with the flip:** the ~8 popover tests, and the native-dialog oracle's Rule 6/8, main-thread and top-layer-ink entries that assume modal, move to an explicit modal demo variant (`is_modal: true`), and the non-modal default gets its own coverage for 1-3; add a breaking-change note for existing users. The "No recommendation recorded" sentence above is superseded by this decision. |
| 170 | Cross-component CSS overrides: 190 sites in 12 sheets declare, in one component's stylesheet, a property that another component's own rule on the same element also declares, so the winner is whichever sheet loads last | **OPEN (construction started)** -- recorded 2026-10-05 (round 3: the FIELD-VAR and STYLE-DELIVERY lanes); the first conversion (Field -> Label) and the gate with a shrink-only baseline landed; the burn-down and the layer prototype are scheduled in the next-session order **beside the 135 -> 145 -> 111 chain (step 2): it overlaps 145's state recipes** | M (the decision and the prototype are S; the burn-down is one lane per sheet group over 190 sites) | **The class (found twice, so a construction and not patches).** The Field label was 14 px on the home gallery and 12.8 px on `/component/field/`: `.dx-field-label` (field sheet) and `.dx-label` (label sheet) tie at (0,1,0), the later sheet wins, and sheet order is per route. The style-delivery lane then found 208 of 2943 sheet pairs ordered both ways across routes, and `scripts/compare-ssg-styles.mjs` found the home page's Field label as the one route (1 of 83) that differs between a bundled and an unbundled build. A specificity bump (`label.dx-field-label`) only moves the contest. **Landed 2026-10-05 (round 3): slot variables.** The base component reads `--dx-<component>-<property>` with its own default and the composer sets the VARIABLE on the base's element, never the property: Label reads `--dx-label-gap`, `-color`, `-font-size`, `-font-weight` and `-line-height` (shadcn defaults; documented in label's docs "Slot variables"), and Field sets `--dx-label-line-height: 1.375` and the invalid colour. A custom property is a different property from the one the base declares, so nothing contests anything: no specificity race, no order dependence (measured order-independent). Cost: a standalone Label changes from 12.8 px / 400 to 14 px / 500, which is shadcn's own. **Gate:** `scripts/check-cross-component-overrides.sh` (in `scripts/run-gates.sh`) with the shrink-only debt register `scripts/check-cross-component-overrides.baseline.tsv`: **190 sites in 12 sheets** (149 `composed:<Y>`, where X's class sits on Y's root, derived from the Rust, and 41 `tag`, a bare `input`, `select`, `textarea`, `button` or `label` selector, the only way left to reach another component's root because `check-dx-class-prefix.sh` forbids naming its class): sidebar 65, message_scroller 25, date_picker 17, button_group 16, toolbar 16, chart 14, input_group 10, command 9, form 7, data_table 6, field 4, item 1. A site not in the baseline fails the gate; `--update-baseline` only shrinks (`--allow-grow` needs a reason in the commit). **Recommendation (the lane's; not owner-reviewed): (1) slot variables for component-to-component composition:** burn the baseline down in this order, sidebar, message_scroller, date_picker, button_group, toolbar, input_group, chart, form (then command, data_table, field, item), each sheet adding slots to the base it composes and documenting them. **(2) Later, an opt-in single `@layer dx` for the APP-override boundary** (an app's unlayered CSS beats the library whatever the specificity or order): prototype first, and diff a layered build against an unlayered one with `scripts/compare-ssg-styles.mjs` (expect 0 differing routes) before anything ships. The risks to list first (analysis from the cascade rules, not measured): unlayered app resets and element rules beat every library class rule; Tailwind v4's own layers make the position of `dx` in the order decide whether `p-4` beats a dx rule, and the library cannot know the app's layer names; `!important` reverses layer order, so a library `!important` (the overlay-off backdrop, reduced motion, the forced-colors outline) would beat an app's. Layers do not remove the contest between two library sheets inside one layer, so (2) complements (1) and never replaces it. **(3) Explicitly NOT a `dx.compose` sub-layer** (it keeps the same-property design, needs every composing rule filed by hand and an `@layer` order statement ahead of both sheets that a copied-in component cannot guarantee, and does not help the 41 `tag` sites). **Order and overlap:** 145's state recipes (focus, invalid, disabled) are the rules most likely to add new sites: build them as slot variables. `date_picker`'s sheet belongs to 145's floating-surface lane, so take that sheet after it; take the other sheets inside or after the phase C lane that repaints them. **Done when:** the baseline is empty or each remaining line has a stated exception, and the layer prototype's diff is recorded either way. Evidence and the full comparison: [round-3 note](./research/round3-2026-10-05.md), section 7. |

Rows 21 and 106 no longer carry the "frozen anchor" / engine diagnosis for these cases. |
| 173 | **The deployed site has no `404.html`:** unknown URLs (e.g. `/dioxus-components/charts/no-such-chart/`) get GitHub Pages' own 404 instead of the app's not-found page | **OPEN** -- found 2026-10-05 in round 3's final verification; **next-session step 1** | S | `docs/` (the committed deploy output) has no `404.html`, and `scripts/deploy-preview.sh` does not emit one, so every URL the SSG did not prerender is answered by GitHub's generic 404 and never reaches the app's router. The phone-picker spec's 4 not-found cases fail on a plain static server for exactly this reason (a static server answers an unknown path with its own 404, not the app). **Fix:** have `deploy-preview.sh` emit a `404.html` that boots the app (the standard SPA fallback: the app shell, with its asset references correct under the base path) so the router renders its not-found page, then test it on a plain static server with an unknown URL under the base path (the phone-picker spec's 4 not-found cases are the ready-made check). Files: `scripts/deploy-preview.sh`; the new `docs/404.html` is generated output. No overlap with the other step-1 lanes. |
| 174 | **Home/overview page scroll jank, re-profiled 2026-10-10** | **PENDING-PROFILE** -- opened 2026-10-10; a profiling lane is running; nothing scheduled until its numbers are in | S to profile; fix size unknown until then | The owner reported scroll jank on the home/overview page again (see rows 116 and 142-143 for the earlier scroll and main-thread work and `dev-docs/research/` for the baselines). **PENDING-PROFILE: profile results: see scratchpad report, to be summarised.** The main loop fills this cell in (what the profile showed, which routes and components, whether it is a release-build long task or a debug-only artefact: timing numbers count only on a release SSG build, see CLAUDE.md), then sets the status and a fix scope. Do not guess a cause before the summary lands. |
